The storm and rage of Mrs. Iden whenever she knew he was paddling in the grass was awful. She would come shuffling out—she had a way of rubbing her shoes along the ground when irritated with her hands under her apron, which she twisted about—and pelt him with scorn.

===Chinese===
每当艾登太太（Mrs. Iden）发觉他又在草地上蹚水时，那暴风骤雨般的怒火简直吓人。她会拖着脚蹭出来——她一生气就双手揪着围裙在身前绞来绞去，脚上的鞋子一路在地上蹭着——然后劈头盖脸地丢来一通嘲骂。

===Original===
"There, put your boots on—do, and hide your nasty feet!" (Iden had a particularly white skin, and feet as white as a lady's.) "Disgusting! Nobody ever does it but you, and you ought to be ashamed of yourself! Anything more disgusting I never heard of. Nobody else but you would ever think of such a thing; makes me feel queer to see you."

===Chinese===
"行了，穿上你的靴子吧——快点，把你那双恶心的脚藏起来！"（艾登皮肤特别白，双脚白得跟淑女的一样。）"真恶心！除了你没别人干这种事，你该害臊才对！再没比这更恶心的事了。除了你谁会想出这种事来；看见你我就浑身不自在。"

===Original===
Shuffling about, and muttering to herself, "Nobody else"—that was the sin and guilt of it—by-and-by Mrs. Iden would circle round to where he had left his boots, and, suddenly seizing them, would fling them in the ditch.

===Chinese===
她拖着脚来回踱步，嘴里念叨着"没别人"——这正是他罪孽和过失所在——不一会儿，艾登太太就绕到他脱靴子的地方，猛地抓起靴子，一把扔进了沟里。

===Original===
And I verily believe, in the depth of her indignation, if she had not been afraid to touch firearms, she would have brought out the gun, and had a shot at him.

===Chinese===
我深信不疑，若不是她惧怕碰枪，她盛怒之下准会搬出猎枪来朝他开上一枪。

===Original===
After a time Iden left his old post at the russet apple, and went up the meadow to the horse-chestnut trees that he himself had planted, and there, in peace and quietness and soft cool shadow, waded about in the dew, without anyone to grumble at him.

===Chinese===
过了一阵，艾登离开了褐苹果树下的老地方，走到草场上头他自己种的那些七叶树下，在那儿平平静静地、在柔和清凉的树荫中踏着露水漫步，再没有人来抱怨他了。

===Original===
How crookedly things are managed in this world!

===Chinese===
这世上的事安排得何等歪歪扭扭！

===Original===
It is the modern fashion to laugh at the East, and despise the Turks and all their ways, making Grand Viziers of barbers, and setting waiters in high places, with the utmost contempt for anything reasonable—all so incongruous and chance-ruled. In truth, all things in our very midst go on in the Turkish manner; crooked men are set in straight places, and straight people in crooked places, just the same as if we had all been dropped promiscuously out of a bag and shook down together on the earth to work out our lives, quite irrespective of our abilities and nature. Such an utter jumble!

===Chinese===
如今时兴嘲笑东方人，鄙夷土耳其人及其种种作风——让理发师当上大维齐尔，把侍者安插到高位上，对一切合情合理的事物嗤之以鼻——全都这么不伦不类、听天由命。其实，我们身边的一切也无不如此：歪人被摆到正位上，正人被塞进歪处，仿佛我们全是从一个袋子里胡乱倒出来、撒在地上各奔前程的，全然不顾各自的能力与天性。好一锅大杂烩！

===Original===
Here was Iden, with his great brain and wonderful power of observation, who ought to have been a famous traveller in unexplored Africa or Tibet, bringing home rarities and wonders; or, with his singular capacity for construction, a leading engineer, boring Mont Cenis Tunnels and making Panama Canals; or, with his Baconian intellect, forming a new school of philosophy—here was Iden, tending cows, and sitting, as the old story goes, undecidedly on a stile—sitting astride—and eternally sitting, and unable to make up his mind to get off on one side or the other.

===Chinese===
艾登这人，天资卓越，观察力惊人，本该成为名扬天下的旅行家，深入未探明的非洲或西藏，带回奇珍异宝；或者凭他那超凡的建造才能，当个首席工程师，去凿穿蒙塞尼隧道、开挖巴拿马运河；又或者凭他那培根式的才智，开创一个新哲学流派——可艾登呢，却在放牛，而且正如老故事里说的那样，犹犹豫豫地骑在篱笆上——两腿叉开坐着——永远坐着，下不了决心从哪边跳下来。

===Original===
Here was Mrs. Iden, who had had a beautiful shape and expressive eyes, full in her youth of life and fire, who ought to have led the gayest life in London and Paris alternately, riding in a carriage, and flinging money about in the most extravagant, joyous, and good-natured manner—here was Mrs. Iden making butter in a dull farmhouse, and wearing shoes out at the toes.

===Chinese===
艾登太太年轻时身段曼妙、双眸传神，浑身充满生命的活力与热情，本该在伦敦和巴黎之间过着最欢快的日子，坐马车兜风，以最挥霍、最快活、最和善的方式挥金如土——可艾登太太呢，却在一个死气沉沉的农庄里搅黄油，脚上的鞋子都磨穿了脚趾。

===Original===
So our lives go on, rumble-jumble, like a carrier's cart over ruts and stones, thumping anyhow instead of running smoothly on new-mown sward like a cricket-ball.

===Chinese===
我们的生活就是这样磕磕绊绊、乱七八糟，像运货马车碾过车辙和石块一样颠簸，哪儿都在乱撞，而非像板球在新割的草坪上那样平滑地滚动。

===Original===
It all happens in the Turkish manner.

===Chinese===
一切尽以土耳其式的方式发生。

===Original===
Another time there would come a letter from one of the Flammas in London. Could they spare a little bag of lavender?—they grew such lovely sweet lavender at Coombe Oaks. Then you might see Mr. and Mrs. Iden cooing and billing, soft as turtledoves, and fraternising in the garden over the lavender hedge. Here was another side, you see, to the story.

===Chinese===
另一次，会收到来自伦敦弗拉玛（Flamma）家的一封信。能不能匀一小袋薰衣草给他们？——他们库姆橡树庄园（Coombe Oaks）种的薰衣草又香又甜，可爱极了。这时你就能看到艾登先生和太太像斑鸠一样柔情脉脉，在花园里的薰衣草篱笆旁相亲相爱。你看，这故事还有另外一面呢。

===Original===
Mrs. Iden was very fond of lavender, the scent, and the plant in every form. She kept little bags of it in all her drawers, and everything at Coombe Oaks upstairs in the bedrooms had a faint, delicious lavender perfume. There is nothing else that smells so sweet and clean and dry. You cannot imagine a damp sheet smelling of lavender.

===Chinese===
艾登太太非常喜欢薰衣草，爱它的香气，也爱它的一切形态。她所有抽屉里都放着薰衣草小布袋，库姆橡树庄园楼上卧室里的每样东西都带着一缕淡雅怡人的薰衣草清香。没有别的什么气味能如此甜美、洁净又干爽。你绝想象不出一张潮湿的床单能散发出薰衣草的香气来。

===Original===
Iden himself liked lavender, and used to rub it between his finger and thumb in the garden, as he did, too, with the black-currant leaves and walnut-leaves, if he fancied anything he had touched might have left an unpleasant odour adhering to his skin. He said it cleaned his hands as much as washing them.

===Chinese===
艾登自己也喜欢薰衣草，常在花园里用拇指和食指揉搓它；若是觉得碰过什么沾上了难闻的气味，他还会揉搓黑加仑叶和核桃叶。他说这样洗手跟用水洗一样干净。

===Original===
Iden liked Mrs. Iden to like lavender because his mother had been so fond of it, and all the sixteen carved oak-presses which had been so familiar to him in boyhood were full of a thick atmosphere of the plant.

===Chinese===
艾登乐见艾登太太也喜欢薰衣草，因为他母亲生前就对它情有独钟，而他少年时代无比熟悉的那十六只雕花橡木衣柜里，至今仍弥漫着薰衣草的浓郁气息。

===Original===
Long since, while yet the honeymoon bouquet remained in the wine of life, Iden had set a hedge of lavender to please his wife. It was so carefully chosen, and set, and watched, that it grew to be the finest lavender in all the country. People used to come for it from round about, quite certain of a favourable reception, for there was nothing so sure to bring peace at Coombe Oaks as a mention of lavender.

===Chinese===
很久以前，当蜜月的花束还插在生命之酒中时，艾登就种了一道薰衣草篱笆来取悦妻子。他精心挑选、仔细栽种、悉心看护，终于种出了全乡最好的薰衣草。四面八方的人常来讨要，全然不担心被拒，因为在库姆橡树庄园，没有比提到薰衣草更能确保宾主尽欢的事了。

===Original===
But the letter from the Flammas was the great event—from London, all that way, asking for some Coombe Oaks lavender! Then there was billing and cooing, and fraternising, and sunshine in the garden over the hedge of lavender. If only it could have lasted! Somehow, as people grow older there seems so much grating of the wheels.

===Chinese===
可弗拉玛家的这封信却是那年头的大事——从伦敦那么远的地方写来，竟然想要库姆橡树庄园的薰衣草！于是便是一番卿卿我我、手足情深，薰衣草篱笆上空阳光灿烂。但愿这般情感能长久！然而不知怎的，人一上了年纪，生活的轮轴就像生了锈，到处咯咯作响。

===Original===
In time, long time, people's original feelings get strangely confused and overlaid. The churchwardens of the eighteenth century plastered the fresco paintings of the fourteenth in their churches—covered them over with yellowish mortar. The mould grows up, and hides the capital of the fallen column; the acanthus is hidden in earth. At the foot of the oak, where it is oldest, the bark becomes dense and thick, impenetrable, and without sensitiveness; you may cut off an inch thick without reaching the sap. A sort of scale or caking in long, long time grows over original feelings.

===Chinese===
日久天长，人们最初的感情便渐渐变得迷离而层层覆盖。十八世纪的教堂管事用灰浆涂盖了十四世纪的壁画——将它们埋在黄褐色的灰泥之下。霉菌蔓生，遮住了倒塌柱子的柱头；莨苕叶饰深埋泥土。橡树最古老的底部，树皮变得又密又厚，坚不可摧，毫无知觉；你砍下一寸厚也见不到树汁。一层类似鳞片或硬壳的东西，在漫长的岁月里渐渐长在了最初的感情之上。

===Original===
There was no one in the world so affectionate and loving as Mrs. Iden—no one who loved a father so dearly; just as Amaryllis loved *her* father.

===Chinese===
世间再没有比艾登太太更深情慈爱的人了——再没有谁如此挚爱自己的父亲；正如阿玛丽莉斯深爱*她的*父亲一样。

===Original===
But after they had lived at Coombe Oaks thirty years or so, and the thick dull bark had grown, after the scales or caking had come upon the heart, after the capital of the column had fallen, after the painting had been blurred, it came about that old Flamma, Mrs. Iden's father, died in London.

===Chinese===
然而在库姆橡树庄园住了大约三十年之后——在那层厚重迟钝的树皮长成之后，在那层鳞甲或硬壳覆上心头之后，在柱头倒塌之后，在壁画模糊之后——艾登太太的父亲老弗拉玛在伦敦去世了。

===Original===
After thirty years of absolute quiet at Coombe Oaks, husband and wife went up to London to the funeral, which took place at one of those fearful London cemeteries that strike a chill at one's very soul. Of all the horrible things in the world there is nothing so calmly ghastly as a London cemetery.

===Chinese===
经过库姆橡树庄园三十年的绝对宁静之后，夫妻俩前往伦敦参加葬礼。葬礼在一处令人毛骨悚然的伦敦公墓举行，那地方直往人的灵魂深处灌入寒意。世间种种可怕之物中，再没有比伦敦公墓更阴森惨淡的了。

===Original===
In the evening, after the funeral, Mr. and Mrs. Iden went to the theatre.

===Chinese===
葬礼当晚，艾登先生和太太去了剧院。

===Original===
"How frivolous! How unfeeling!" No, nothing of the sort; how truly sad and human, for to be human is to be sad. That men and women should be so warped and twisted by the pressure of the years out of semblance to themselves; that circumstances should so wall in their lives with insurmountable cliffs of granite facts, compelling them to tread the sunless gorge; that the coldness of death alone could open the door to pleasure.

===Chinese===
"多轻浮！多无情！"不，绝非如此；这恰恰是何等真切的悲凉与人性，因为做人便是悲凉。男人和女人竟被岁月的压力扭曲得面目全非，不复当年模样；环境竟用花岗岩般不可逾越的峭壁将他们的生活层层围堵，逼他们踏入不见天日的峡谷；唯有死亡的冰冷才能打开通向快乐的大门。

===Original===
They sat at the theatre with grey hearts. With the music and the song, the dancing, the colours and gay dresses, it was sadder there than in the silent rooms at the house where the dead had been. Old Flamma alone had been dead *there*; they were dead here. Dead in life—at the theatre.

===Chinese===
他们怀着灰暗的心坐在剧院里。音乐与歌声、舞蹈、色彩与华丽的衣裳，让这里比停放遗体的寂静房间更加悲伤。老弗拉玛只是在*那里*死了；他们却在这里死了。在活着的时候就死了——在剧院里。

===Original===
They had used to go joyously to the theatre thirty years before, when Iden came courting to town; from the edge of the grave they came back to look on their own buried lives.

===Chinese===
三十年前，当艾登进城求爱时，他们曾高高兴兴地去剧院；如今，他们从坟墓的边缘回过头来，看着自己被埋葬的一生。

===Original===
If you will only *think*, you will see it was a most dreadful and miserable incident, that visit to the theatre after the funeral.

===Chinese===
只要你肯*想一想*，你就会明白，葬礼之后去剧院这件事，是多么可怕而悲惨。

## V / 第V章

===Original===
He was not asleep—he was thinking. Sometimes, of course, it happened that slumber was induced by the position in which he placed himself; slumber, however, was not his intent. He liked to rest after his midday meal and think. There was no real loss of time in it—he had been at work since half-past five.

His especial and striking characteristic was a very large, high, and noble forehead—the forehead attributed to Shakespeare and seen in his busts. Shakespeare's intellect is beyond inquiry, yet he was not altogether a man of action. He was, indeed, an actor upon the stage; once he stole the red deer (delightful to think of that!), but he did not sail to the then new discovered lands of America, nor did he fight the Spaniards. So much intellect is, perhaps, antagonistic to action, or rather it is averse to those arts by which a soldier climbs to the position of commander. If Shakespeare by the chance of birth, or other accident, had had the order of England's forces, we should have seen generalship such as the world had not known since Caesar.

His intellect was too big to climb backstairs till opportunity came. We have great thoughts instead of battles.

Iden's forehead might have been sculptured for Shakespeare's. There was too much thought in it for the circumstances of his life. It is possible to think till you cannot act.

===Chinese===
他并未入睡——他是在思考。当然，有时他摆出的姿势会让他打起瞌睡；但睡觉并非他的本意。他喜欢在午餐后休息思考。这并非真的浪费时间——他从五点半就开始干活了。

他最显著的特征是一个非常宽大、高挺、高贵的额头——就是人们认为莎士比亚拥有的那种额头，在他的半身像上可见。莎士比亚（Shakespeare）的才智毋庸置疑，但他并非完全是个行动派。他确实是舞台上的演员；有一次他还偷过赤鹿（想想都觉得愉快！），但他没有航向当时新发现的美洲大陆，也没有与西班牙人作战。或许，过多的才智反而与行动相悖，或者说，它对士兵晋升为指挥官所需的那些技艺心存抵触。倘若莎士比亚因出生的偶然或其他机缘而执掌了英格兰的军权，我们本会看到自恺撒以来世人未曾见过的将帅之才。

他的才智太过宏阔，不屑于钻营逢迎，只等时机降临。我们拥有的是伟大的思想，而非战役。

艾登（Iden）的额头简直像是为莎士比亚雕刻的。对于他的人生境遇而言，这里面承载的思虑实在太重了。人可以一直思考，直到无法行动。

===Original===
After the mice descended Iden did sleep for a few minutes. When he awoke he looked at the clock in a guilty way, and then opening the oven of the grate, took out a baked apple. He had one there ready for him almost always—always, that is, when they were not ripe on the trees.

A baked apple, he said, was the most wholesome thing in the world; it corrected the stomach, prevented acidity, improved digestion, and gave tone to all the food that had been eaten previously. If people would only eat baked apples they would not need to be forever going to the chemists' shops for drugs and salines to put them right. The women were always at the chemists' shops—you could never pass the chemists' shops in the town without seeing two or three women buying something.

The apple was the apple of fruit, the natural medicine of man—and the best flavoured. It was compounded of the sweetest extracts and essences of air and light, put together of sunshine and wind and shower in such a way that no laboratory could imitate: and so on in a strain and with a simplicity of language that reminded you of Bacon and his philosophy of the Elizabethan age.

===Chinese===
老鼠们离去后，艾登确实睡了几分钟。醒来时，他心虚地看了一眼钟，然后打开炉栅的烤箱，取出一个烤苹果。那里几乎总为他备着一个——只要树上的苹果还没熟。

他说，烤苹果是世上最有益健康的东西；它能调理肠胃，防止胃酸过多，促进消化，并为之前吃下的所有食物增添活力。如果人们肯吃烤苹果，就不必总是跑去药房买药物和盐水来调理自己了。女人们总是待在药房里——你每次经过镇上的药房，总能看到两三个女人在买东西。

苹果是水果之王，是人类天然的药物——而且风味最佳。它由空气和阳光中最甜美的精华与精粹调配而成，由阳光、风和阵雨以实验室无法仿制的方式组合而成：他继续以这种腔调，用质朴的语言侃侃而谈，让人想起培根（Bacon）和他那个伊丽莎白时代的哲学。

===Original===
Iden in a way certainly had a tinge of the Baconian culture, naturally, and not from any study of that author, whose books he had never seen. The great Bacon was, in fact, a man of orchard and garden, and gathered his ideas from the fields.

Just look at an apple on the tree, said Iden. Look at a Blenheim orange, the inimitable mixture of colour, the gold and bronze, and ruddy tints, not bright colours—undertones of bright colours—smoothed together and polished, and made the more delightful by occasional roughness in the skin. Or look at the brilliant King Pippin. Now he was getting older he found, however, that the finest of them all was the russet. For eating, at its proper season, it was good, but for cooking it was simply the Imperial Caesar and Sultan of apples; whether for baking, or pies, or sauce, there was none to equal it. Applesauce made of the real true russet was a sauce for Jove's own table. It was necessary that it should be the real russet. Indeed in apple trees you had to be as careful of breeding and pedigree as the owners of racing stables were about their horses.

Ripe apples could not be got all the year round in any variety; besides which, in winter and cold weather the crudity of the stomach needed to be assisted with a little warmth; therefore bake them.

===Chinese===
艾登身上确实带有几分培根式的文化气质，不过是天生的，而非研读过那位作家的著作——他从未见过培根的书。事实上，伟大的培根本人也是果园和菜园中人，他的思想是从田野间采集而来的。

艾登说，你只需看看树上的苹果。看看布伦海姆橙（Blenheim orange），那无与伦比的色彩交融，金色、古铜色与红润的色调——不是明艳的色彩，而是明艳色彩的底色——被揉合在一起，打磨光滑，偶尔果皮上的粗糙反而增添了韵味。或者看看那光彩夺目的国王皮平（King Pippin）。不过，随着年纪渐长，他发现所有苹果中最好的当属褐皮苹果（russet）。在恰当的时节，它作为鲜果食用固然是好，但用来烹饪的话，它简直就是苹果中的恺撒大帝和苏丹；无论是烤制、做派还是做酱，都无人能及。用正宗的褐皮苹果做成的苹果酱，那是款待朱庇特（Jove）御桌的酱汁。它必须是正宗的褐皮苹果。确实，在苹果树的培育中，你得像赛马场主对待他们的马匹那样，讲究血统和谱系。

苹果不可能全年都成熟，品种也有限；况且在冬天和寒冷的天气里，胃需要一点温热来辅助消化；因此要把苹果烤着吃。

===Original===
People did not eat nearly enough fruit nowadays; they had too much butcher's meat, and not enough fruit—that is, homegrown fruit, straight from orchard or garden, not the half-sour stuff sold in the shops, picked before it was ready.

The Americans were much wiser (he knew a good deal about America—he had been there in his early days, before thought superseded action)—the Americans had kept up many of the fine old English customs of two or three hundred years since, and among these was the eating of fruit. They were accused of being so modern, so very, very modern, but, in fact, the country Americans, with whom he had lived (and who had taught him how to chop) maintained much of the genuine antique life of old England.

They had first-rate apples, yet it was curious that the same trees produced an apple having a slightly different flavour to what it had in this country. You could always distinguish an American apple by its peculiar piquancy—a subacid piquancy, a wild strawberry piquancy, a sort of woodland, forest, backwoods delicacy of its own. And so on, and so on—"talk, talk, talk," as Mrs. Iden said.

===Chinese===
如今人们吃的水果远远不够；他们吃了太多的肉，而水果不足——这里指的是自家种的水果，直接从果园或菜园里摘下来的，而不是店里卖的那种半生不熟、酸溜溜的东西。

美国人聪明多了（他对美国了解颇多——他在年轻时去过那里，在思想取代行动之前）——美国人保留了许多两三百年前的英国古老美好习俗，其中就包括吃水果。人们指责他们太现代了，非常、非常现代，但事实上，他在乡下一起生活的美国乡民们（正是他们教会了他如何劈柴）保留了许多古老的英格兰真正的古朴生活方式。

他们有一流的苹果，但奇怪的是，同样的树在那边结出的苹果，味道与在本国略有不同。你总能通过美国苹果特有的辛辣味辨别出来——一种微酸的辛辣，一种野草莓的辛辣，一种属于林地、森林、蛮荒之地的独特风味。诸如此类，滔滔不绝——正如艾登夫人（Mrs. Iden）所说："唠叨，唠叨，唠叨。"

===Original===
After his baked apple he took another guilty look at the clock, it was close on four, and went into the passage to get his hat. In farmhouses these places are called passages; in the smallest of villas, wretched little villas not fit to be called houses, they are always "halls."

In the passage Mrs. Iden was waiting for him, and began to thump his broad though bowed back with all her might.

"Sleep, sleep, sleep!" she cried, giving him a thump at each word. "You've slept two hours. (Thump.) You sleep till you stupefy yourself (thump), and then you go and dig. What's the use of digging? (Thump.) Why don't you make some money? (Thump.) Talk and sleep! (Thump.) I hate it. (Thump.) You've rubbed the paint off the wainscot with your sleep, sleep, sleep (thump)—there's one of your hairs sticking to the paint where your head goes. (Thump.) Anything more hateful—sleep (thump), talk (thump), sleep (thump). Go on!"

===Chinese===
吃完烤苹果后，他又心虚地看了一眼钟，快四点了，于是走进过道去拿帽子。在农舍里，这些地方叫过道；在最小的别墅里——那些破败的小别墅根本不配称为宅邸——它们总是被称为"门厅"。

艾登夫人在过道里等着他，开始用尽全力捶打他那宽阔却佝偻的背。

"睡，睡，睡！"她一边喊，每说一个字就捶一下。"你睡了两个小时。（捶。）你睡到自己都昏昏沉沉（捶），然后就去挖地。挖地有什么用？（捶。）你为什么不去挣点钱？（捶。）唠叨和睡觉！（捶。）我讨厌这些。（捶。）你睡觉、睡觉、睡觉，把护墙板上的漆都蹭掉了（捶）——你脑袋靠着的地方，头发都粘在漆上了。（捶。）还有什么比这更可恶的——睡觉（捶），唠叨（捶），睡觉（捶）。接着走！"

===Original===
She had thumped him down the passage, and across the covered-in court to the door opening on the garden. There he paused to put on his hat—an aged, battered hat—some sort of nondescript bowler, broken, grey, weather-stained, very battered and very aged—a pitiful hat to put above that broad, Shakespearian forehead. While he fitted it on he was thumped severely: when he opened the door he paused, and involuntarily looked up at the sky to see about the weather—a habit all country people have—and so got more thumping, ending as he started out with a tremendous push. He did not seem to resent the knocks, nor did the push accelerate his pace; he took it very much as he took the March wind.

Mrs. Iden slammed the door, and went in to clear the dinner things, and make ready for tea. Amaryllis helped her.

"He'll want his tea in half an hour," said Mrs. Iden. "What's the use of his going out to work for half an hour?"

Amaryllis was silent. She was very fond of her father; he never did anything wrong in her eyes, and she could have pointed out that when he sat down to dinner at one he had already worked as many hours as Mrs. Iden's model City gentleman in a whole day. His dinner at one was, in fact, equivalent to their dinner at seven or eight, over which they frequently lingered an hour or two. He would still go on labouring, almost another half day. But she held her peace, for, on the other hand, she could not contradict and argue with her mother, whom she knew had had a wearisome life and perpetual disappointments.

===Chinese===
她一路捶着他走过过道，穿过带顶棚的庭院，来到通向花园的门口。他在那里停下脚步戴帽子——一顶破旧不堪的帽子——某种不伦不类的圆顶礼帽，破破烂烂，灰色的，风雨侵蚀过的，非常破旧，非常老旧——戴在那宽阔的、莎士比亚式的额头上实在可怜。他戴帽子时被狠狠捶打：他打开门时又停下脚步，不由自主地抬头看看天气——这是所有乡下人都有的习惯——于是又挨了一顿捶打，最后出门时还被猛地推了一把。他似乎并不反感这些捶打，推搡也没有加快他的脚步；他泰然处之，就像对待三月的寒风一样。

艾登夫人（Mrs. Iden）砰地关上门，进去收拾碗碟，准备茶点。阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）帮着她。

"再过半小时他就想喝茶了，"艾登夫人说，"他出去干这半小时的活有什么用？"

阿玛丽莉斯沉默不语。她非常爱她的父亲；在他眼里，他从未做错过什么，她本可以指出，他一点钟坐下来吃午餐时，已经干了和艾登夫人所推崇的那种体面城市绅士一整天一样多的活。他一点钟吃午餐，实际上相当于他们在七八点钟才吃的那顿晚餐——他们经常在晚餐桌上流连一两个小时。他之后还会继续干活，几乎是又一个半天。但她保持沉默，因为另一方面，她不能反驳和顶撞她的母亲，她知道母亲过着疲惫的生活，充满了无尽的失望。

===Original===
Mrs. Iden grumbled on to herself, working herself into a more fiery passion, till at last she put down the teapot, and rushed into the garden. There as she came round the first thing she saw was the daffodil, the beautiful daffodil Amaryllis had discovered. Beside herself with indignation—what was the use of flowers or potatoes?—Mrs. Iden stepped on the border and trampled the flower under foot till it was shapeless. After this she rushed indoors again and upstairs to her bedroom, where she locked herself in, and fumbled about in the old black oak chest of drawers till she found a faded lavender glove.

That glove had been worn at the old "Ship" at Brighton years and years ago in the honeymoon trip: in those days bridal parties went down by coach. Faded with years, it had also faded from the tears that had fallen upon it. She turned it over in her hands, and her tears spotted it once more.

Amaryllis went on with the tea-making; for her mother to rush away in that manner was nothing new. She toasted her father a piece of toast—he affected to despise toast, but he always ate it if it was there, and looked about for it if it was not, though he never said anything. The clock struck five, and out she went to tell him tea was ready. Coming round the house she found her daffodil crushed to pieces.

"Oh!" The blood rushed to her forehead; then her beautiful lips pouted and quivered; tears filled her eyes, and her breast panted. She knew immediately who had done it; she ran to her bedroom to cry and to hide her grief and indignation.

===Chinese===
艾登夫人继续自顾自地抱怨，越想越生气，最后放下茶壶，冲进了花园。她一转过弯，第一眼看到的就是那朵水仙花——阿玛丽莉斯发现的那朵美丽的水仙。她气得发狂——花或土豆有什么用？——艾登夫人踏上花坛，用脚把花踩得面目全非。之后她又冲进屋，上楼回到自己的卧室，锁上门，在旧的黑橡木抽屉柜里摸索，直到找出一只褪色的薰衣草色手套。

那只手套是多年前在布莱顿（Brighton）的老"船"旅馆度蜜月时戴过的：那时候新娘的队伍都是坐马车去的。岁月使它褪了色，泪水也让它褪了色。她把手套在手里翻来覆去，泪水又滴落在上面。

阿玛丽莉斯继续准备茶点；母亲那样冲出去对她来说并不新鲜。她给父亲烤了一片吐司——他装作看不起吐司，但只要有他就总会吃掉，没有的话还会四处找，虽然他什么也不说。钟敲了五下，她出去告诉父亲茶点准备好了。绕到房子那边，她发现自己的水仙花被踩得粉碎。

"哦！"血涌上了她的额头；接着她美丽的嘴唇撅起并颤抖起来；泪水盈满了双眼，胸口剧烈起伏。她立刻知道是谁干的；她跑进自己的卧室，哭泣着，掩饰着她的悲伤与愤怒。

## VI

===Original===
Lady-day Fair came round by and by, and Amaryllis, about eleven o'clock in the morning, went down the garden to the end of the orchard, where she could overlook the highway without being seen, and watch the folk go past. Just there the road began to descend into a hollow, while the garden continued level, so that Amaryllis, leaning her arm on the top of the wall, was much higher up than those who went along. The wall dropped quite fourteen feet down to the road, a rare red brick wall﻿—thick and closely-built, the bricks close together with thin seams of mortar, so that the fibres of the whole mass were worked and compressed and bound firm, like the fibres of a piece of iron. The deep red bricks had a colour﻿—a certain richness of stability﻿—and at the top this good piece of workmanship was protected from the weather by a kind of cap, and ornamented with a projecting ridge. Within the wall Amaryllis could stand on a slight bank, and easily look over it. Without there was a sheer red precipice of fourteen feet down to the dusty sward and nettles beside the road.

Some bare branches of a plum tree trained against the wall rose thin and tapering above it in a bunch, a sign of bad gardening, for they ought to have been pruned, and the tree, indeed, had an appearance of neglect. One heavy bough had broken away from the nails and list, and drooped to the ground, and the shoots of last year, not having been trimmed, thrust themselves forward presumptuously.

Behind the bunch of thin and tapering branches rising above the wall Amaryllis was partly hidden, but she relied a great deal more for concealment upon a fact Iden had taught her, that people very seldom look up; and consequently if you are only a little higher they will not see you. This she proved that morning, for not one of all who passed glanced up from the road. The shepherd kept his eye fixed on his sheep, and the drover on his bullocks; the boys were in a hurry to get to the fair and spend their pennies; the wenches had on a bit of blue ribbon or a new bonnet, and were perpetually looking at the traps that overtook them to see if the men admired their finery. No one looked up from the road they were pursuing.

The photographer fixes the head of the sitter by a sort of stand at the back, which holds it steady in one position while the camera takes the picture. In life most people have their heads fixed in the claws of some miserable pettiness, which interests them so greatly that they tramp on steadily forward, staring ahead, and there's not the slightest fear of their seeing anything outside the rut they are travelling.

Amaryllis did not care anything about the fair or the people either, knowing very well what sort they would be; but I suspect, if it had been possible to have got at the cause which brought her there, it would have been traced to the unconscious influence of sex, a perfectly innocent prompting, quite unrecognised by the person who feels it, and who would indignantly deny it if rallied on the subject, but which leads girls of her age to seize opportunities of observing the men, even if of an uninteresting order. Still they are men, those curious beings, that unknown race, and little bits of knowledge about them may, perhaps, be picked up by a diligent observer.

===Chinese===
圣母领报节集市一天天临近了。阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）大约上午十一点钟时走进花园，来到果园尽头，那里可以俯瞰大路而不被人发觉，方便观看路人经过。恰巧在那处路面开始下坡伸入低洼地带，而花园地面却依然平坦，所以阿玛丽莉斯把胳膊搭在墙头，便比下面经过的人高出许多。这道墙垂直落差足有十四英尺，直抵路面，是一堵难得一见的红砖墙——厚实而紧密，砖块挨着砖块，灰缝极薄，整个墙体的纤维经过压实绑紧，如同铁器的纹理一般牢固。深红色的砖块有一种颜色——一种沉稳丰润的色泽——墙顶用一种帽状结构保护着这件精良之作免受风雨侵蚀，还饰有一道凸出的脊线。墙内侧，阿玛丽莉斯站在一道矮土堆上，可以轻松探头望出去。墙外则是一道十四英尺的陡直红砖峭壁，直落到底下路面旁蒙尘的草坪和荨麻丛中。

一棵倚墙而植的李树伸出几根光秃秃的枝条，在墙头上方纤细地向上伸展成一簇，这是拙劣园艺的标志——本该修剪掉才是，这棵树看上去确实疏于照料。一根沉重的大枝从钉子和板条上脱落下来，垂到了地面，去年的枝条未经修剪，肆无忌惮地向前探出。

在这簇伸出墙头的纤细枝条后面，阿玛丽莉斯半遮半掩，但她更多地依赖一个艾登（Iden）教给她的事实来隐匿自己：人们极少抬头看；因此只要你稍微站高一点，他们就看不见你。那天早上她证实了这一点，因为所有路过的人没有一个从路上抬起头来。牧羊人眼睛盯着羊群，赶牛人盯着牛群；男孩们急着赶到集市去花掉他们的便士；姑娘们戴着一截蓝色缎带或一顶新帽子，不停地回头张望超过她们的轻便马车，看那些男人是否在欣赏她们的盛装。没有一个人从他们行走的路上抬起头来。

摄影师用一种后方支架固定被拍摄者的头部，在相机拍照时让头部保持不动。在生活中，大多数人的头也被某种可悲的琐事钳住了，那些事让他们如此着迷，以至于他们稳稳地朝前走着，两眼直盯着前方，根本不可能看到旅途轨道以外的任何东西。

阿玛丽莉斯对集市和那些人全都不在意，她很清楚来的是什么样的人；但我猜想，如果有可能追溯她来到这里的原因，多半会追溯到性别本能的无意识影响——一种完全无害的冲动，连感受到它的人都完全没有意识到，如果被人取笑还会愤怒否认，但它确实促使她这个年纪的女孩寻找机会观察男性，即使对象并不怎么有趣。不过他们毕竟是男性——那些神秘的生物，那个未知的种族——一个勤勉的观察者或许能从中拾得一些关于他们的知识碎片。

===Original===
The men who drifted along the road towards the Fair were no "mashers, by Jove!" Some of them, though young, were clad antiquely enough in breeches and gaiters﻿—not sportsmen's breeches and gaiters, but old-fashioned "granfer" things; the most of them were stout and sturdy, in drab and brown suits of good cloth, cut awry. Hundreds of them on foot, in traps, gigs, fourwheels, and on horseback, went under Amaryllis: but, though they were all Christians, there was not one "worth a Jewess' eye."

She scorned them all.

This member of the unknown race was too thickly made, short set, and squat; this one too fair﻿—quite white and moist-sugar looking; this one had a straight leg.

Another went by with a great thick and long black beard﻿—what a horrid thing, now, when kissing!﻿—and as he walked he wiped it with his sleeve, for he had just washed down the dust with a glass of ale. His neck, too, was red and thick; hideous, yet he was a "stout knave," and a man all over, as far as body makes a man.

But women are, like Shakespeare, better judges. "Care I for the thews and sinews of a man?" They look for something more than bulk.

===Chinese===
沿着大路向集市漂来的那些男人可不是什么"老天爷，好一个纨绔子弟！"其中一些虽然年轻，却穿着相当老派的短裤和绑腿——不是猎装短裤和绑腿，而是祖父辈那种老式行头；大多数人身板壮实、体格敦厚，穿着剪裁歪斜的褐色或棕色上等呢料套装。成百上千的人步行或坐着轻便马车、单马双轮车、四轮马车、骑着马从阿玛丽莉斯眼前经过：可尽管他们都是基督徒，却没有一个"值得犹太女人看上一眼"的。

她瞧不上他们所有人。

这个神秘种族的一员太粗短了，矮墩墩的，像个肉团；那个太白——白得像湿漉漉的白糖；这个腿太直。

又一个走过来，留着一把又浓又长的黑胡子——天哪，接吻的时候该多恶心！——他边走边用袖子擦胡子，因为他刚用一杯麦芽酒压下了灰尘。他的脖子又红又粗；丑是丑，却是个"壮实的家伙"，单看身板，是个十足的男人。

不过女人和莎士比亚一样，是更好的评判者。"我在乎男人的肌肉和筋骨吗？"她们看重的东西不止是块头。

===Original===
A good many of these fellows were more or less lame, for it is astonishing if you watch people go by and keep account of them what a number have game legs, both young and old.

A young buck on a capital horse was at the first glance more interesting﻿—paler, rakish, a cigar in his mouth, an air of viciousness and dash combined, fairly well dressed, pale whiskers and beard; in short, he knew as much of the billiard-table as he did of sheep and corn. When nearer Amaryllis disliked him more than all the rest put together; she shrank back a little from the wall lest he should chance to look up; she would have feared to have been alone with such a character, and yet she could not have said why. She would not have feared to walk side by side with the great black beard﻿—hideous as he was﻿—nor with any of the rest, not even with the roughest of the labourers who tramped along. This gentleman alone alarmed her.

There were two wenches, out for their Fair Day holiday, coming by at the same time; they had on their best dresses and hats, and looked fresh and nice. They turned round to watch him coming, and half waited for him; when he came up he checked his horse, and began to "cheek" them. Nothing loth, the village girls "cheeked" him, and so they passed on.

One or two very long men appeared, unusually clumsy, even in walking they did not know exactly what to do with their legs. Amaryllis had no objection to their being tall﻿—indeed, to be tall is often a passport to a "Jewess' eye"﻿—but they were so clumsy.

===Chinese===
这些家伙中有不少人或多或少都是瘸腿的，因为如果你留意观察路人并统计一下，不论老少，跛脚的人数之多令人吃惊。

一个骑着好马的年轻人第一眼看上去更有趣——更苍白、更风流，嘴里叼着雪茄，带着一股邪气与潇洒并存的派头，穿着还算考究，淡色络腮胡和连鬓胡子；简而言之，他对台球桌的了解和对羊群、谷物的了解一样多。等到近了，阿玛丽莉斯比讨厌其他所有人加起来还要讨厌他；她微微从墙边缩回身子，生怕他碰巧抬头看；她害怕和这样的人单独在一起，却又说不出究竟是为什么。她不会害怕和那个大黑胡子——尽管他丑得吓人——并肩行走，也不会害怕其他任何人，甚至不怕路上那些最粗鲁的苦力。唯独这位绅士让她感到恐惧。

与此同时有两个出来过集市日的姑娘路过；她们穿着最好的裙子和帽子，看起来清新可人。她们转过身来看他骑马过来，半停下来等他；等他走近了，他勒住马，开始和她们调起情来。两个村姑也毫不示弱地和他对嘴，就这样过去了。

接着出现了两个非常高的人，异常笨拙，连走路都不知道该怎么摆弄自己的腿。阿玛丽莉斯并不嫌弃他们个子高——事实上，高个子往往是赢得"犹太女人看上一眼"的通行证——但他们实在太笨拙了。

===Original===
Of the scores who went by in traps and vehicles she could not see much but their clothes and their faces, and both the clothes and the faces were very much alike. Rough, good cloth, ill-fitting (the shoulders were too broad for the tailor, who wanted to force Bond Street measurements on the British farmer's back); reddish, speckled faces, and yellowish hair and whiskers; big speckled hands, and that was all. Scores of men, precisely similar, were driven down the road. If those broad speckled hands had been shown to Jacob's ewes he need not have peeled rods to make them bring forth speckled lambs.

Against the stile a long way up the road there was a group of five or six men, who were there when she first peered over the wall, and made no further progress to the Fair. They were waiting till some acquaintance came by and offered a lift; lazy dogs, they could not walk. They had already been there long enough to have walked to the Fair and back, still they preferred to fold their hands and cross their legs, and stay on. So many people being anxious to get to the town, most of those who drove had picked up friends long before they got here.

===Chinese===
那些坐在轻便马车和车辆里经过的数十人，她只能看到他们的衣服和脸，而这两样东西都极其相似。粗糙的上等呢料、不合身的剪裁（裁缝想把邦德街的尺码硬套在英国农夫宽阔的背上）；泛红的斑点脸庞、淡黄的头发和络腮胡；一双粗糙的大手——仅此而已。数十个如出一辙的男人赶着车沿路而下。倘若把那些粗糙的大手展示给雅各（Jacob）的母羊看，他根本无需剥枝条就能让它们生出有斑点的羊羔了。

远处路边的台阶旁站着五六个男人，她第一次探身过墙时他们就在那儿了，始终没往集市方向再走一步。他们在等某个熟人路过好搭车；一群懒骨头，连路都懒得走。他们待在那儿的时间够走到集市再走回来的了，可他们宁愿双手一揣、两腿一交叉，就这么耗下去。这么多人急着进城，大多数赶车的人早在到达这里之前就已经捎上了朋友。

===Original===
The worst walker of all was a constable, whose huge boots seemed to take possession of the width of the road, for he turned them out at right angles, working his legs sideways to do it, an extraordinary exhibition of stupidity and ugliness, for which the authorities who drilled him in that way were responsible, and not the poor fellow.

Among the lowing cattle and the baaing sheep there drifted by a variety of human animals, tramps and vagrants, not nearly of so much value as the wool and beef.

It is curious that these "characters"—as they are so kindly called—have a way of associating themselves with things that promise vast enjoyment to others. The number of unhappy, shirtless wretches who thread their path in and out the coaches at the Derby is wonderful. While the champagne fizzes above on the roof, and the footman between the shafts sits on an upturned hamper and helps himself out of another to pie with truffles, the hungry, lean kine of human life wander round about sniffing and smelling, like Adam and Eve after the fall at the edge of Paradise.

There are such incredible swarms of vagrants at the Derby that you might think the race was got up entirely for their sakes. There would be thousands at Sandown, but the gate is locked with a half-crown bolt, and they cannot get a stare at the fashionables on the lawn. For all that, the true tramp, male or female, is so inveterate an attendant at races and all kinds of accessible entertainments and public events that the features of the fashionable are better known to him than to hundreds of well-to-do people unable to enter society.

===Chinese===
最糟糕的步行者是一个警察，他的大靴子似乎把整条路的宽度都据为己有了——因为他把脚尖朝外撇成直角，双腿侧着才能迈步，这是一种极其愚蠢和丑陋的表演，该为此负责的是那些把他训练成这副模样的当权者，而不是这个可怜的家伙。

在哞哞叫的牛群和咩咩叫的羊群中间，漂过各色人形动物——流浪汉和乞丐——他们的价值远远比不上那些羊毛和牛肉。

说来奇怪，这些"人物"——人们总是善意地这样称呼他们——有一种本事，总能把自己和那些许诺给他人巨大欢乐的事物联系在一起。德比马赛（Derby）上那些衣衫褴褛、一无所有的可怜虫穿行于马车之间的数量令人咋舌。当车顶上香槟嘶嘶作响、车夫坐在翻转的板条箱上从另一个箱子里拿出松露馅饼大快朵颐的时候，那些饥肠辘辘、瘦骨嶙峋的人形牲口就在周围嗅来嗅去，像被逐出乐园后徘徊在伊甸园边缘的亚当和夏娃。

桑当（Sandown）赛马场上也有数以千计的流浪汉，多到你会以为赛马完全是为他们举办的。不过桑当的大门用半克朗的锁锁着，他们连看一眼草坪上时髦人士的机会都没有。尽管如此，真正的流浪汉——无论男女——都极其热衷于参加赛马和各种能够进入的娱乐活动与公共场合，以至于时髦人物的面孔在他们眼中比数百个无法跻身社交圈的体面人士还要熟悉。

===Original===
So they paddled along to the fair, slip-slop, in the dust, among the cattle and sheep, hands in pockets, head hanging down, most of them followed at a short distance by a Thing.

This Thing is upright, and therefore, according to the old definition, ought to come within the genus Homo. It wears garments rudely resembling those of a woman, and there it ends. Perhaps it was a woman once; perhaps it never was, for many of them have never had a chance to enter the ranks of their own sex.

Amaryllis was too young, and, as a consequence, too full of her own strength and youth and joy in life to think for long or seriously about these curious Things drifting by like cattle and sheep. Yet her brow contracted, and she drew herself together as they passed—a sort of shiver, to think that there should be such degradation in the world. Twice when they came along her side of the road she dropped pennies in front of them, which they picked up in a listless way, just glancing over the ear in the direction the money fell, and went on without so much as recognizing where it came from.

If sheep were treated as unfortunate human beings are, they would take a bitter revenge; though they are the mildest of creatures, they would soon turn round in a venomous manner. If they did not receive sufficient to eat and drink, and were not well sheltered, they would take a bitter revenge: *they would die*. Loss of £sd!

But human beings have not even got the courage or energy to do that; they put up with anything, and drag on—miserables that they are.

I said they were not equal in value to the sheep—why, they're not worth anything when they're dead. You cannot even sell the skins of the Things!

Slip-slop in the dust they drive along to the fair, where there will be an immense amount of eating and a far larger amount of drinking all round them, in every house they pass, and up to midnight. They will see valuable animals, and men with well-lined pockets. What on earth can a tramp find to please him among all this? It is not for him; yet he goes to see it.

===Chinese===
就这样他们拖着脚步走向集市，在尘土中踉踉跄跄，在牛群和羊群中间穿行，手插在口袋里，脑袋耷拉着，大多数人身后不远处都跟着一个"东西"。

这东西直立行走，所以按照古老的定义，理应归入人类（Homo）的属。它穿着粗陋地模仿女人的衣物，仅此而已。也许它曾经是个女人；也许从来不是，因为它们中的许多从未有机会跻身自己性别的行列。

阿玛丽莉斯太年轻了，正因为年轻，她浑身充满了力量、青春和生活的喜悦，不可能长久或认真地去思考这些像牛羊一样漂过的奇怪东西。然而当它们经过时，她还是皱起了眉头，身体微微蜷缩——一种不自觉的战栗——想到世界上竟然有这样的堕落。有两次，当它们来到她这边的路边时，她把便士丢在它们面前，它们无精打采地捡起来，只是朝钱币落下的方向瞥了一眼，就继续往前走了，甚至没有意识到钱是从谁那里来的。

如果羊被对待得像这些不幸的人类那样，它们会进行痛苦的报复；虽然它们是最温和的生灵，但很快就会以恶毒的方式反咬一口。如果它们得不到足够的食物和水，没有良好的遮风避雨之处，它们会进行痛苦的报复：它们会死。损失英镑、先令、便士！

但人类连那样的勇气和精力都没有；他们什么都忍耐，就这么拖下去——这些可怜虫。

我说它们的价值不如羊——为什么呢，它们死了连一文钱都不值。你甚至连那些东西的皮都卖不掉！

就这样在尘土中踉踉跄跄地走向集市，那里会有海量的食物和更多的酒水围绕着他们，经过的每一所房子里都有，一直持续到午夜。他们会看到珍贵的牲畜和口袋鼓鼓的人。一个流浪汉在这所有一切中究竟能找到什么令他高兴的呢？这些东西不是为他准备的；可他还是要去看。

## VII

===Original===
The crowd began to pass more thickly, when Amaryllis saw a man coming up the road in the opposite direction to that in which the multitude was moving. They were going to the fair; he had his back to it, and a party in a trap rallied him smartly for his folly.

"What! bean't you a-going to fair? Why, Measter Duck, what's up? Looking for a thunderstorm?"—which young ducks are supposed to enjoy. "Ha! ha! ha!"

Measter Duck, with a broad grin on his face, nevertheless plodded up the hill, and passed beneath Amaryllis.

She knew him very well, for he lived in the hamlet, but she would not have taken any notice of him had he not been so elaborately dressed. His high silk hat shone glossy; his black broadcloth coat was new and carefully brushed; he was in black all over, in contrast with the mass of people who had gone by that morning. A blue necktie, bright and clean, spotless linen, gloves rolled up in a ball in one hand, whiskers brushed, boots shining, teeth clean, Johnny was off to the fair!

===Chinese===
路上的人流渐渐稠密起来，阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）看见一个人沿着大路迎面走来，与人群行进的方向恰好相反。大伙儿都往集市去；他却背对着集市，一辆轻便马车上的人毫不留情地取笑他的犯傻。

"哎！你不去赶集吗？鸭老爷（Measter Duck），怎么了？在等雷雨？"——据说小鸭子是喜欢雷雨的。"哈哈哈！"

鸭老爷满脸堆笑，仍旧步履沉稳地走上山来，从阿玛丽莉斯脚下经过。

她对他非常熟悉，他就住在那个小村子里，但若不是他穿戴得如此考究，她原本不会留意他。他那顶高筒礼帽擦得锃亮；黑色宽幅呢子外套是新做的，仔细刷过；他通身黑色，与那天早上经过的那一大群人形成鲜明对比。一条蓝色领带鲜亮干净，衬衫洁白无瑕，一副手套团成一团攥在手里，连鬓胡子梳理过，皮鞋锃亮，牙齿也干干净净——约翰尼（Johnny）赶集去也！

===Original===
The coat fitted him to a nicety; it had, in fact, no chance to do otherwise, for his great back and shoulders stretched it tight, and would have done so had it been made like a sack. Of all the big men who had gone by that day Jack Duck was the biggest; his back was immense, and straight, too, for he walked upright for a farmer, nor was his bulk altogether without effect, for he was not overburdened with abdomen, so that it showed to the best advantage. He was a little over the average height, but not tall; he had grown laterally.

He could lift two sacks of wheat from the ground. You just try to lift *one*.

His sleeves were too long, so that only the great knuckles of his speckled hands were visible. Red whiskers, red hair, blue eyes, speckled face, straight lips, thick, like the edge of an earthenware pitcher, and of much the same coarse red hue, always a ready grin, a round, hard head, which you might have hit safely with a mallet; and there is the picture.

===Chinese===
那件外套合身极了——事实上它也没有不合身的余地，因为他宽阔的后背和肩膀把它撑得紧绷绷的，哪怕做成麻袋样式也一样会撑紧。当天经过的大个子男人里，杰克·达克（Jack Duck）是最大的；他的后背宽阔无比，而且挺直——对一个农夫来说走姿相当端正——他的体格也不是全无效果，因为他没有大腹便便，所以宽厚的身板得以最佳呈现。他比平均身高略高一点，但算不上高；他是横向发展的。

他能从地上提起两袋麦子。你去试着提提*一袋*看看。

他的袖子太长，只露出那双斑驳大手上粗壮的指关节。红胡子，红头发，蓝眼睛，斑驳的脸，笔直的嘴唇，厚厚的，像陶罐的口沿，颜色也差不多是那种粗糙的红，总是一副随时准备咧嘴笑的样子，一颗圆滚滚的硬脑袋——你尽可以用木槌放心敲打。这就是他的肖像了。

===Original===
For some reason, very big men do not look well in glossy black coats and silk hats; they seem to want wideawkes, bowlers, caps, anything rather than a Paris hat, and some loose-cut jacket of a free-and-easy colour, suitable for the field, or cricket, or boating. They do not belong to the town and narrow doorways; Nature grew them for hills and fields.

Compared with the Continental folk, most Englishmen are big, and therefore, as their "best" suits do not fit in with their character as written in limbs and shoulders, the Continent thinks us clumsy. The truth is, it is the Continent that is little.

===Chinese===
不知为什么，大个子男人穿黑色亮面礼服、戴丝绸礼帽就是不好看；他们似乎该配平顶草帽、圆顶礼帽、便帽——什么都行，就是别戴巴黎式的礼帽——再配一件宽松剪裁、颜色随便的外套，适合下地、打板球或划船的那种。他们不属于城市和狭窄的门廊；大自然生养他们是为了山丘和田野。

跟欧洲大陆的人相比，大多数英国男人都算高大，因此当他们的"最好的"套装与他们四肢和肩膀所展现的性格格格不入时，大陆人便觉得我们笨拙。事实是，笨拙的是大陆人——他们太小了。

===Original===
"Isn't he ugly?" thought Amaryllis, looking down on poor John Duck. "Isn't he ugly?" Now the top of the wall was crusted with moss, which has a way of growing into bricks and mortar, and attaching particles of brick to its roots. As she watched the people she unconsciously trifled with a little piece of moss—her hand happened at the moment to project over the wall, and as John Duck went under she dropped the bit of moss straight on his glossy hat. Tap! the fragment of brick adhering to the moss struck the hollow hat smartly like a drum.

She drew back quickly, laughing and blushing, and angry with herself all at the same time, for she had done it without a thought.

Jack pulled off his hat, saw nothing, and put it on again, suspecting that someone in a passing gig had "chucked" something at him.

In a minute Amaryllis peeped over the wall, and, seeing his broad back a long way up the road, resumed her stand.

"How ever could I do such a stupid thing?" she thought. "But isn't he ugly? Aren't they *all* ugly? All of them—horridly ugly."

===Chinese===
"他可真丑啊？"阿玛丽莉斯低头看着可怜的杰克·达克（John Duck），心里想着。"他可真丑啊？"此刻墙头覆着一层苔藓，苔藓总爱钻进砖缝和灰浆里，根上还能粘住砖屑。她望着路人，不知不觉地把玩起一小块苔藓——她的手恰好探出了墙外，杰克·达克从下面经过时，她把那块苔藓正正地丢在了他锃亮的礼帽上。嗒！苔藓上粘着的那片碎砖敲在空心帽顶上，发出一声清脆的鼓响。

她迅速缩回来，又笑又脸红，同时又生自己的气，因为她完全是无意中做出来的。

杰克摘下帽子，什么也没看见，又戴了回去，怀疑是旁边经过的轻便马车上有人朝他"扔"了什么东西。

过了一分钟，阿玛丽莉斯探头往外看，见他宽厚的背影已经在大路远处了，才重新站好。

"我怎么干了这么蠢的事？"她想。"可他真的很丑啊。他们难道不*都*丑吗？所有人——丑得可怕。"

===Original===
The entire unknown race of Man was hideous. So coarse in feature—their noses were thick, half an inch thick, or enormously long and knobbed at the end like a walking-stick, or curved like a reaping-hook, or slewed to one side, or flat as if they had been smashed, or short and stumpy and incomplete, or spotted with red blotches, or turned up in the vulgarest manner—nobody had a good nose.

Their eyes were goggles, round and staring—like liquid marbles—they had no eyelashes, and their eyebrows were either white and invisible, or shaggy, as if thistles grew along their foreheads.

Their cheeks were speckled and freckled and red and brick-dust and leather-coloured, and enclosed with scrubby whiskers, like a garden hedge.

Upon the whole, those who shaved and were smooth looked worse than those who did not, for they thus exposed the angularities of their chins and jaws.

They wore such horrid hats on the top of these roughly-sketched faces—sketched, as it were, with a bit of burnt stick. Some of them had their hats on the backs of their heads, and some wore them aslant, and some jammed over their brows.

They went along smoking and puffing, and talking and guffawing in the vulgarest way, *en route* to swill and smoke and puff and guffaw somewhere else.

Whoever could tell what they were talking about? these creatures.

They had no form or grace like a woman—no lovely sloped shoulders, no beautiful bosom, no sweeping curve of robe down to the feet. No softness of cheek, or silky hair, or complexion, or taper fingers, or arched eyebrows; no sort of style whatever. They were mere wooden figures; and, in short, sublimely ugly.

===Chinese===
整个人类这个未知种族都是丑陋的。五官如此粗陋——鼻子又厚，足有半英寸厚，或者长得离谱、末端鼓起像根手杖，或者弯得像把镰刀，或者歪向一边，或者扁塌塌的像被砸过，或者又短又粗又残缺，或者布满红色斑块，或者朝天翘得俗不可耐——没有一个人的鼻子长得好看。

他们的眼睛像护目镜，圆鼓鼓、直愣愣的——像液态弹珠——没有睫毛，眉毛不是白得看不见，就是乱蓬蓬的，好像额头沿上长了一排蓟草。

他们的脸颊斑斑点点，有雀斑，有红色，有砖粉色，有皮革色，周围围着一圈乱糟糟的连鬓胡子，像一圈花园篱笆。

总体来看，那些刮了胡子、面部光滑的人反而比不刮的更难看，因为他们就这样暴露了下巴和颌骨的棱角。

这些粗陋的面孔——仿佛是用一段烧焦的木炭随手画出来的——顶上还戴着那么难看的帽子。有的把帽子歪在后脑勺，有的戴得斜斜的，有的扣在眉骨上。

他们一路走一路抽烟喷雾，说着话，粗声大笑，俗不可耐，*赶往*另一个地方去继续灌酒、抽烟、喷雾、粗笑。

谁又能知道这些家伙在说什么呢？

他们没有女人的体态和优雅——没有好看的斜肩，没有美丽的胸脯，没有长裙拂地的优雅弧线。没有柔软的脸颊，没有丝绸般的头发，没有好肤色，没有纤细的手指，没有弯弯的眉毛；没有一丝一毫的风度。他们不过是些木头人偶；总而言之，丑得登峰造极。

===Original===
There was a good deal of truth in Amaryllis' reflections; it was a pity a woman was not taken into confidence when the men were made.

Suppose the women were like the men, and we had to make love to such a set of bristly, grisly wretches!—pah! shouldn't we think them ugly! The patience of the women, putting up with us so long!

As for the muscles on which we pride ourselves so much, in a woman's eyes (though she prefers a strong man) they simply increase our extraordinary ugliness.

But if we look pale, and slim, and so forth, then they despise us, and there is no doubt that altogether the men were made wrong.

"And Jack's the very ugliest of the lot," thought Amaryllis. "He just *is* ugly."

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯的这番想法倒颇有几分道理；可惜造男人的时候没有把女人叫来参详参详。

要是女人也长得跟男人一样，而我们非得去向这么一帮毛扎扎、阴森森的可怜虫求爱不可！——呸！我们还不得觉得她们丑死了！女人的耐心，居然容忍我们这么久！

至于我们引以为豪的肌肉，在女人眼里（虽然她们更喜欢强壮的男人）不过是给我们本已惊人的丑陋再添几分罢了。

可如果我们长得白净清瘦，她们又瞧不起我们，总而言之，男人就是被造错了。

"而杰克是所有人里最丑的，"阿玛丽莉斯想。"他简直*就是*丑。"

===Original===
Pounding up the slope, big John Duck came by-and-by to the gateway, and entering without ceremony, as is the custom in the country, found Mr. Iden near the back door talking to a farmer who had seated himself on a stool.

He was a middle-aged man, stout and florid, rough as a chunk of wood, but dressed in his best brown for the fair. Tears were rolling down his vast round cheeks as he expatiated on his grievances to Mr. Iden:—

"Now, just you see how I be helped up with this here 'ooman," he concluded as Duck arrived. Mr. Iden, not a little glad of an opportunity to escape a repetition of the narrative, to which he had patiently listened, took Jack by the arm, and led him indoors. As they went the man on the stool extended his arm towards them hopelessly:—"Just you see how I be helped up with this here 'ooman!"

A good many have been "helped up" with a woman before now.

===Chinese===
大个子杰克·达克砰砰砰地走上斜坡，来到大门口，像乡间惯例那样不敲门便径直走了进去，在后门附近发现艾登（Iden）先生正和一个坐在凳子上的农夫说话。

那农夫是个中年人，身材壮实、面色红润，粗得像一块木头，但为了赶集穿上了最好的棕色衣服。他正向艾登先生倾诉自己的满腹委屈，泪水顺着圆鼓鼓的大脸滚落——

"来，你瞧瞧我被这娘们折腾成什么样了，"杰克进门时他刚好说完。艾登先生正巴不得有机会逃开——他已经耐心听了一遍，实在不想再听第二遍——于是拉起杰克的胳膊，领他进了屋。他们走的时候，凳子上的男人朝他们徒劳地伸出胳膊——"你瞧瞧我被这娘们折腾成什么样了！"

被一个女人"折腾"过的人自古以来可不少。

===Original===
Mrs. Iden met Jack with a gracious smile—she always did—yet there could not have imagined a man less likely to have pleased her.

A quick, nervous temperament, an eye sharp to detect failings or foolishness, an admirer of briskness and vivacity, why did she welcome John Duck, that incarnation of stolidity and slowness, that enormous mountain of a man? Because extremes meet? No, since she was always complaining of Iden's dull, motionless life; so it was not the contrast to her own disposition that charmed her.

John Duck was Another Man—not Mr. Iden.

The best of matrons like to see Another Man enter their houses; there's no viciousness in it, it is simply nature, which requires variety. The best of husbands likes to have another woman—or two, or three—on a visit; there's nothing wrong, it is innocent enough, and but gives a spice to the monotony of existence.

Besides, John Duck, that mountain of slowness and stolidity, was not perhaps a fool, notwithstanding his outward clumsiness. A little attention is appreciated even by a matron of middle age.

===Chinese===
艾登夫人（Mrs. Iden）微笑着和杰克打招呼——她一向如此——可你无论如何也想不到竟会有这么一个男人能讨她欢心。

一个敏捷而神经质的性情，一双善于挑错和发现蠢事的眼睛，欣赏干脆利落和活泼生动，她为什么要热情迎接杰克·达克（John Duck）——那个迟钝木讷的化身，那个巨大的肉山？是因为物极必反？不对，因为她一直在抱怨艾登沉闷无趣的生活；所以吸引她的并不是和她自己性情的反差。

杰克·达克是"另一个男人"——不是艾登先生。

再贤淑的主妇也乐意看到"另一个男人"走进自家门；这里面没有邪念，不过是天性使然，而天性需要多样性。再忠诚的丈夫也乐意让另一个女人——或两个、三个——来家里做客；没有什么不对的，完全是清白无事的，不过是给单调的生活添一点滋味罢了。

况且杰克·达克——那座迟钝木讷的大山——尽管外表笨拙，说不定并不蠢。一点殷勤即便是对一个中年主妇来说也是受用的。

===Original===
"Will you get us some ale?" said Iden; and Mrs. Iden brought a full jug with her own hands—a rare thing, for she hated the Goliath barrel as Iden enjoyed it.

"Going to the fair, Mr. Duck?"

"Yes, m'm," said John, deep in his chest and gruff, about as a horse might be expected to speak if he had a voice. "You going, m'm? I just come up to ask if you'd ride in my dog-trap?"

John had a first-rate turnout.

Mrs. Iden, beaming with smiles, replied that she was not going to the fair.

"Should be glad to take you, you know," said John, dipping into the ale. "Shall you be going presently?"—to Mr. Iden. "Perhaps you'd have a seat?"

"Hum!" said Iden, fiddling with his chin, a trick he had when undecided. "I don't zactly know; fine day, you see; want to see that hedge grubbed; want to fill up the gaps; want to go over to the wood meads; thought about—"

===Chinese===
"给我们弄点麦芽酒来好吗？"艾登说。艾登夫人亲自端来满满一壶——这可是稀罕事，因为她讨厌歌利亚（Goliath）酒桶，而艾登却引以为乐。

"赶集去吗，鸭先生？"

"嗯，太太，"杰克说，声音低沉粗哑，差不多就像一匹马若能开口说话你所期待的那种声音。"您去吗，太太？我来就是想问您愿不愿意坐我的狗车去？"

杰克有一辆一流的轻便马车。

艾登夫人笑容满面地回答说她不去赶集。

"很乐意送您去，您知道的，"杰克说着，在麦芽酒里蘸了蘸。"您一会儿去吗？"——转向艾登先生。"也许您会有个位子坐？"

"嗯！"艾登说，摸着下巴——这是他拿不定主意时的习惯动作。"我说不准；您看，天气这么好；想去把那道树篱刨掉；想把缺口补上；想去木草地那边看看；想——"

===Original===
"There, take and go!" said Mrs. Iden. "Sit there thinking—take and go."

"I can't say zactly, John; don't seem to have anything to go vor."

"What do other people go for?" said Mrs. Iden, contemptuously. "Why can't you do like other people? Get on your clean shirt, and go. Jack can wait—he can talk to Amaryllis while you dress."

"Perhaps Miss would like to go," suggested John, very quietly, and as if it was no consequence to him; the very thing he had called for, to see if he could get Amaryllis to drive in with him. He knew that Mrs. Iden never went anywhere, and that Mr. Iden could not make up his mind in a minute—he would require three or four days at least—so that it was quite safe to ask them first.

"Of course she would," said Mrs. Iden. "She is going—to dine with her grandfather; it will save her a long walk. You had better go and ask her; she's down at Plum Corner, watching the people."

"So I wull," said Jack, looking out of the great bow window at the mention of Plum Corner—he could just see the flutter of Amaryllis' dress in the distance between the trees. That part of the garden was called Plum Corner because of a famous plum tree—the one that had not been pruned and was sprawling about the wall.

===Chinese===
"得了，去就去！"艾登夫人说。"坐在这儿想——去就去。"

"我说不准，约翰；似乎没什么非去不可的理由。"

"别人去是为什么？"艾登夫人轻蔑地说。"你为什么不能像别人一样？穿上干净衬衫，去。杰克等得及——你换衣服的时候他可以和阿玛丽莉斯说话。"

"也许小姐愿意去，"杰克轻声提议，仿佛这事对他无所谓；他来就是为了这个——看看能不能让阿玛丽莉斯跟他坐车去。他知道艾登夫人从来不出门，也知道艾登先生不可能一时拿定主意——至少要磨蹭三四天——所以先问他们俩是完全没有风险的。

"她当然愿意去，"艾登夫人说。"她是打算去的——去她外公家吃饭；坐你的车正好省得她走一大段路。你最好去问问她；她在梅子角（Plum Corner）那边，看路人呢。"

"那我去，"杰克一听到梅子角便朝大弓形窗望去——他能远远看到阿玛丽莉斯的裙裾在树丛间飘动。花园那一片叫梅子角，是因为有一棵出名的李树——就是那棵没修剪、在墙头四散蔓延的李树。

===Original===
Mr. Iden had planted that plum tree specially for Mrs. Iden, because she was so fond of a ripe luscious plum. But of late years he had not pruned it.

"Vine ale!" said John, finishing his mug. "Extra vine ale!"

"It be, bean't it?" said Mr. Iden.

It really was humming stuff, but John well knew how proud Iden was of it, and how much he liked to hear it praised.

The inhabitants of the City of London conceitedly imagine that no one can be sharp-witted outside the sound of Bow Bells—country people are stupid. My opinion is that clumsy Jack Duck, who took about half an hour to write his name, was equal to most of them.

===Chinese===
艾登先生种那棵李树是专门为艾登夫人种的，因为她特别爱吃熟透多汁的李子。可近年来他没再修剪过。

"好酒！"杰克喝完杯中酒，说道。"顶好的酒！"

"是不错吧？"艾登先生说。

那酒确实是上好的佳酿，但杰克很清楚艾登对自己的酒有多自豪，也有多爱听人夸赞。

伦敦城的居民自负地以为弓区钟声（Bow Bells）以外就没有聪明人——乡下人都是蠢货。我的看法是，笨手笨脚的杰克·达克——他写自己名字大约要花上半个钟头——不比他们中大多数差。

## VIII

===Original===
The ale being ended, Iden walked with him through the orchard.

"Famous wall that," said John, presently, nodding towards the great red brick wall which adorned that side of the place. "Knowed how to build walls in those days."

"No such wall as that anywhere about here," said Iden, as proud of his wall as his ale. "No such bricks to be got. Folk don't know how to put up a wall now—you read in the papers how the houses valls down in Lunnon."

"Sort of cracks and comes in like—jest squashes up," said John.

===Chinese===
酒喝完了，艾登（Iden）领着他穿过果园。

"这墙可真不错，"约翰（John）说道，朝那边那堵气派的红砖墙点了点头。"那年头的人确实会砌墙。"

"这附近哪还有这样的墙，"艾登说，对自己的墙和自己的酒一样得意。"这砖哪儿也找不着。如今的人早不会砌墙了——你在报纸上看到没有，伦敦的那些房子动不动就塌。"

"裂了缝就往里塌——整个儿瘪下去，"约翰说。

===Original===
"Now, that's a real bit of brickwork," said Iden. "That'll last—ah, last—"

"No end to it," said John, who had admired the wall forty times before, thinking to himself as he saw Amaryllis leaning over the corner, "Blessed if I don't think as 'twas she as dropped summat on my hat." This strengthened his hopes; he had a tolerably clear idea that Mr. and Mrs. Iden were not averse to his suit; but he was doubtful about Amaryllis herself.

Amaryllis had not the slightest idea Duck had so much as looked at her—he called often, but seemed absorbed in the ale and gossip. Fancy her scorn if she had guessed!

===Chinese===
"这才是真正的砖砌活儿，"艾登说。"这墙能撑——啊，撑下去——"

"没个尽头，"约翰说。他已经把这面墙夸了四十回了，此刻看见阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）靠在墙角，心里暗想："老天作证，我觉得就是她往我帽子上掉了什么东西。"这念头让他信心大增；他相当清楚艾登夫妇对他的追求并不反感；但阿玛丽莉斯本人的态度，他没把握。

阿玛丽莉斯压根儿不知道达克（Duck）正眼瞧过她——他倒是常来，但好像只顾着喝酒聊天。她要是猜到了，不知道会怎么瞧不起他呢！

===Original===
John Duck was considered one of the most eligible young men thereabouts, for though by no means born in the purple of farming, it was believed he was certain to be very "warm" indeed when his father died. Old Duck, the son of a common labourer, occupied two or three of the finest farms in the neighbourhood. He made his money in a wagon—a curious place, you will say; why so? Have you ever seen the dingy, dark china-closets they call offices in the City? Have you ever ascended the dirty, unscrupbed, disgraceful staircase that leads to a famous barrister's "chambers"? These are far less desirable, surely, than a seat in a wagon in a beautiful meadow or cornfield. Old Duck, being too ponderous to walk, was driven about in a wagon, sitting at the rear with his huge, short legs dangling down; and, the wagon being halted in a commanding position, he overlooked his men at work.

One day he was put in a cart instead, and the carter walking home beside the horse, and noting what a pull it was for him up the hills, and drawling along half asleep, quite forgot his master, and dreamed he had a load of stones. By-and-by, he pulled out the bar, and shot Old Duck out. "A shot me out," grumbled the old man, "as if I'd a been a load of flints."

===Chinese===
约翰·达克（John Duck）被公认为这一带最体面的年轻人之一，虽说他绝不是生在农庄主的紫色贵胄之家，但人人都相信等他老爹一死，他准能"阔"起来。老达克是个普通庄稼汉的儿子，却经营着附近两三座最好的农场。他发财全靠一辆大车——你会说这可真新鲜；为什么这么说呢？你可曾见过伦敦城里那些昏暗肮脏、管它们叫"事务所"的瓷器铺？你可曾爬上那又脏又破、丢人现眼的楼梯，去找某位赫赫有名的出庭律师的"办公室"？比起在美景如画的草地或麦田里坐在大车上舒服，那些地方可差远了。老达克身板太沉，走不动路，每天让人用大车拉着到处转悠，坐在车尾，两条粗短腿耷拉着；大车停在高处，他就居高临下看着雇工们干活。

有一天他被装进了一辆运货马车，赶车人牵着马往回走，爬坡时觉得吃力，正迷迷糊糊地拖着步子走，完全忘了车上坐的是谁，满脑子想的是一车石头。过了一会儿，他拔掉车挡，把老达克倒了出去。"像倒一车燧石似的把我倒出来了，"老人嘟囔道。

===Original===
Riding about in this rude chariot the old fellow had amassed considerable wealth—his reputation for money was very great indeed—and his son John would, of course, come in for it.

John felt sure of Mr. and Mrs. Iden, but about Amaryllis he did not know. The idea that she had dropped "summat" on his hat raised his spirits immensely.

Now Amaryllis was not yet beautiful—she was too young; I do not think any girl is really beautiful so young—she was highly individualized, and had a distinct character, as it were, in her face and figure. You saw at a glance that there was something about her very different from other girls, something very marked, but it was not beauty yet.

Whether John thought her handsome, or saw that she would be, or what, I do not know; or whether he looked "forrard," as he would have said.

===Chinese===
这老头儿坐着这辆粗陋的车到处晃悠，竟攒下了偌大的家业——他有钱的名声可大着呢——他的儿子约翰自然会继承这一切。

约翰觉得吃准了艾登夫妇的心思，但阿玛丽莉斯那边就难说了。想到她往他帽子上"掉了什么东西"，他心里高兴极了。

阿玛丽莉斯现在还算不上漂亮——她年纪太小；我看这么小的姑娘还谈不上真正的美——她极有个性，那张脸、那身段，仿佛都带着鲜明的棱角。你一眼就能看出她跟别的姑娘大不相同，有种很突出的东西，但那还不是美。

约翰是觉得她好看，还是看出她将来会好看，还是别的什么，我就不知道了；或者用他自己的话说，他是在"向前看"。

> "Heigh for a lass with a tocher!"

===Original===
John had never read Burns, and would not have known that tocher meant dowry; nor had he seen the advice of Tennyson—

> "Doesn't thee marry for money,
 
 But go where money lies."

but his native intelligence needed no assistance from the poets, coronetted or otherwise.

===Chinese===
约翰没读过彭斯（Burns），也不知道"tocher"是嫁妆的意思；他也没见过丁尼生（Tennyson）的那句劝告——

> "你别为钱嫁人，
 
 可要嫁到有钱的地方去。"

但他天生的精明劲儿用不着什么诗人——不论顶着冠冕的还是不顶的——来指点。

===Original===
It was patent to everyone that her father, Iden, was as poor as the raggedest coat in Christendendom could make him; but it was equally well known and a matter of public faith, that her grandfather, the great miller and baker, Lord Lardy-Cake, as the boys called him derisively, had literally bushels upon bushels of money. He was a famous stickler for ancient usages, and it was understood that there were twenty thousand spade guineas in an iron box under his bed. Any cottager in the whole country side could have told you so, and would have smiled at your ignorance; the thing was as well known as that St. Paul's is in the City.

Besides which there was another consideration, old Granfer Iden was a great favourite at Court—Court meaning the mansion of the Hon. Raleigh Pamment, the largest landowner that side of the county. Granfer Iden entered the Deer Park (which was private) with a special key whenever he pleased, he strolled about the gardens, looked in at the conservatory, chatted familiarly with the royal family of Pamment when they were at home, and when they were away took any friend he chose through the galleries and saloons.

===Chinese===
人人都知道她父亲艾登穷得叮当响，穷得跟基督教世界里最破烂的外套似的；但同样尽人皆知、被当成公论的是，她祖父——那位大磨坊主兼面包师，孩子们嘲笑地叫他"奶油糕老爷"（Lord Lardy-Cake）——手里攥着成堆成堆的金币。他是个出了名的守旧派，大家都知道他床底下的铁皮箱子里装着两万把铲形几尼（spade guineas）。乡下随便哪个佃农都能告诉你这回事，还会笑话你连这都不知道；这事儿跟圣保罗大教堂（St. Paul's）在伦敦城里一样出名。

此外还有一层考虑，老格兰弗·艾登（Granfer Iden）在"宫廷"里极受宠——这里说的"宫廷"，是指本郡最大的地主、尊敬的拉利·帕门特（Raleigh Pamment）老爷的庄园。老格兰弗·艾登随时都能拿着特制钥匙进鹿苑（那是私人猎场），在花园里溜达，逛逛温室，帕门特家的人在时就和他们聊天，人家不在时就随便带朋友在长廊和大厅里转悠。

===Original===
"Must be summat at the bottom on't," said John Duck to himself many a time and oft. "They stuck-up proud folk wouldn't have he there if there wasn't summat at the bottom on't." A favourite at Court could dispense, no doubt, many valuable privileges.

Amaryllis heard their talk as they came nearer, and turned round and faced them. She wore a black dress, but no hat; instead she had carelessly thrown a scarlet shawl over her head, mantilla fashion, and held it with one hand. Her dark ringlets fringed her forehead, blown free and wild; the fresh air had brought a bright colour into her cheeks. As is often the case with girls whose figure is just beginning to show itself, her dress seemed somewhat shortened in front—lifted up from her ankles, which gave the effect of buoyancy to her form, she seemed about to walk though standing still. There was a defiant light in her deep brown eyes, that sort of "I don't care" disposition which our grandmothers used to say would take us to the gallows. Defiance, wilfulness, rebellion, was expressed in the very way she stood on the bank, a little higher than they were, and able to look over their heads.

"Marning," said John, rocking his head to one side as a salute.

"Marning," repeated Amaryllis, mocking his broad pronunciation.

===Chinese===
"这里头肯定有门道，"约翰·达克一遍又一遍地自言自语。"那些傲慢的家伙才不会让他进去呢，要是里头没有门道的话。"在"宫廷"里受宠的人，自然能施舍不少值钱的好处。

阿玛丽莉斯听到他们走近了说话声，转过身来面对着他们。她穿着一件黑裙子，没戴帽子；而是随意披了一条猩红的披肩，像西班牙mantilla那样盖在头上，用一只手拢着。深色的鬈发垂在额前，被风吹得自由而狂放；清新的空气在她脸颊上染出了一层红润。正如那些身形刚发育的女孩常有的样子，她的裙子在前面似乎短了些——从脚踝处往上提起，使她的身姿带有一种轻盈感，仿佛站着不动也随时要走。她深棕色的眼睛里有一股桀骜的光芒，就是祖母们常说的那种会把我们送上绞刑架的"满不在乎"的性子。她站在土坡上——比他们高一点，能俯视他们的头顶——那站姿本身就透着倔强、任性、反叛。

"早上好，"约翰说，把脑袋歪向一边算是打招呼。

"早上好，"阿玛丽莉斯学着他那土气的口音重复道。

===Original===
As John could not get any further Iden helped him.

"Jack's going to the fair," he said, "and thought you would like to ride with him. Run in and dress."

"I shan't ride," said Amaryllis, "I shall walk."

"Longish way," said John. "Mor'n two mile."

"I shall walk," said Amaryllis, decidedly.

"Lot of cattle about," said John.

"Better ride," said Iden.

"No," said Amaryllis, and turned her back on them to look over the wall again.

She was a despot already. There was nothing left for them but to walk away.

"However," said Iden, always trying to round things off and make square edges smooth, "very likely you'll overtake her and pick her up."

"Her wull go across the fields," said John. "Shan't see her."

As he walked down the road home for his dog-trap he looked up at the corner of the wall, but she was not looking over then. Mrs. Iden had fetched her in, as it was time to dress.

===Chinese===
约翰接不上话了，艾登便替他帮腔。

"杰克要去赶集，"他说，"想着你兴许乐意跟他一道骑马去。进去换身衣裳吧。"

"我不骑马，"阿玛丽莉斯说，"我走路去。"

"路不近呢，"约翰说。"有两英里多。"

"我走路去，"阿玛丽莉斯斩钉截铁地说。

"路上牛多，"约翰说。

"还是骑马好，"艾登说。

"不，"阿玛丽莉斯说，背对他们，又去看墙外了。

她已经是个小暴君了。他们两个只好走开。

"不过，"艾登总是想打圆场、让方的也变圆，"你们也许能追上她、捎上她。"

"她会抄田间小路走，"约翰说。"碰不着她。"

约翰走回家去取捕狗夹子，路过墙角时抬头望了望，但她已经不在那儿了。艾登夫人把她叫进去了，该换衣裳了。

===Original===
"I don't want to go," said Amaryllis, "I hate fairs—they are so silly."

"But you must go," said Mrs. Iden. "Your grandfather sent a message last night; you know it's his dinner-day."

"He's such a horrid old fellow," said Amaryllis, "I can't bear him."

"How dare you speak of your grandfather like that? you are getting very rude and disrespectful."

There was no depending on Mrs. Iden. At one time she would go on and abuse Granfer Iden for an hour at a time, calling him every name she could think of, and accusing him of every folly under the sun. At another time she would solemnly inform Amaryllis that they had not a farthing of money, and how necessary it was that they should be attentive and civil to him.

===Chinese===
"我不想去，"阿玛丽莉斯说，"我讨厌赶集——太无聊了。"

"可你必须去，"艾登夫人说。"你祖父昨晚捎了话来；你知道今天是他请客的日子。"

"他是个讨厌的老头儿，"阿玛丽莉斯说，"我受不了他。"

"你竟敢这么说你祖父？你越来越没规矩、越来越没大没小了。"

艾登夫人这人靠不住。有时候她能连着骂上一个小时老格兰弗·艾登，把能想到的词儿都用上，数落他天底下的蠢事；有时候又一本正经地告诉阿玛丽莉斯，他们一个子儿也没有，对他们祖父必须殷勤、恭敬才行。

===Original===
Amaryllis very slowly put her hat on and the first jacket to hand.

"What! aren't you going to change your dress?"

"No, that I'm not."

"Change it directly."

"What, to go in and see that musty old—"

"Change it directly; I *will* be obeyed."

Amaryllis composedly did as she was bid.

One day Mrs. Iden humoured her every whim and let her do just as she pleased; the next she insisted on minute obedience.

"Make haste, you'll be late; now, then, put your things on—come."

So Amaryllis, much against her will, was bustled out of the house and started off. As John had foreseen, she soon quitted the road to follow the path across the fields, which was shorter.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯磨磨蹭蹭地戴上帽子，随手抓了件夹克。

"什么！你不换裙子？"

"不换。"

"马上换。"

"什么，去见那个发霉的老——"

"马上换；我*要*你听话。"

阿玛丽莉斯不动声色地照做了。

艾登夫人哪天由着她的性子来，什么都随她；第二天又要求她事事顺从。

"快点，要迟到了；好了，穿上你的衣服——来。"

于是阿玛丽莉斯极不情愿地被赶出了家门，上路了。正如约翰所料，她很快就离开了大路，抄田间小路走，那条路近一些。

===Original===
An hour or so later Iden came in from work as usual, a few minutes before dinner, and having drawn his quart of ale, sat down to sip it in the bow window till the dishes were brought.

"You're not gone, then?" said Mrs. Iden, irritatingly.

"Gone—wur?" said Iden, rather gruffly for him.

"To fair, of course—like other people."

"Hum," growled Iden.

"You know your father expects all the family to come in to dinner on fair day; I can't think how you can neglect him, when you know we haven't got a shilling—why don't you go in and speak to him?"

"You can go if you like."

"Me go!" cried Mrs. Iden. "Me go!" in shrill accents of contempt. "I don't care a button for all the lardy-cake lot! Let him keep his money. I'm as good as he is any day. My family go about, and do some business—"

"*Your* family," muttered Iden. "The Flammas!"

"Yes, *my* family—as good as yours, I should think! What's your family then, that you should be so grand? You're descended from a lardy-cake!"

"You be descended from a quart pot," said Iden.

===Chinese===
大约一小时后，艾登像往常一样收工回家，离晚饭还有几分钟，他打了一夸脱酒，在凸窗边坐下慢慢喝，等着上菜。

"你没去？"艾登夫人气人地说。

"去——啥？"艾登对他来说算是粗声粗气地回了一句。

"去赶集啊，当然——像人家那样。"

"哼，"艾登嘟囔了一声。

"你知道你爹逢集就盼着全家去吃晚饭；咱们一个子儿也没有，你怎么还能这么怠慢他——你怎么不去跟他说道说道？"

"你乐意去就去。"

"我去！"艾登夫人尖叫道。"我去！"声音里满是轻蔑。"我才不在乎那帮奶油糕老爷的人呢！让他留着他的钱好了。我哪天都不比他差。我家的人出去做生意，干的是正经事——"

"*你*家的人，"艾登嘀咕道。"弗拉马（Flammas）家！"

"对，*我*家的人——我看不比你家差！你家又算什么，就这么摆谱？你祖上是个做奶油糕的！"

"你祖上是个开酒馆的，"艾登说。

===Original===
This was an allusion to Mrs. Iden's grandfather, who had kept a small wayside public. There was no disgrace in it, for he was a very respectable man, and laid the foundation of his family's fortune, but it drove Mrs. Iden into frenzy.

"You talk about a quart pot—you," she shrieked. "Why, your family have drunk up thousands of pounds—you know they have. Where's the Manor? they swilled it away. Where's Upper Court? they got it down their throats. They built a house to drink in and nothing else. You know they did. You told me yourself. The most disgraceful set of drunkards that ever lived!"

"Your family don't drink, then, I suppose?" said Iden.

"Your lot's been drinking two hundred years—why, you're always talking about it."

"Your family be as nervous as cats—see their hands shake in the morning."

"They go to business in the City and do something; they don't mess about planting rubbishing potatoes." Mrs. Iden was London born.

"A pretty mess they've made of their business, as shaky as their hands. Fidgetty, miserable, nervous set they be."

===Chinese===
这话影射的是艾登夫人的祖父，他曾开过一家路边小酒馆。这没什么丢人的，因为他是个体面人，也为家里打下了家业的根基，但这话还是把艾登夫人气得发了疯。

"你还好意思提酒馆——*你*，"她尖叫道。"你家的人喝掉了成千上万的英镑——你知道的。庄园在哪儿？让他们灌下了肚子。上庄园在哪儿？让他们从嗓子眼儿里送进去了。他们盖了座房子，专门用来喝酒，没干别的。你知道的，你自己跟我说的。天底下最丢人的一帮酒鬼！"

"你家的人不喝酒，是吧？"艾登说。

"你家那帮人喝了两百年了——你自个儿一天到晚念叨呢。"

"你家的人早上手直哆嗦——跟猫似的。"

"人家在城里做生意，干正经事；不像你们，种些没用的土豆瞎折腾。"艾登夫人是伦敦生的。

"生意做得可真漂亮，跟他们那双手一样哆哆嗦嗦。一帮坐立不安、可悲、神经兮兮的家伙。"

===Original===
"They're not stocks and stones like yours, anyhow, as stolid, and slow, and stupid; why, you do nothing but sleep, sleep, sleep, and talk, talk, talk. You've been talking with the lazy lot over at the stile, and you've been talking with that old fool at the back door, and talking with Jack Duck—and that's your second mug! You're descended from a nasty, greasy lardy-cake! There!"

Iden snatched a piece of bread from the table and thrust it in one pocket, flung open the oven-door, and put a baked apple in the other pocket, and so marched out to eat what he could in quiet under a tree in the fields.

In the oratory of abuse there is no resource so successful as raking up the weaknesses of the opponent's family, especially when the parties are married, for having gossiped with each other for so long in the most confidential manner, they know every foible. How Robert drank, and Tom bet, and Sam swore, and Bill knocked his wife about, and Joseph did as Potiphar's spouse asked him, and why your uncle had to take refuge in Spain; and so on to an indefinite extent, like the multiplication table.

===Chinese===
"你们家人又不是木头石头，像你们家人那样迟钝、迟缓、蠢笨；你们一天到晚就睡觉、睡觉、睡觉，聊天、聊天、聊天。你在篱笆口跟那帮懒汉聊，你在后门口跟那个老傻瓜聊，你跟杰克·达克聊——这已经是你的第二杯了！你祖上是个恶心的、油腻腻的奶油糕！给你！"

艾登一把从桌上抓了块面包塞进一个口袋，猛地拉开烤箱门，抓了个烤苹果塞进另一个口袋，就这么走了出去，到田里的一棵树下安安静静地吃他的东西。

在骂人的学问里，没有什么比翻对手家里的老底更管用的了，尤其是结了婚的夫妻之间——他们彼此知根知底，什么隐私都聊过，每条软肋都一清二楚。罗伯特怎么喝酒，汤姆怎么赌钱，萨姆怎么骂人，比尔怎么揍老婆，约瑟夫怎么按波提乏（Potiphar）的老婆吩咐办事，你舅舅为什么不得不逃到西班牙——诸如此类，无穷无尽，跟乘法表似的。

## X

===Original===
When Mrs. Iden threw his lardy-cake descent in Iden's face she alluded to Grandfather Iden's being a baker and miller, and noted for the manufacture of these articles. A lardy, or larded, cake is a thing, I suppose, unknown to most of this generation; they were the principal confectionery familiar to country folk when Grandfather Iden was at the top of his business activity, seventy years since, in the Waterloo era.

===Chinese===
艾登太太把艾登的猪油蛋糕出身甩在他脸上时，说的就是艾登爷爷（Grandfather Iden）当面包师兼磨坊主、以制作猪油蛋糕闻名的事。猪油蛋糕，恐怕如今这一代人大半都没见过；那是七十年前、滑铁卢时代，艾登爷爷生意最红火时乡下人最熟悉的点心。

===Original===
A lardy-cake is an oblong, flat cake, crossed with lines, and rounded at the corners, made of dough, lard, sugar, and spice. Our ancestors liked something to gnaw at, and did not go in for lightness in their pastry; they liked something to stick to their teeth, and after that to their ribs. The lardy-cake eminently fulfilled these conditions; they put a trifle of sugar and spice in it, to set it going as it were, and the rest depended on the strength of the digestion. But if a ploughboy could get a new, warm lardy-cake, fresh from the oven, thought himself blessed.

===Chinese===
猪油蛋糕是一种长方扁平的糕点，表面划着交叉纹路，四角浑圆，用面团、猪油、糖和香料做成。我们的祖辈喜欢有嚼头的东西，点心不求轻软，只求能粘住牙齿，再沉到肚肠里去。猪油蛋糕完美地满足了这些条件；里头搁了一丁点儿糖和香料权作引子，余下的全凭消化的本事了。不过若一个犁田的小伙子能拿到一块刚出炉、热腾腾的猪油蛋糕，他便觉得自己有福了。

===Original===
Grandfather Iden had long since ceased any serious business, but he still made a few of these renowned cakes for his amusement, and sold a good few at times to the carters' lads who came in to market.

===Chinese===
艾登爷爷早已不再正经经营什么生意了，但偶尔兴起还会做几块这种远近闻名的猪油蛋糕来消遣，赶集时卖给进城的车夫小伙们，也卖出不少。

===Original===
Amaryllis knew the path perfectly, but if she had not, the tom-tomming of drums and blowing of brass, audible two miles away, would have guided her safely to the fair. The noise became prodigious as she approached—the ceaseless tom-tom, the beating of drums and gongs outside the show vans, the shouting of the showmen, the roar of a great crowd, the booing of cattle, the baaing of sheep, the neighing of horses—altogether the "rucket" was tremendous.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）对这条路再熟不过了，就算不熟，两英里外就能听到的鼓声咚咚和铜管号鸣也会把她安全引到集市上去。越走近，那声响便越发惊人——连绵不断的咚咚鼓声、演艺篷车外面锣鼓的敲击声、卖艺人的吆喝声、汹涌人群的喧嚣声、牛的哞叫声、羊的咩咩声、马的嘶鸣声——总而言之，这场"大热闹"着实惊天动地。

===Original===
She looked back from the hill close to the town and saw the people hurrying in from every quarter—there was a string of them following the path she had come, and others getting over distant stiles. A shower had fallen in the night, but the ceaseless wheels had ground up the dust again, and the lines of the various roads were distinctly marked by the clouds hanging above them. For one on business, fifty hastened on to join the uproar.

===Chinese===
她从镇子附近的小丘上回头望去，看见人们从四面八方匆匆赶来——身后小路上走着一长串跟她走同一条路的人，远处还有人在翻越篱笆门。夜里下过一阵雨，但来来往往的车轮又把尘土碾了起来，条条道路上方悬着的尘云清晰地标出了路的走向。去做正经事的一个人，就有五十个人急匆匆赶来凑这份热闹。

===Original===
Suppose the Venus de Medici had been fetched from Florence and had been set up in the town of Woolhorton, or the Laocoön from Rome, or the Milo from Paris, do you think all these people would have scurried in such haste to admire these beautiful works? Nothing of the sort; if you want a crowd you must make a row. It is really wonderful how people do thoroughly and unaffectededly enjoy a fearful disturbance; if the cannon could be shot off quietly, and guns made no noise, battles would not be half so popular to read about. The silent arrow is uninteresting, and if you describe a medieval scramble you must put in plenty of splintering lances, resounding armour, shrieks and groans, and so render it lively.

===Chinese===
倘若有人把佛罗伦萨的维纳斯雕像搬来安放在伍尔霍顿（Woolhorton），或者把罗马的拉奥孔群像、巴黎的断臂维纳斯运来，你想这些人会如此急匆匆赶来欣赏这些绝世美作吗？绝不会；要想聚起人山人海，非得闹出大动静不可。人们真心实意、毫不做作地享受一场可怕的骚动，这实在令人惊叹；假如大炮能悄无声息地发射，枪支不发出声响，战争题材的读物绝不会受欢迎一半。无声的箭乏善可陈，要描写中世纪的混战，你非得写上折断的长矛、铿锵的铠甲、尖叫声和呻吟声不可，这样才热闹。

===Original===
"This is the patent age of new inventions," and someone might make a profit by starting a fête announcing that a drum or a gong would be provided for every individual, to be beaten in a grand universal chorus.

===Chinese===
"这是发明专利的新时代"，说不定有人搞一场庆典来赚上一笔——宣告为每位来宾提供一面鼓或一面锣，大家齐鸣，来一场盛大的全民合奏。

===Original===
Amaryllis had no little difficulty in getting through the crowd till she found her way behind the booths and slipped along the narrow passage between them and the houses. There was an arched entrance, archaeologically interesting, by which she paused a moment, half inclined to go up and inquire for her boots. The shoemaker who lived there had had them since Christmas, and all that wanted doing was a patch on one toe; they were always just going to be done, but never finished. She read the inscription over his door, "Tiras Wise, Shoemaker; Established 1697." A different sort of shoe-maker to your lively Northampton awls; a man who has been in business two hundred years cannot be hurried. She sighed, and passed on.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯费了好大的劲才穿过人群，找到摊棚后面的通道，沿着摊棚与房屋之间的窄巷溜过去。巷口有一道拱门，颇有考古趣味，她停下来犹豫了片刻，差点想上去问问她的靴子修好了没有。住在那里的鞋匠从圣诞节起就收了她的靴子，一只靴头只需打个补丁；靴子总是"快修好了"，却永远修不完。她读了读门楣上的题字："蒂拉斯·怀斯（Tiras Wise），鞋匠；创立于1697年。"这号鞋匠可跟你在热闹的北安普顿见过的大不一样；一个做了两百年生意的人，你是催不动他的。她叹了口气，继续往前走。

===Original===
The step to Grandfather Iden's door consisted of one wide stone of semicircular shape, in which the feet of three generations of customers had worn a deep groove. The venerable old gentleman, for he was over ninety, was leaning on the hatch (or lower half of the door), in the act of handing some of his cakes to two village girls who had called for them. These innocent, hamlet girls, supposed to be so rurally simple, had just been telling him how they never forgot his nice cakes, but always came every fair day to buy some. For this they got sixpence each, it being well known that the old gentleman was so delighted with anybody who bought his cakes he generally gave them back their money, and a few coppers besides.

===Chinese===
艾登爷爷家门口的台阶是一块半圆形的大石头，三代顾客的脚已磨出了一道深深的凹槽。这位德高望重的老先生——他已年逾九旬——正倚在半截门板上，把几块蛋糕递给两个上门来买的村姑。这两个天真淳朴的乡村姑娘，看上去老实巴交的，方才一直在告诉他她们从没忘记他那好吃的蛋糕，每次逢集都会来买几块。为此每人得了六便士——大家都知道，这位老先生但凡有人来买他的蛋糕，高兴之余总要把钱还给人家，还外搭几个铜板。

===Original===
He took Amaryllis by the arm as she stood on the step and pulled her into the shop, asked her if her father were coming, then walked her down by the oven-door, and made her stand up by a silver-mounted peel, to see how tall she was. The peel is the long wooden rod, broad at one end, with which loaves are placed in the baker's oven. Father Iden being proud of his trade, in his old age had his favourite peel ornamented with silver.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯站在台阶上时，他一把拉住她的胳膊把她拽进店里，问她父亲来不来，然后领她走到烤炉门旁边，让她靠在一把镶银的铲子旁，量量她长了多高。烤铲是一种长长的木棒，一头扁宽，用来把面包送进炉膛。艾登老爹（Father Iden）以自己的手艺为傲，年老后专门给他最爱的那把烤铲镶上了银饰。

===Original===
"Too fast—too fast," he said, shaking his head, and coughing; "you grow too fast; there's the notch I cut last year, and now you're two inches taller." He lowered the peel, and showed her where his thumb was—quite two inches higher than the last year's mark.

===Chinese===
"太快了——太快了，"他摇着头，咳嗽着说；"你长得太快了；你看这是去年我刻的记号，如今你又高了两英寸。"他放下烤铲，指给她看他的大拇指位置——比去年的刻痕足足高了两英寸。

===Original===
"I want to be tall," said Amaryllis.

===Chinese===
"我就想长得高，"阿玛丽莉斯说。

===Original===
"I daresay—I daresay," said the old man, in the haste manner of feeble age, as he cut another notch to record her height. The handle of the peel was notched all round, where he had measured his grandchildren; there were so many marks it was not easy to see how he distinguished them.

===Chinese===
"那是——那是，"老人以老年人特有的急促语调应道，一边刻下新的记号来记录她的身高。烤铲的柄上四周刻满了记号，都是他量孙子孙女身高时留下的；刻痕多得密密麻麻，真不知他是怎么分辨的。

===Original===
"Is your father coming?" he asked, when he had finished with the knife.

===Chinese===
"你父亲来不来？"他放下刀子后问道。

===Original===
"I don't know." This was Jesuitically true—she did *know*—she could not be certain; but in her heart she was sure he would not come. But she did not want to hear any hard words said about him.

===Chinese===
"我不知道。"这话在耶稣会式的层面上是实话——她确实*不*知道——她不能确定；但心里明白他不会来。只是她不想听到别人说他的坏话。

===Original===
"Has he sent anything? Have you brought anything for me? No. No. Hum!—ha!"—fit of coughing—"Well, well—come in; dinner's late, there's time to hear you read—you're fond of books, you read a great deal at home,"—and so talking, half to himself and half to her, he led the way into the parlour by the shop.

===Chinese===
"他捎了什么没有？你给我带了什么没有？没有。没有。哼！——哈！"——一阵咳嗽——"行了行了——进来吧；晚饭还早，有空听你读书——你爱看书，在家读了不少，"他这样半自言自语半对她说着，领她穿过店铺走进了里屋。

===Original===
Bowed by more than ninety years, his back curved over forwards, and his limbs curved in the opposite direction, so that the outline of his form resembled a flattened capital S. For his chin hung over his chest, and his knees never straightened themselves, but were always more or less bent as he stood or walked. It was much the attitude of a strong man heavily laden and unable to stand upright—such an attitude as big Jack Duck in his great strength might take when carrying two sacks of wheat at once. There was as heavy a load on Grandfather Iden's back, but Time is invisible.

===Chinese===
九十余载的岁月压弯了他的脊背，他的背向前弓起，四肢却向相反方向弯曲，整个身形的轮廓像一个被压扁了的大写S。他的下巴垂在胸口上方，膝盖从来伸不直，无论站着还是走路都多少弯着。这很像一个壮汉负重过沉、直不起腰的样子——大力士大杰克·达克（Jack Duck）扛着两袋麦子时大概也是这副模样。艾登爷爷背上也负着同样沉重的担子，只不过时间是看不见的。

===Original===
He wore a grey suit, as a true miller and baker should, and had worn the same cut and colour for years and years. In the shop, too, he always had a grey hat on, perhaps its original hue was white, but it got to appear grey upon him; a large grey chimney-pot, many sizes too big for his head apparently, for it looked as if forever about to descend and put out his face like an extinguisher. Though his boots were so carefully polished, they quickly took a grey tint from the flour dust as he pottered about the bins in the morning. The ends of his trousers, too long for his antique shanks, folded and creased over his boots, and almost hid his grey cloth under-gaiters.

===Chinese===
他穿着一身灰色套装，正像一个真正的磨坊主兼面包师该穿的样子，而且多年来一直是同样的剪裁和颜色。在店里他也总是戴着一顶灰帽子——或许原本是白色的，但戴在他头上就显得灰扑扑的——一顶灰色的大烟囱帽，看起来比他的脑袋大了好几号，仿佛随时要滑下来把他的脸罩住，像熄灯罩一样。尽管他的靴子擦得一丝不苟，可早上在面粉箱之间走来走去，很快就沾上了一层灰蒙蒙的面粉。他的裤腿对那双古旧的瘦腿来说太长了，折着叠在靴子上面，几乎把灰色的布绑腿都遮住了。

===Original===
A great knobbed old nose—but stay, I will not go further, it is not right to paint too faithfully the features of the very aged, which are repellent in spite of themselves; I mean, they cannot help their faces, their sentiments and actions are another matter; therefore I will leave Father Iden's face as a dim blot on the mirror; you look in it and it reflects everywhere, except one spot.

===Chinese===
一只布满疙瘩的大鼻子——不过且住，我不再往下描摹了，过于逼真地刻画极高龄者的面容并不恰当，它们不由自主地令人望而生畏；我是说，他们对自己的面容无能为力，他们的性情和作为则是另一回事；所以，就让艾登老爹的脸像镜面上一块模糊的斑点吧——你往镜子里看，它到处都能照见，唯独那块斑点照不出来。

===Original===
Amaryllis followed him jauntily—little did she care, reckless girl, for the twenty thousand guineas in the iron box under his bed.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯轻快地跟在他身后——这个率性的姑娘才不在乎他床底下铁盒子里那两万基尼呢。

===Original===
The cottage folk, who always know so much, had endless tales of Iden's wealth; how years ago bushels upon bushels of pennies, done up in five-shilling packets, had been literally carted like potatoes away from the bakehouse to go to London; how ponies were laden with sacks of silver groats, all paid over that furrowed counter for the golden flour, dust more golden than the sands of ancient Pactolus.

===Chinese===
村里人向来消息灵通，关于艾登家的财富有说不完的旧闻轶事：多少年前，一蒲式耳一蒲式耳的便士，按五先令一扎包好，像运土豆一样从面包房里一车车拉走运往伦敦；小马驮着一袋袋银格罗特，在那布满沟痕的柜台上换取金色的面粉——那面粉的金色比古帕克托洛斯河（Pactolus）的金沙还要灿烂。

===Original===
Reckless Amaryllis cared not a pin for all the spade guineas in the iron box.

===Chinese===
无所顾忌的阿玛丽莉斯对铁盒子里那些铲形基尼一个子儿也不稀罕。

===Original===
The old man sat down by the fire without removing his hat, motioning to her to shut the door, which she was loth to do, for the little room was smothered with smoke. Troubled with asthma, he coughed incessantly, and mopped his mouth with a vast silk handkerchief, but his dull blood craved for warmth, and he got his knees close to the grate, and piled up the coal till it smoked and smoked, and filled the close apartment with a suffocating haze of carbon. To be asked into Father Iden's sanctuary was an honour, but, like other honards, it had to be paid for.

===Chinese===
老人在炉边坐下，帽子也不摘，示意她把门关上。她不太情愿关门，因为这间小屋烟雾弥漫、令人窒息。他患有哮喘，不停地咳嗽，用一块巨大的丝帕抹着嘴，但迟钝的血液渴望温暖，他把膝盖凑近炉篦，不停地添煤，直到浓烟滚滚，整间密不透风的小屋被呛人的碳雾填满。被请进艾登老爹的内室是一种荣耀，但如同其他荣耀一样，是要付出代价的。

===Original===
Amaryllis gasped as she sat down, and tried to breathe as short as possible, to avoid inhaling more than she could bear.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯坐下来时被烟呛得直喘，尽量浅浅地呼吸，以免吸进超出她承受能力的烟气。

===Original===
"Books," said her grandfather, pointing to the bookcases, which occupied three sides of the room. "Books—you like books; look at them—go and see."

===Chinese===
"书，"她祖父指着占了三面墙的书架说。"书——你喜欢书；看看它们——去看看吧。"

===Original===
