To humour him, Amaryllis rose, and appeared to look carefully along the shelves which she had scanned so many times before. They contained very good books indeed, such books as were not to be found elsewhere throughout the whole town of Woolhorton, and perhaps hardly in the county, old and rare volumes of price, such as Sotheby, Wilkinson, and Co. delight to offer to collectors, such as Bernard Quaritch, that giant of the modern auction room, would have written magnificent cheques for.

===Chinese===
为了顺着他，阿玛丽莉斯站起身来，装作仔细打量那些她已看过无数遍的书架。里面确实是些好书，整个伍尔霍顿镇上别处找不到，也许整个郡也难得一见——那些珍贵的古版稀本，正是苏富比-威尔金森公司（Sotheby, Wilkinson, and Co.）乐于卖给收藏家的那种，正是伯纳德·夸里奇（Bernard Quaritch）——现代拍卖场上的巨人——会开出巨额支票的那种。

===Original===
Did you ever see the Giant Quaritch in the auction-room bidding for books? It is one of the sights of London, let me tell you, to anyone who thinks or is alive to the present day. Most sights are reputations merely—the pale reflection of things that were real once. This sight is something of the living time, the day in which we live. Get an *Athenaeum* in the season, examine the advertisements of book auctions, and attend the next great sale of some famous library.

===Chinese===
你见过巨人夸里奇在拍卖场上竞拍图书吗？我告诉你，这对任何有思想、对当下时代有所感知的人来说，都是伦敦的一景。大多数景点不过是虚名——曾经真实存在过的事物的苍白映像。这一景却是活生生的当下、我们身处的这个时代的产物。在拍卖季买一份《雅典娜神殿》杂志（*Athenaeum*），查查书刊拍卖广告，去参加下一场著名藏书的大拍卖吧。

===Original===
You have a recollection of the giant who sat by the highway and devoured the pilgrims who passed? This giant sits in the middle of the ring and devours the books set loose upon their travels after the repose of centuries.

===Chinese===
你可还记得那个坐在大路旁吞食过路朝圣者的巨人？这位巨人坐在拍卖圈的正中央，把那些沉睡了数百年后重获自由、踏上旅程的书籍一一吞入腹中。

===Original===
What prices to give! No one can withstand him. From Paris they send agents with a million francs at their back; from Berlin and Vienna come the eager snappers-up of much considered trifles, but in vain. They only get what the Giant chooses to leave them.

===Chinese===
开什么价都行！没有人能抵挡他。巴黎方面派出背后有百万法郎撑腰的代理人；柏林和维也纳来了那些对琐碎之物趋之若鹜的急切买家，但都是徒劳。他们只能得到巨人挑剩下给他们的东西。

===Original===
Books that nobody ever heard of fetch £50, £60, £100, £200; wretched little books never opened since they were printed; dull duodecimos on the course of the river Wein; nondescript indescribable twaddling local books in Italian, Spanish, queer French, written and printed in some unknown foreign village; read them—you might as well try to amuse yourself with a Chinese pamphlet! What earthly value they are of cannot be discovered. They were composed by authors whose names are gone like the sand washed by the Nile into the sea before Herodotus. They contain no beautiful poetry, no elevated thought, no scientific discovery; they are simply so much paper, printing, and binding, so many years old, and it is for that age, printing, and binding that the money is paid.

===Chinese===
没人听说过的书能卖五十、六十、一百、两百英镑；那些自印刷以来从未被人翻开过的可怜巴巴的小书；关于魏恩河（Wein）水道的枯燥十二开本；用意大利文、西班牙文、古怪法文书写的、在某个无名外国村庄里出版印刷的、不知所云的本地杂书——读它们？你还不如去找一本中文小册子来消遣！它们到底有什么价值，实在无从得知。它们的作者早已被世人遗忘，如同希罗多德之前就被尼罗河冲入大海的泥沙一般。它们不含优美的诗歌、没有崇高的思想、没有科学发现；它们不过是纸张、印刷和装订的产物，存在了那么多年，人们为之付钱，买的就是那份年份、印刷和装订。

===Original===
I have read a good many books in my time—I would not give sixpence for the whole lot.

===Chinese===
我这辈子也读了不少书——但这堆东西，我连六便士也不愿出。

===Original===
They are not like a block-book—first efforts at printing; nor like the first editions of great authors; there is not the slightest intrinsic value in them whatever.

===Chinese===
它们既不像木刻版书——印刷术最初的努力；也不像伟大作家的初版书；它们本身没有丝毫内在价值。

===Original===
Yet some of them fetch prices which not long ago were thought tremendous even for the Shakespeare folio.

===Chinese===
然而其中有些卖出了不久前人们认为连莎士比亚对开本都不值的天价。

===Original===
Hundreds and hundreds of pounds are paid for them. Living and writing authors of the present day are paid in old songs by comparison.

===Chinese===
几百几百英镑地成交。相比之下，当今在世的、还在写作的作家们拿到的稿费简直不值一提。

===Original===
Still, this enormous value set on old books is one of the remarkable signs of the day. If anyone wishes to know what Today is, these book-auctions are of the things he should go to see.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，对古旧书籍赋予如此巨大的价值，仍是当今时代最引人注目的征兆之一。若有人想知道今日是何面目，这些书刊拍卖会便是他应当去看的地方。

===Original===
Such books as these lined Grandfather Iden's shelves; among them there were a few that I call *real* old books, an early translation or two, an early Shakespeare, and once there had been a very valuable Boccaccio, but this had gone into Lord Pamment's library, "Presented by James Bartholomew Iden, Esq."

===Chinese===
艾登爷爷的书架上摆的就是这类书；其中有几本我称之为*真正*的古书——一两部早期译本、一部早期莎士比亚，还曾有一本非常珍贵的薄伽丘（Boccaccio），不过这本已经进了帕门特勋爵（Lord Pamment）的藏书室，题词写着"詹姆斯·巴塞洛缪·艾登先生（James Bartholomew Iden, Esq.）敬赠"。

===Original===
The old man often went to look at and admire his Boccaccio in my Lord's library.

===Chinese===
老人常去勋爵的藏书室里观赏他那本薄伽丘。

## XI

===Original===
There was one peculiarity in all the books on Grandfather Iden’s shelves, they were all very finely bound in the best style of hand-art, and they all bore somewhere or other a little design of an ancient Roman lamp.

Hand-art is a term I have invented for the workmanship of good taste﻿—it is not the sculptor’s art, nor the painter’s﻿—not the art of the mind, but the art of the hand. Some furniture and cabinet work, for instance, some pottery, bookbinding like this, are the products of hand-art.

"Do you see the Lamp?" asked the old man, when Amaryllis had stared sufficiently at the backs of the books.

"Yes, I can see the Lamp."

"House of Flamma," said old Iden.

===Chinese===
艾登爷爷（Grandfather Iden）书架上所有的书都有一个共同的奇特之处：它们全都是以最好的手工艺术风格精心装帧的，而且每一本都印着一盏古罗马油灯的小图案。

所谓"手工艺术"，是我自创的一个词，用来形容那种品味精良的手工制品——它不是雕刻家的艺术，也不是画家的艺术——不是心灵的艺术，而是双手的艺术。某些家具和细木工制品，比如一些陶器，还有像这样的书籍装帧，都是手工艺术的产物。

"你看到那盏灯了吗？"老人问道，此时阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）已经把那些书的书脊端详够了。

"嗯，我看到那盏灯了。"

"弗拉玛家族（House of Flamma），"老艾登（Iden）说道。

===Original===
"House of Flamma," repeated Amaryllis, hastily, eager to show that she understood all about it. She feared lest he should enter into the history of the House of Flamma and of his connection with it; she had heard it all over and over again; her mother was a Flamma; she had herself some of the restless Flamma blood in her. When anything annoyed her or made her indignant her foot used to tap the floor, and her neck flush rosy, and her face grow dusky like the night. Then, striving to control herself, she would say to herself, "I *will* not be a Flamma."

Except her dear mother and one other, Amaryllis detested and despised the whole tribe of the Flammas, the nervous, excitable, passionate, fidgetty, tipsy, idle, good-for-nothing lot; she hated them all, the very name and mention of them; she sided with her father as an Iden against her mother’s family, the Flammas. True they were almost all flecked with talent like white foam on a black horse, a spot or two of genius, and the rest black guilt or folly. She hated them; she would not be a Flamma.

How should she at sixteen understand the wear and tear of life, the pressure of circumstances, the heavy weight of difficulties﻿—there was something to be said even for the miserable fidgetty Flammas, but naturally sixteen judged by appearances. Shut up in narrow grooves and working day after day, year after year, in a contracted way, by degrees their constitutional nervousness became the chief characteristic of their existence. It was Intellect overcome﻿—overburdened﻿—with two generations of petty cares; Genius dulled and damped till it went to the quart pot.

Sixteen could scarcely understand this. Amaryllis detested the very name; she would not be a Flamma.

But she was a Flamma for all that; a Flamma in fire of spirit, in strength of indignation, in natural capacity; she drew, for instance, with the greatest ease in pencil or pen-and-ink, drew to the life; she could write a letter in sketches.

===Chinese===
"弗拉玛家族，"阿玛丽莉斯急匆匆地重复道，生怕显得自己不懂。她唯恐老人要讲起弗拉玛家族的来龙去脉以及他与这个家族的渊源——那些故事她已经听过无数遍了；她的母亲就是弗拉玛家的人；她自己血管里也流淌着弗拉玛家族不安分的血液。每当遇到恼人或令她愤慨的事，她的脚就会不自觉地叩击地面，脖颈涨得绯红，面容暗沉如夜。然后她竭力克制自己，暗暗对自己说："我*决不*做弗拉玛家的人。"

除了亲爱的母亲和另一个人之外，阿玛丽莉斯对整个弗拉玛家族深恶痛绝、嗤之以鼻——那一大家子神经质、爱激动、脾气暴躁、坐立不安、嗜酒贪杯、游手好闲、一无是处的人；她恨他们每一个人，连这个姓氏、连别人提起这个名字都让她生厌；她站在父亲一边，以艾登家人的身份对抗母亲的娘家——弗拉玛家。诚然，他们几乎个个都像黑马身上的白斑一样点缀着几分才气，有那么一两处天才的闪光，但其余尽是黑色的罪过或愚蠢。她恨他们；她绝不做弗拉玛家的人。

十六岁的她怎能理解生活的磨损、环境的压迫、困苦的重压——就连那些可怜兮兮、坐立不安的弗拉玛家人也有值得同情之处，但十六岁的人自然只看表面。他们被困在逼仄的轨道里，日复一日、年复一年地以拘束的方式劳作，天生的神经质渐渐成了他们生存的主要特征。那是才智被击败——被压垮——被两代人的琐碎忧虑压垮；天分被磨钝、被浇灭，直到沦落到酒杯里去。

十六岁的人很难理解这些。阿玛丽莉斯厌恶这个姓氏；她绝不做弗拉玛家的人。

然而她骨子里就是一个弗拉玛——在精神的烈火中、在愤慨的力量中、在天赋的禀赋中都是弗拉玛；比如她画铅笔画和钢笔画都轻而易举，画什么像什么；她能用速写来写信。

===Original===
Her indignation sometimes at the wrongfulness of certain things seemed to fill her with a consuming fire. Her partisanship for her father made her sometimes inwardly rage for the lightning, that she might utterly erase the opposer. Her contempt of sycophancy, and bold independence led her constantly into trouble.

Flamma means a flame.

Yet she was gentleness itself too; see her at the bookshelves patiently endeavouring to please the tiresome old man.

"Open that drawer," said he, as she came to it.

Amaryllis did so, and said that the coins and medals in it were very interesting, as they really were. The smoke caught her in the throat, and seemed to stop the air as she breathed from reaching her chest. So much accustomed to the open air, she felt stifled.

===Chinese===
她有时因某些不公之事而生的义愤，似乎在她体内燃起了一团吞噬一切的烈火。她对父亲的袒护有时令她内心如雷电般狂怒，恨不得将反对者一举抹去。她对谄媚的蔑视和大胆的独立精神不断给她招惹麻烦。

Flamma的意思是"火焰"。

然而她同时也是温柔的化身；且看她在书架前耐心地努力取悦那个烦人的老头。

"打开那个抽屉，"她走近时他吩咐道。

阿玛丽莉斯照做了，还说里面的硬币和勋章非常有趣，确实如此。烟雾呛住了她的喉咙，呼吸时空气似乎到不了胸腔就被堵住了。她习惯了户外的空气，此刻只觉得窒息。

===Original===
Then he asked her to read to him aloud, that he might hear how she enunciated her words. The book he gave her was an early copy of Addison, the page a pale yellow, the type old-fount, the edges rough, but where in a trim modern volume will you find language like his and ideas set forth with such transparent lucidity? How easy to write like that!﻿—so simple, merely a letter to an intimate friend; but try!

Trim modern volumes are so very hard to read, especially those that come to us from New York, thick volumes of several hundred pages, printed on the thinnest paper in hard, unpleasant type. You cannot read them; you *work* through them.

The French have retained a little of the old style of book in their paper bound franc novels, the rough paper, thick black type, rough edges are pleasant to touch and look at﻿—they feel as if they were done by hand, not turned out hurriedly smooth and trim by machinery.

Docile to the last degree with him, Amaryllis tried her utmost to read well, and she succeeded, so far as the choking smoke would let her. By grunting between his continuous fits of coughing the old man signified his approval.

===Chinese===
接着老人让她朗读给他听，好听听她吐字是否清楚。他递给她的书是一册艾迪生（Addison）的早期版本，书页泛着淡黄，字体是老式铅字，书边毛糙，但要在一本装帧齐整的现代书中，你到哪里去找像他那样的文笔，以及如此通透明晰地阐述思想的文字？写成那样看起来多容易啊！——多么简单，不过是一封写给密友的信罢了；但你试试看！

现代的齐整书本实在很难读，尤其是从纽约运来的那种，厚厚几百页，印在最薄的纸上，用的是生硬、令人不适的铅字。你没法"读"它们，你是在"啃"它们。

法国人倒还保留了一点旧式书籍的风格，那些一法郎一本的纸面小说，粗纸、粗黑的字体、毛糙的书边，摸着看着都令人愉悦——它们感觉像是手工做出来的，而不是匆忙地被机器打磨得光滑齐整。

阿玛丽莉斯对他顺从到了极点，拼尽全力想读好，就呛人的烟雾所允许的程度而言，她读得相当好。老人在一阵阵咳嗽的间隙咕哝了几声，表示赞许。

===Original===
Amaryllis would have been respectful to any of the aged, but she had a motive here; she wanted to please him for her father’s sake. For many years there had been an increasing estrangement between the younger and the elder Iden; an estrangement which no one could have explained, for it could hardly be due to money matters if Grandfather Iden was really so rich. The son was his father’s tenant﻿—the farm belonged to Grandfather Iden﻿—and perhaps the rent was not paid regularly. Still that could not have much mattered﻿—a mere trifle to a man of old Iden’s wealth. There was something behind, no one knew what; possibly they scarcely knew themselves, for it is a fact that people frequently fall into a quarrel without remembering the beginning.

Amaryllis was very anxious to please the old man for her father’s sake; her dear father, whom she loved so much. Tradesmen were forever worrying him for petty sums of money; it made her furious with indignation to see and hear it.

So she read her very best, and swallowed the choking smoke patiently.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯对任何老人都会恭敬有加，但此番她别有用心；她想为了父亲的缘故取悦他。多年来，小艾登和老艾登之间的隔阂日益加深；这种隔阂无人能解释，因为如果艾登爷爷真的那么有钱，就不太可能是因为钱的事。儿子是父亲的佃户——农场属于艾登爷爷——也许房租没有按时交纳。但那也不至于有什么大不了——对老艾登这样富有的人来说不过是一桩小事。背后另有隐情，没人知道是什么；可能他们自己也说不清，因为事实上人们常常吵着吵着就忘了起因。

阿玛丽莉斯为了父亲，非常渴望讨好这位老人；她深爱着亲爱的父亲。商贩们整天为几个小钱来纠缠他；看到和听到这些，她就气得怒火中烧。

于是她拿出最好的状态朗读，耐心地吞咽着呛人的烟雾。

===Original===
Among the yellow pages, pressed flat, and still as fresh as if gathered yesterday, Amaryllis found bright petals and coloured autumn leaves. For it was one of the old man’s ways to carry home such of these that pleased him and to place them in his books. This he had done for half a century, and many of the flower petals and leaves in the grey old works of bygone authors had been there a generation. It is wonderful how long they will endure left undisturbed and pressed in this way; the paper they used in old books seems to have been softer, without the hard surface of our present paper, more like blotting paper, and so keeps them better. Before the repulsion between father and son became so marked, Amaryllis had often been with her grandfather in the garden and round the meadows at Coombe Oaks, and seen him gather the yellow tulips, the broad-petalled roses, and in autumn the bright scarlet bramble leaves. The brown leaves of the Spanish chestnut, too, pleased him; anything with richness of colour. The old and grey, and withered man gathered the brightest of petals for his old and grey, and forgotten books.

===Chinese===
在那些压平的泛黄书页之间，花瓣和彩叶仍然鲜艳如新，仿佛昨天才采来一般。这是老人的一种习惯：把喜欢的花草带回家，夹进书里。他这样做了半个世纪，那些古老灰色旧书中夹着的许多花瓣和叶片已经在那里躺了一代人了。它们竟然能在无人打扰的情况下保存这么久，真令人惊叹；旧书用的纸似乎比现在的纸更柔软，没有我们如今那种硬挺的纸面，更像吸墨纸，所以更能保存好这些花草。在父子之间产生明显的嫌隙之前，阿玛丽莉斯常跟着祖父在库姆橡树（Coombe Oaks）的花园和草地上转悠，看着他采集黄色的郁金香、宽瓣的玫瑰，到了秋天就采鲜红的荆棘叶。西班牙栗树的褐色叶子也让他喜欢；凡是色彩浓郁的东西他都爱。这个苍老枯萎的老人，为他那些苍老褪色、被人遗忘的书本采来了最鲜艳的花瓣。

===Original===
Now the sight of these leaves and petals between the yellow pages softened her heart towards him; he was a tyrant, but he was very, very old, they were like flowers on a living tomb.

In a little while Grandfather Iden got up, and going to a drawer in one of the bookcases, took from it some scraps of memoranda; he thrust these between her face and the book, and told her to read them instead.

"These are your writing."

"Go on," said the old man, smiling, grunting, and coughing, all at once.

"In 1840," read Amaryllis, "there were only two houses in Black Jack Street." "Only *two* houses!" she interposed, artfully.

"Two," said the grandfather.

"One in 1802," went on Amaryllis, "while in 1775 the site was covered with furze." "How it has changed!" she said. He nodded, and coughed, and smiled; his great grey hat rocked on his head and seemed about to extinguish him.

"There’s a note at the bottom in pencil, grandpa. It says, ‘A hundred voters in this street, 1884.’ "

"Ah!" said the old man, an ah! so deep it fetched his very heart up in coughing. When he finished, Amaryllis read on﻿

===Chinese===
此刻看到泛黄书页间夹着的这些叶片和花瓣，她的心对老人柔软下来；他是个暴君，但他已经非常非常老了，它们就像一座活坟墓上长出的花朵。

过了一会儿，艾登爷爷站起来，走到一个书柜的抽屉前，从里面取出几片备忘的纸条；他把这些纸条塞到她脸和书之间，叫她改读这些。

"这是您写的字。"

"念下去，"老人微笑着、咕哝着、咳嗽着，三种声音同时发出。

"一八四零年，"阿玛丽莉斯读道，"黑杰克街上只有两栋房子。""只有*两栋*房子！"她巧妙地插了一句。

"两栋，"祖父说。

"一八零二年，"阿玛丽莉斯接着读，"而一七七五年，这片地方还长满了荆豆。""变化真大啊！"她说。老人点了点头，又咳嗽，又微笑；他那顶灰色大帽子在头上摇摇晃晃，似乎要把他整个人盖住。

"爷爷，底下有一行铅笔小字。上面写着：'一八八四年，此街选民一百人。'"

"啊！"老人叫了一声，那声"啊"如此深沉，把他的心都咳了出来。等他咳完了，阿玛丽莉斯继续读下去——

===Original===
"In 1802 there were only ten voters in the town."

"Ah!" His excitement caused such violent coughing Amaryllis became alarmed, but it did him no harm. The more he coughed and choked the livelier he seemed. The thought of politics roused him like a trumpet﻿—it went straight to his ancient heart.

"Read that again," he said. "How many voters now?"

"A hundred voters in this street, 1884."

"We’ve got them all"﻿—coughing﻿—"all in my lord’s houses, everyone; vote Conservative, one and all. What is it?" as someone knocked. Dinner was ready, to Amaryllis’s relief.

"Perhaps you would like to dine with me?" asked the grandfather, shuffling up his papers. "There﻿—there," as she hesitated, "you would like to dine with young people, of course﻿—of course."

===Chinese===
"一八零二年，镇上只有十个选民。"

"啊！"他的激动引发了剧烈的咳嗽，阿玛丽莉斯吓了一跳，但对他并无大碍。他咳得越厉害、呛得越凶，反而越显得精神。政治的话题就像军号一样唤醒了他——它直抵他那颗苍老的心。

"再读一遍，"他说，"现在有多少选民？"

"一八八四年，此街选民一百人。"

"我们全拿到了"——咳嗽着——"全在我老爷的房子里，每一个都是；全是保守党，无一例外。什么事？"有人敲门了。晚饭准备好了，阿玛丽莉斯松了一口气。

"也许你愿意和我一起吃晚饭？"祖父一边把文件拢到一起，一边问道。"来吧——来吧，"见她犹豫，"你当然愿意和年轻人一起吃——当然了。"

## XII

===Original===
Old Grandfather Iden always dined alone in the parlour, with his housekeeper to wait on him; they were just bringing in his food. The family and visitors had their meals in a separate and much more comfortable apartment in another part of the house, which was large. Sometimes, as a great favour and special mark of approval, the old Pacha would invite you to eat with him.

Amaryllis, though anxious to please him, hesitated, not only because of the smoke, but because she knew he always had pork for dinner.

The rich juices of roast pork sustained his dry and withered frame—it was a sort of Burgundy of flesh to him. As the good wine of Burgundy fills the blood with iron and strengthens the body, so the rich juice of the pork seemed to supply the oil necessary to keep the sinews supple and to prevent the cartilages from stiffening.

The scientific people say that it is the ossification of the cartilages—the stiffening of the firmer tissues—that in time interferes with the processes of life. The hinges rust, as if your tricycle had been left out in the rain for a week—and the delicate watchwork of the human frame will not run.

===Chinese===
老格兰弗·艾登总是一个人在客厅里吃饭，由管家伺候着；他们正往里端他的饭菜。家里人和客人都在宅子另一头一间单独的、舒适得多的屋子里吃饭，那屋子很大。有时候，作为莫大的恩宠和特别的赏识，这位老帕夏（Pacha）会邀请你跟他一道吃。

阿玛丽莉斯虽然很想讨好他，却迟疑不决，不光是因为烟熏火燎，还因为她知道他晚饭总吃猪肉。

烤猪肉丰腴的肉汁维持着他那干枯衰朽的身躯——对他就像是肉做的勃艮第酒。正如上好的勃艮第酒能把铁质注入血液、强身健体，猪肉丰厚的汁液似乎也能提供必要的油脂，使筋腱柔韧、防止软骨硬化。

科学界人士说，正是软骨的骨化——较硬组织的僵化——会逐渐妨碍生命机能的运转。铰链生锈了，就像你的三轮车在雨地里搁了一个星期——人体那精巧的钟表装置就走不动了。

===Original===
If suppleness could only be maintained there is no reason why it should not continue to work for a much longer period, for a hundred and fifty, two hundred years—as long as you fancy. But nothing has yet been devised to keep up the suppleness.

Grandfather Iden found the elixir of life in roast pork. The jokers of Woolhorton—there are always jokers, very clever they think themselves—considered the reason it suited him so well was because of the pig-like obstinacy of his disposition.

Anything more contrary to common sense than for an old man of ninety to feed on pork it would be hard to discover—so his friends said.

===Chinese===
只要能保持柔韧性，没有理由不让他继续运转更久——一百五十年、两百年，随你想象。但至今还没有发明出保持柔韧性的灵丹妙药。

老格兰弗·艾登在烤猪肉里找到了长生不老药。伍尔霍顿（Woolhorton）那帮爱开玩笑的人——总有那么些自以为聪明的爱开玩笑的人——认为猪肉之所以对他这么合适，是因为他的性子跟猪一样倔。

九旬老人还吃猪肉，这在常理上简直再荒唐不过了——他的朋友们都这么说。

===Original===
"Pork," said the physician, had down from London to see him on one occasion, "pork is the first on the list of indigestible articles of food. It takes from six to eight hours for the gastric apparatus to reduce its fibres. The stomach becomes overloaded—acidity is the result; nightmares, pains, and innumerable ills are the consequence. The very worst thing Mr. Iden could eat."

"Hum," growled the family doctor, a native of Woolhorton, when he heard of this. "Hum!" low in his throat, like an irate bulldog. If in the least excited, like most other country folk, he used the provincial pronunciation. "Hum! A' have lived twenty years on pork. Let'n yet it!"

Grandfather Iden intended to eat it, and did eat it six days out of seven, not, of course, roast pork every dinner; sometimes boiled pork; sometimes he baked it himself in the great oven. Now and then he varied it with pig-meat—good old country meat, let me tell you, pig-meat—such as sparerib, griskin, blade-bone, and that mysterious morsel, the "mouse." The chine he always sent over for Iden junior, who was a chine eater—a true Homeric diner—and to make it even, Iden junior sent in the best apples for sauce from his favourite russet trees. It was about the only amenity that survived between father and son.

===Chinese===
"猪肉，"有一次特意从伦敦请来给他看病的大夫说，"猪肉在不易消化的食物清单上排第一。胃部装置要花六到八个小时才能分解它的纤维。胃负担过重——导致酸性反流；噩梦、疼痛以及数不清的毛病接踵而至。艾登先生最不该吃的东西就是这个。"

"哼，"家里的医生——伍尔霍顿本地人——听闻此言，低吼了一声。"哼！"从喉咙深处发出，像一条愤怒的斗牛犬。稍一激动，他跟大多数乡下人一样，就用起了土腔。"哼！他吃猪肉吃了二十年了。让他吃去！"

老格兰弗·艾登打算吃猪肉，也确实七天里有六天都吃，当然不是顿顿吃烤猪肉；有时吃煮猪肉；有时他自己在大烤箱里烤。偶尔也换换花样，吃些别的猪肉——我跟你说，乡下的好猪肉——比如肋排、里脊、肩胛骨，还有那神秘的小块肉，"老鼠肉"。脊骨他总是叫人送给小艾登（Iden junior），小艾登是个爱吃脊骨的人——十足的荷马式食客——为了回礼，小艾登就从他心爱的褐苹果树上摘最好的苹果送去给他做酱。这大概是父子之间仅剩的一点温情了。

===Original===
The pig-meat used to be delicious in the old house at home, before we all went astray along the different paths of life; fresh from the pigs fed and killed on the premises, nutty, and juicy to the palate. Much of it is best done on a gridiron—here's heresy! A gridiron is flat blasphemy to the modern school of scientific cookery. Scientific fiddlestick! Nothing like a gridiron to set your lips watering.

But the "mouse,"—what was the "mouse?" The London butchers can't tell me. It was a titbit. I suppose it still exists in pigs; but London folk are so ignorant.

Grandfather Iden ate pig in every shape and form, that is, he mumbled the juice out of it, and never complained of indigestion.

===Chinese===
在老家那座老宅子里，我们还没各自走上人生歧路之前，猪肉可是美味的；都是用自养自宰的猪做的，有坚果的香气，吃在嘴里汁水丰盈。很多部位最好在烤架上做——这可是异端之说！在现代科学烹饪学派眼里，用烤架简直是亵渎。科学个鬼！没有什么比烤架更让人流口水的了。

可那"老鼠肉"——到底是什么"老鼠肉"？伦敦的屠夫们答不上来。那是一道美味的尖儿。我猜猪身上还是有的；只是伦敦人太无知了。

老格兰弗·艾登把猪肉各种花样都吃遍了，也就是说，他把汁水咂干净，从没闹过消化不良。

===Original===
He was up at five o'clock every morning of his life, pottering about the great oven with his baker's man. In summer if it was fine he went out at six for a walk in the Pines—the promenade of Woolhorton.

"If you wants to get well," old Dr. Butler used to say, "you go for a walk in the marning afore the aair have been braathed auver."

Before the air has been breathed over—inspired and re-inspired by human crowds, while it retains the sweetness of the morning, like water fresh from the spring; that was when it possessed its value, according to bluff, gruff, rule-of-thumb old Butler. Depend upon it, there is something in his dictum, too.

Amaryllis hesitated at the thought of the pork, for he often had it underdone, so the old gentleman dismissed her in his most gracious manner to dine with the rest.

===Chinese===
他这辈子每天早上五点就起床，和他的面包师傅一起在大烤箱旁边转悠。夏天如果天好，六点钟他就出门去松林散步——那是伍尔霍顿的散步胜地。

"你要是想好起来，"老巴特勒（Butler）大夫过去常说，"你就趁着早上空气还没被人家呼来吸去之前，出去走走。"

空气被呼来吸去——被人群反复吸入吐出之前，还保留着清晨的甘甜，就像刚从泉眼里出来的水；在粗犷、暴躁、凭经验办事的老巴特勒看来，那才是它有价值的时候。信我的，这话也有几分道理。

阿玛丽莉斯一想到猪肉就犹豫了，因为他常吃半生不熟的，于是老先生便以最慈祥的姿态打发她去跟其他人一道吃。

===Original===
She went down the corridor and took the seat placed for her. There was a posy of primroses beside her napkin—posies of primroses all round the table.

This raging old Tory of ninety years would give a shilling for the earliest primrose the boys could find for him in the woods. Someone got him a peacock's feather which had fallen from Beaconsfield's favourites—a real Beaconsfield peacock-feather—which he had set in the centre of a splendid screen of feathers that cost him twenty guineas. The screen was upstairs in the great drawing-room near a bow window which overlooked the fair.

People, you see, took pains to get him feathers and anything he fancied, on account of the twenty thousand spade guineas in the iron box under the bed.

===Chinese===
她沿着走廊走到她的座位坐下。餐巾旁边放着一束报春花——整张桌子上都摆着一束束报春花。

这个九旬的激进托利党老家伙，为了男孩子们能在林子里给他找到最早的一朵报春花，肯出一先令。有人给他弄来一根孔雀翎，是从比肯斯菲尔德（Beaconsfield）心爱的孔雀身上掉下来的——一根真正的比肯斯菲尔德孔雀翎——他把它插在一座华丽的羽毛屏风正中，那屏风花了他二十几尼。屏风搁在楼上大客厅里，旁边是一扇凸窗，能俯瞰集市。

你看，人们费尽心思给他弄羽毛和他喜欢的任何东西，都是因为床底下铁皮箱子里那两万把铲形几尼。

===Original===
His daughters, elderly, uninteresting married folk, begged him not to keep a peacock's feather in the house—it would certainly bring misfortune. The superstition was so firmly rooted in their minds that they actually argued with him—*argued* with Grandfather Iden!—pointing out to him the fearful risk he was running. He puffed and coughed, and grew red in the face—the great grey hat shook and tottered with anger; not for all the Powers of Darkness would he have given up that feather.

The chairs round the large table were arranged in accordance with the age of the occupants. There were twenty-one grandchildren, and a number of aunts, uncles, and so on; a vague crowd that does not concern us. The eldest sat at the head of the table, the next in age followed, and so all round the dishes. This arrangement placed Amaryllis rather low down—a long way from the top and fountain of honour—and highly displeased her. She despised and disliked the whole vague crowd of her relations, yet being there, she felt that she ought to have had a position above them all. Her father—Iden, junior—was old Iden's only son and natural heir; therefore her father's chair ought to have been at the top of the table, and hers ought to have been next to his.

===Chinese===
他的女儿们——上了年纪的、无聊的已婚妇人——恳求他不要在家里留孔雀翎——那肯定会招来灾祸。这迷信在她们脑子里扎了根，她们居然跟他争辩起来——*跟*老格兰弗·艾登*争辩*！——向他指出他冒的风险有多大。他呼哧呼哧喘气，咳嗽，脸涨得通红——那顶灰色大礼帽气得直发抖；就算拿所有黑暗势力来换，他也不肯交出那根孔雀翎。

大桌子周围的椅子是按就座者的年龄排的。有二十一个孙辈，还有一大帮姑姑、叔叔什么的；一群面目模糊的人，跟我们无关。最年长的坐在桌子顶端，依次按年龄往下排，绕着菜碟子排一圈。这么一排，阿玛丽莉斯的位置就靠后了——离权力的顶端和源泉很远——这让她极其不快。她瞧不起也不喜欢那一帮面目模糊的亲戚，但既然来了，她觉得自己本该凌驾于所有人之上。她父亲——小艾登（Iden junior）——是老艾登唯一的儿子、理所当然的继承人；所以她父亲的椅子本该在桌子顶端，她的椅子也该紧挨着他。

===Original===
Instead of which, as her father was not the eldest, his seat was some distance from the top, and hers again, was a long way from his.

All the other chairs were full, but her father's chair was empty.

The vague crowd were so immensely eager to pay their despicable court to the Spade-Guinea Man, not one of them stopped away; the old, the young, the lame, the paralytic, all found means to creep in to Grandfather Iden's annual dinner. His only son and natural heir was alone absent. How eagerly poor Amaryllis glanced from time to time at that empty chair, hoping against hope that her dear father would come in at the Psalms, or even at the sermon, and disappoint the venomous, avaricious hearts of the enemies around her.

For well she knew how delighted they were to see his chair empty, as a visible sign and token of the gulf between father and son, and well she knew how diligently each laboured to deepen the misunderstanding and set fuel to the flame of the quarrel. If the son were disinherited, consider the enormous profit to the rest of them!

===Chinese===
可实际上，她父亲不是长子，他的座位离顶端有一段距离，她的座位又离他很远。

其他椅子都坐满了，只有她父亲的椅子空着。

那一帮面目模糊的人急于向铲形几尼老爷献殷勤，没有一个缺席；老的、少的、瘸的、瘫的，各显神通，都挤进来参加老格兰弗·艾登一年一度的晚宴。唯独他唯一的儿子、理所当然的继承人没有来。可怜的阿玛丽莉斯多么热切地一次又一次瞥向那把空椅子，抱着渺茫的希望，盼着她亲爱的父亲能在唱圣诗时、甚至在听布道时走进来，让周围那帮心怀叵测、贪得无厌的家伙大失所望。

她很清楚他们看到那把空椅子有多高兴，那是父子之间鸿沟的明显标志；她也很清楚他们各怀鬼胎，怎样竭力加深误会、火上浇油。要是儿子被剥夺了继承权，想想他们能捞到多大的好处！

===Original===
Grandfather Iden made no secret of the fact that he had not signed a will. It was believed that several rough drafts had been sketched out for him, but, in his own words—and he was no teller of falsehoods—he had not decided on his will. If only they could persuade him to make his will they might feel safe of something; but suppose he went off pop, all in a moment, as these extraordinarily healthy old people are said to do, and the most of his estate in land! Consider what a contingency—almost all of it would go to his own son. Awful thing!

Amaryllis was aware how they all stared at her and quizzed her over and over; her hair, her face, her form, but most of all her dress. They were so poor at home she had not had a new dress this twelvemonth past; it was true her dress was decent and comfortable, and she really looked very nice in it to any man's eye; but a girl does not want a comfortable dress, she wants something in the style of the day, and just sufficiently advanced to make the women's eyes turn green with envy. It is not the men's eyes; it is the women's eyes.

===Chinese===
老格兰弗·艾登从不讳言自己还没立遗嘱。据说有好几份草稿已经替他拟好了，但用他自己的话来说——他从不说假话——他还没决定立什么样的遗嘱。只要他们能劝他立下遗嘱，多少能踏实些；可万一他像那些特别硬朗的老人一样，说走就走，而他的家业大头全是田产呢！想想这风险有多大——几乎全部会落到他亲儿子手里。太可怕了！

阿玛丽莉斯知道他们怎样盯着她，怎样一遍又一遍地打量她；她的头发、她的脸蛋、她的身段，但最多的还是她的衣裳。家里太穷了，她一整年没添过新衣裳；不错，她的衣裳体面又舒适，在任何男人眼里她穿着都好看得很；但姑娘要的不是舒适的衣裳，她要的是时兴的款式，时髦得刚好能让女人们嫉妒得眼睛发绿。不是男人的眼睛；是女人的眼睛。

===Original===
Amaryllis sat up very quiet and unconcerned, trying with all her might to make them feel she was the Heiress, not only an only son's only daughter, but the only son's only offspring—doubly the Heiress of Grandfather Iden.

The old folk, curious in such matters, had prophesied so soon as she was born that there would be no more children at Coombe Oaks, and so it fell out. For it had been noticed in the course of generations, that in the direct line of Iden when the first child was a daughter there were none to follow. And further, that there never was but one Miss Iden at a time.

If the Direct Line had a daughter first, they never had any more children; consequently that daughter was the only Miss Iden.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯端坐着，安静而漠不关心，拼尽全力让他们感觉到她是继承人，不仅是独子的独女，而且是独子唯一的骨血——双重的继承人，老格兰弗·艾登的继承人。

那些好奇的老人们，在她一出生时就预言库姆橡树庄（Coombe Oaks）再也不会有别的孩子了，事实果然如此。因为历代人都注意到，在艾登家族的直系一脉，如果头胎是女儿，后面就不会再有孩子了。而且，同一时期从来只有一位艾登小姐。

如果直系先得了女儿，就再不会有别的孩子；所以那女儿便是唯一的艾登小姐。

===Original===
If the Direct Line had a son, they never had a second son, though they might have daughters; but then, in order that there should still be only one Miss Iden, it always happened that the first died, or was married early, before the second came into existence.

Such was the tradition of the Iden family; they had a long pedigree, the Idens, reaching farther back than the genealogies of many a peer, and it had been observed that this was the rule of their descent.

Amaryllis was the only Miss Iden, and the heiress, through her father, of the Spade-Guinea Man. She tried to make them feel that she knew it and felt it; that she was the Iden of the Idens. Her proud face—it was a very proud face naturally—darkened a little, and grew still more disdainful in its utter scorn and loathing of the vague crowd of enemies.

===Chinese===
如果直系先得了儿子，就不会再有第二个儿子，虽然可能有女儿；但为了确保仍然只有一位艾登小姐，长女总是夭折，或者在第二个孩子出生之前就早早嫁出去了。

这就是艾登家族的传统；他们有着漫长的家谱，艾登家的历史比许多贵族的谱系还要久远，而人们注意到，这便是他们世代相传的规矩。

阿玛丽莉斯是唯一的艾登小姐，也是铲形几尼老爷通过她父亲传下来的继承人。她竭力让他们感觉到她知道这一点、也感受得到这一点；她是艾登家的艾登。她那张骄傲的脸——生来就很骄傲——微微沉了下来，对那一帮面目模糊的敌人愈发鄙夷，充满了彻头彻尾的蔑视与厌恶。

## XIII

===Original===
To one, as it were, in the gallery, it was a delight to see her; her sweet cheeks, fresh as the dawn, reddening with suppressed indignation; her young brow bent; her eyes cast down—don't you think for a moment she would deign to look at them—pride in her heart, and resolute determination to fight for her dear father and mother.

===Chinese===
在一旁旁观，看她真是一种享受：她甜美的双颊如黎明般清新，此刻因压抑的愤慨而泛红；年轻的眉头紧蹙；双眼低垂——别以为她会屈尊看他们一眼——心中满是骄傲，决意为她亲爱的父母挺身而出。

===Original===
But she felt as she sat so unconcerned that there was a crack in her boot unmended, and it seemed as if everyone could see it though under the solid table. She had not had a really sound pair of boots for many, many months; they could not afford her a new pair at home, and the stupid shoemaker, "Established 1697," was such a time repairing her others.

===Chinese===
但她安坐时感到自己靴子上有一道裂口还没补上，尽管被厚实的桌子挡着，似乎每个人都看得见。她已经好多好多个月没有穿过一双真正完好的靴子了；家里给她买不起新的，那个"创立于1697年"的笨鞋匠修她另一双靴子修了那么久。

===Original===
She would not look at them, but she knew that they were all dressed better than she was; there were some of them very poor, and very vulgar, too, but they were all dressed better than her, and without a doubt had sound boots on their feet.

===Chinese===
她不愿看他们，但知道他们所有人都穿得比她好；其中有几个其实也很穷，也很粗俗，可穿的都比她强，而且毫无疑问脚上都蹬着完好的靴子。

===Original===
The cottagers in Coombe hamlet always had sound boots; she never had; nor, indeed, her mother. Her father had a pair, being compelled by the character of his work in the fields to take care of himself so far, though he wore a ragged coat. But neither mother nor daughter ever had a whole pair of boots—whole and sound as the very cottagers had.

===Chinese===
库姆小村（Coombe hamlet）的农户总有一双完好的靴子；她从来没有过；她母亲也没有。父亲有一双，是田间劳作的性质迫使他不得不这样照顾自己，尽管他穿着一件破旧的外套。但母亲和女儿都从来没有过一双完整完好的靴子——像那些农户穿的那样完整而结实。

===Original===
If Amaryllis had sat there with naked feet she would have been prouder than ever, and that is why I always loved her so; she was not to be put down by circumstances, she was above external things.

===Chinese===
如果阿玛丽莉斯光着脚坐在那里，她反而会比任何时候都骄傲——这正是我一直如此深爱她的缘故；她不会被境遇压倒，她超越于外在之物。

===Original===
But as time went on, and the dinner was nearly over—she had scarcely eaten anything—and as she glanced from time to time at her father's empty chair, and knew that he would not come, and that his defection would revive the old quarrel which might so easily have been mended, the Flamma blood began to rise and grow hotter and hotter, and the foot with the worn boot on it began to tap the floor.

===Chinese===
然而随着时间推移，晚餐快要结束了——她几乎什么也没吃——她时不时瞥向父亲那把空椅子，知道他不会来，而他的缺席会重新点燃那场本可以轻易化解的老争吵，弗拉玛（Flamma）的血液开始涌动，越来越滚烫，那只穿着破靴子的脚开始敲打地板。

===Original===
The Flamma blood would have liked to have swept the whole company over a precipice into the Red Sea as the herd of swine in old time. It was either the Red Sea or somewhere; geography is of no consequence.

===Chinese===
弗拉玛的血液恨不得把在座所有人扫下悬崖坠入红海，就像古时候那群猪一样。要么是红海，要么是别的什么地方；地理无关紧要。

===Original===
The Flamma blood would have liked to have seen them all poisoned and dying on their seats.

===Chinese===
弗拉玛的血液恨不得看到他们全都中毒，死在各自的座位上。

===Original===
The Flamma blood would have been glad to stick a knife into each of them—only it would not have touched them with the longest hop-pole in Kent, so utter was its loathing of the crew gloating over that empty chair.

===Chinese===
弗拉玛的血液恨不得往每个人身上捅一刀——只可惜它连肯特郡最长的啤酒花杆子都不愿碰他们，它对这群对着那把空椅子幸灾乐祸的人厌恶到了极点。

===Original===
And for once Amaryllis did not check it, and did not say to herself, "I *will* not be a Flamma."

===Chinese===
这一次阿玛丽莉斯没有克制自己，没有对自己说"我*绝不*做弗拉玛家的人"。

===Original===
Towards the end of the tedious banquet the word was passed round that everyone was to sit still, as Grandfather Iden was coming to look at his descendants.

===Chinese===
这场令人厌烦的宴会行将结束之际，消息传遍了席间：所有人都要坐好别动，艾登爷爷（Grandfather Iden）要来看望他的子孙后代了。

===Original===
There was not the least fear of any of them stirring, for they well knew his custom—to walk round, and speak a few words to everyone in turn, and to put a new golden sovereign into their hands. Thirty-two sovereigns it was in all—one for each—but the thirty-third was always a spade-guinea, which was presented to the individual who had best pleased him during the year.

===Chinese===
根本不用担心有人会动，因为他们对他的习惯了如指掌——他会绕着走一圈，依次跟每个人说几句话，然后往他们手里放一枚崭新的金索维林。总共三十二枚索维林，每人一枚，但第三十三枚总是一枚铲形基尼（spade-guinea），奖给那一年最令他满意的那个人。

===Original===
A genial sort of custom, no doubt, but fancy the emulation and the heartburning over the spade-guinea! For the fortunate winner usually considered himself the nearest to the Will.

===Chinese===
这习俗确实称得上和蔼可亲，但你且想想为了那枚铲形基尼而生出的竞争和嫉恨吧！因为幸运的赢家通常自以为最接近遗嘱的受益人选。

===Original===
Amaryllis' cheeks began to burn at the thought that she should have to take his horrible money. A hideous old monster he was to her at that moment—not that he had done anything to her personally—but he left her dear father to be worried out of his life by petty tradesmen, and her dear mother to go without a pair of decent boots, while he made this pompous distribution among these wretches. The hideous old monster!

===Chinese===
一想到自己竟要拿他那可恶的钱，阿玛丽莉斯的脸颊便灼烧起来。在那一刻，他对她而言就是一个可怕的老怪物——并非他本人对她做了什么——而是他放任她亲爱的父亲被小商贩们逼得走投无路，放任她亲爱的母亲连一双体面的靴子都没有，自己却在这群无耻之徒面前大摆排场分发钱财。这个可怕的老怪物！

===Original===
Out in the town the boys behind his back gave him endless nicknames: Granfer Iden, Floury Iden, My Lord Lardy-Cake, Marquis Iden, His Greasy Grace; and, indeed, with his whims and humours, and patronage, his caprices and ways of going on, if he but had a patent of nobility, Grandfather Iden would have made a wonderfully good duke.

===Chinese===
镇上的男孩们背地里给他起了没完没了的绰号：格兰弗·艾登（Granfer Iden）、面粉艾登（Floury Iden）、猪油蛋糕勋爵（My Lord Lardy-Cake）、艾登侯爵（Marquis Iden）、油腻阁下（His Greasy Grace）；说实在的，以他的性情、兴致、恩庇、反复无常和行事风格，只要有一纸贵族封状，艾登爷爷准能当个了不起的公爵。

===Original===
By-and-by in comes the old Pacha, still wearing his great grey tottery hat, and proceeds from chair to chair, tapping folk on the shoulder, saying a gracious word or two, and dropping his new golden sovereigns in their eager palms. There was a loud hum of conversation as he went round; they all tried to appear so immensely happy to see him.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，老帕夏（Pacha）走了进来，仍戴着那顶摇摇欲坠的大灰帽，挨个走到每把椅子旁，拍拍人们的肩膀，说上两句客套话，然后把崭新的金索维林塞进他们急切的手掌里。他走动时周围响起一片热烈的交谈声；每个人都装出见到他无比高兴的样子。

===Original===
Amaryllis did not exactly watch him, but of course knew what he was about, when suddenly there was a dead silence. Thirty-two people suddenly stopped talking as if the pneumatic brake had been applied to their lips by a sixty-ton locomotive.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯没有特意看他，但当然知道他在做什么，突然间一片死寂。三十二个人同时停止了说话，仿佛一台六十吨的机车给他们的嘴唇按上了气动刹车。

===Original===
Dead, ominous silence. You could almost hear the cat licking his paw under the table.

===Chinese===
死一般的、不祥的寂静。你几乎能听见桌子底下猫在舔爪子。

===Original===
Amaryllis looked, and saw the old man leaning with both hands on the back of his son's empty chair.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯望过去，看见老人双手撑在他儿子空椅子的椅背上。

===Original===
He seemed to cling to it as if it was a spar floating on the barren ocean of life and death into which his withered old body was sinking.

===Chinese===
他似乎紧抓着它不放，仿佛那是漂浮在生死荒漠之海上的一根浮木，而他枯瘦的老躯体正在下沉。

===Original===
Perhaps he really would have clung like that to his son had but his son come to him, and borne a little, and for a little while, with his ways.

===Chinese===
也许他的儿子若肯来找他，稍加忍耐他的脾气，哪怕只是一小会儿，他真的会那样紧紧抓住儿子不放。

===Original===
A sorrowful thing to see—the old man of ninety clinging to the back of his son's empty chair. His great grey tottery hat seemed about to tumble on the floor—his back bowed a little more—and he groaned deeply, three times.

===Chinese===
那景象令人心碎——九十岁的老人紧抓着他儿子空椅子的椅背。他那顶摇摇欲坠的大灰帽似乎随时要掉在地上——他的背又弯下去一些——他深深地呻吟了三声。

===Original===
We can see, being out of the play and spectators merely, that there was a human cry for help in the old man's groan—his heart yearned for his son's strong arm to lean on.

===Chinese===
我们作为置身事外的旁观者看得出来，老人的呻吟中有着人类求救的呼声——他的心渴望着儿子强壮的手臂可以依靠。

===Original===
The crowd of relations were in doubt as to whether they should rejoice, whether the groan was a sign of indignation, of anger too deep ever to be forgotten, or whether they should be alarmed at the possibility of reconciliation.

===Chinese===
那群亲戚举棋不定：到底该不该高兴，这呻吟是愤怒的征兆、是永难消解的深重怨怼，还是该为和解的可能性而惊慌？

===Original===
The Flamma blood was up too much in Amaryllis for her to feel pity for him as she would have done in any other mood; she hated him all the more; he was rich, the five-shilling fare was nothing to him, he could hire a fly from the "Lamb Inn," and drive over and make friends with her father in half an hour. Groaning there—the hideous old monster! and her mother without a decent pair of boots.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯的弗拉玛血性此刻太强烈了，她无法像在其他情绪下那样怜悯他；她反而更加憎恨他；他那么有钱，五先令的宴席费对他不算什么，他完全可以从"羔羊旅馆"（Lamb Inn）雇辆马车，半小时内就过来和她父亲言归于好。却坐在那里呻吟——这个可怕的老怪物！而她的母亲连一双体面的靴子都没有。

===Original===
In a moment or two Grandfather Iden recovered himself, and continued the distribution, and by-and-by Amaryllis felt him approach her chair. She did not even turn to look at him, so he took her hand, and placed two coins in it, saying in his most gracious way that the sovereign was for her father, and the guinea—the spade-guinea—for herself. She muttered something—she knew not what—she could but just restrain herself from throwing the money on the floor.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，艾登爷爷缓过神来，继续分发礼物。不久，阿玛丽莉斯感到他走近了她的椅子。她甚至没有转头看他，于是他拉起她的手，往里面放了两枚硬币，用最亲切的口吻说，索维林是给她父亲的，而基尼——那枚铲形基尼——是给她的。她喃喃说了些什么——自己也不知道说了什么——只是勉强克制住没把钱扔到地上。

===Original===
It was known in a moment that Amaryllis had the guinea. Conceive the horror, the hatred, the dread of the crowd of sycophants! That the Heiress Apparent should be the favourite!

===Chinese===
消息立刻传开了：阿玛丽莉斯拿到了基尼。想想那群谄媚之徒的惊恐、憎恨和惶惧吧！法定继承人竟然是最受宠的那一个！

===Original===
Yet more. Half-an-hour later, just after they had all got upstairs into the great drawing-room, and some were officiously and reverently admiring the peacock-feather in the screen, and some looking out of the bow window at the fair, there came a message for Amaryllis to put on her hat and go for a walk with her grandfather.

===Chinese===
还有更惊人的。半小时后，当他们全都上楼进了大客厅，有人正殷勤而虔诚地赞叹屏风上的孔雀羽毛，有人在凸窗前眺望集市，这时传来消息说阿玛丽莉斯该戴上帽子，陪她祖父去散步。

===Original===
There was not one among all the crowd in the drawing-room who had ever been invited to accompany Iden Pacha.

===Chinese===
客厅里那么多人，从来没有一个被邀请过陪艾登帕夏（Iden Pacha）散步。

===Original===
Three days ago at home, if anyone had told Amaryllis that she would be singled out in this way, first to receive the Iden medal—the spade-guinea stamp of approval—and then, above all things, to be honoured by walking out with this "almighty" grandfather, how delighted she would have been at the thought of the triumph!

===Chinese===
三天前在家里，若有人告诉阿玛丽莉斯她会被这样特别对待——先获颁艾登勋章，也就是那枚铲形基尼的认可印记，然后最重要的是，被荣幸地邀请与这位"万能"的祖父一同散步，想到这份荣耀她该有多么欣喜若狂！

===Original===
But now it was just the reverse. Triumph over these people—pah! a triumph over rats and flies or some such creatures. She actually felt lowered in her own esteem by being noticed at all among them. Honoured by this old horror—she revolted at it. *He* honour her with his approval—she hated him.

===Chinese===
但现在恰恰相反。对这些人的胜利——呸！不过是战胜老鼠苍蝇之类的东西罢了。被他们注意到反而让她觉得自尊受损。被这个老怪物"荣幸"地看待——她对此深感厌恶。*他*用他的认可来"荣幸"她——她恨他。

===Original===
The other day a travelling piano was wheeled through Coombe and set up a tune in that lonesome spot. Though it was but a mechanical piece of music, with the cogs as it were of the mechanism well marked by the thump, thump, it seemed to cheer the place—till she went out to the gate to look at the Italian woman who danced about while the grinding was done, and saw that she had a sound pair of boots on. That very morning her mother in crossing the road had set the Flamma rheumatism shooting in her bones, for the dampness of the mud came through the crack in her boot.

===Chinese===
前几天，一架旅行钢琴被推过库姆（Coombe），在那荒凉的地方奏起了一曲。虽然不过是机械演奏，齿轮转动时发出的咚咚声清晰可辨，却似乎给那地方添了几分生气——直到她出门去大门边看那个在磨坊边跳舞的意大利女人，发现她脚上穿着一双完好的靴子。就在那天早上，她母亲过马路时，靴子裂缝让湿泥渗了进去，引发了弗拉玛家族的风湿老毛病，痛入骨髓。

===Original===
This miserable old Iden Pacha thought to honour her while he let her mother walk about with her stocking on the wet ground!

===Chinese===
这个可恶的老艾登帕夏竟想用他的"荣幸"来抬举她，却任由她母亲穿着湿透的袜子在泥地上走路！

===Original===
The Flamma blood was up in her veins—what did she care for guineas!

===Chinese===
弗拉玛的血液在她血管里沸腾——她才不在乎什么基尼！

===Original===
As she was putting her hat on in the bedroom before the glass she looked round to see that no one was watching, and then stooped down and put the spade-guinea in the dust of the floor under the dressing-table. She would have none of his hateful money. The sovereign she took care of because it was for her father, and he might buy something useful with it; he wanted a few shillings badly enough.

===Chinese===
她在卧室镜子前戴帽子时，环顾四周确认没人注意，然后弯下腰把铲形基尼塞到梳妆台下的地板灰尘里。她才不要他那可恶的钱。索维林她收好了，因为那是给父亲的，他可以用它买些有用的东西；他正缺几先令呢。

===Original===
So the spade-guinea remained in the dust of the floor for a week or two, till it pleased the housemaid to move the dressing-table to brush away the accumulation, when she found the shining one in the fluff. Being over thirty, she held her tongue, the guinea henceforward travelled down the stream of Time fast enough though silently, but she took the first opportunity of examining the iron box under the Pacha's bed, thinking perhaps there might be a chink in it. And it was curious how for some time afterwards a fit of extraordinary industry prevailed in the house; there was not a table, a chair, or any piece of furniture that was not chivvied about under pretence of polishing. She actually had a day's holiday and a cast-off gown given to her as a reward for her labours.

===Chinese===
铲形基尼就这样在地板灰尘里待了一两个星期，直到女仆心血来潮挪开梳妆台清扫积灰，在毛絮中发现了那枚闪闪发亮的基尼。她年过三十，所以守口如瓶，基尼从此在时间的河流中静静流淌，但一有机会她就去检查帕夏床底下的铁盒子，想看看有没有缝隙可趁。说来也怪，此后很长一段时间里，家里突然掀起了一股异常勤快的风气；没有一张桌子、一把椅子或任何一件家具不是以打磨为借口被挪来挪去的。她甚至得到了一天假期和一件旧裙子作为对她辛劳的奖赏。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
Amaryllis did not look back as she walked beside her grandfather slowly up the street, or she would have seen the company of relations watching them from the bow-window.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）走在祖父身旁，沿着街道缓缓前行，并没有回头看，否则她会看见那群亲戚正从凸窗后面注视着他们。

===Original===
Iden went straight through the crowd without any hesitation on account of his age—angry as she was, Amaryllis feared several times lest the clumsy people should overturn him, and tried her best to shield him. But he had a knack of keeping on his feet—the sort of knack you learn by skating—and did not totter much more than usual, despite the press.

===Chinese===
艾登（Iden）毫不迟疑地径直穿过人群，全然不因年迈而却步。阿玛丽莉斯虽然生着气，却三番五次担心那些笨手笨脚的人会把他撞倒，竭力护着他。但他自有站稳脚跟的诀窍——滑冰的人常有那种本事——尽管人潮拥挤，他也不比平时多晃几下。

===Original===
The world gets on with very little amusement somehow. Here were two or three thousand people packed in the street, and all they had to enliven their festive gathering was the same old toys their fathers' fathers' fathers had set before them.

===Chinese===
这世上的消遣，不知怎的，总是少得可怜。街面上挤了两三千人，而让他们欢聚一堂、聊以助兴的，不外乎是祖辈的祖辈的祖辈就摆出来的那些老掉牙的玩意儿。

===Original===
Rows of booths for the display of "fairings," gingerbread, nuts, cakes, brandy-balls, and sugarplums stood in the gutter each side.

===Chinese===
街道两侧的排水沟旁，一排排摊位陈列着"集市糕点"、姜饼、坚果、蛋糕、白兰地糖球和蜜饯糖果。

===Original===
The "fairings" were sweet biscuits—they have been made every fair this hundred years.

===Chinese===
所谓"集市糕点"，不过是甜饼干——一百年来每场集市都在做，从未变过。

===Original===
The nuts were dry and hard, just as Spanish nuts always are. The gingerbread was moulded in the same old shapes of clumsy horses outlined with gilt.

===Chinese===
坚果又干又硬，西班牙坚果向来如此。姜饼也是老样子，模子还是那些笨拙的马匹形状，描着金边。

===Original===
There was the same old trumpeting and tootling, tom-tomming, and roaring of showmen's voices. The same old roundabouts, only now they were driven by steam, and short, quick whistles announced that the whirligig caravan was travelling round the world. The fat woman, the strong man, the smashers tapping the "claret," the "Pelican of the Wilderness," that mystic and melancholy bird, the rifle galleries, the popping for nuts—behold these are they our fathers have seen.

===Chinese===
同样的老把戏照旧上演：喇叭呜呜、唢呐嘟嘟、咚咚鼓响、叫卖声此起彼伏。旋转木马还是老样子，只不过如今用蒸汽驱动，短促尖利的汽笛宣告着旋转花车正环游世界。胖女人、大力士、敲"红牌"的击球手、"荒野鹈鹕"——那只神秘而忧伤的鸟——射击场、打坚果——瞧吧，这些都是父辈们见过的。

===Original===
There is nothing new under the sun—not even at Epsom. The first time I saw the wonderful crowd of the Derby Day—perhaps the largest crowd in the world—I could scarcely believe my eyes, for I found on passing through it that the hundreds of thousands of people there had nothing more to amuse them than they would have found at an ordinary country fair. Swings, roundabouts, cockshies at coconuts, rootletum, tootletum, and beer. That was all. No new amusement whatsoever: a very humdrum sort of world, my masters!

===Chinese===
太阳底下无新事——连埃普索姆也一样。我头一回见到德比赛日那人山人海的壮观场面——也许是全世界最多的人群——简直不敢相信自己的眼睛，因为当我穿过人群后发现，那几十万人的消遣，跟普通的乡村集市上能找到的并无二致。秋千、旋转木马、投椰子球、叮叮咚咚、嘟嘟嘟嘟，再就是啤酒。仅此而已。什么新花样也没有：这世界真是乏味得很呐，诸位！

===Original===
The next finest crowd is the crowd on August bank-holiday all along the Brighton beach, and there it is just the same. Nothing for the folk but Punch, brass bands, and somersaulters—dull old stories in my grandmother's time.

===Chinese===
第二壮观的人群要数八月银行假日挤满布莱顿海滩的那一拨，可那也是一样的光景。老百姓能得到的不过是潘趣木偶戏、铜管乐队和翻筋斗的杂耍——在我祖母那个年代就已经是老掉牙的把戏了。

===Original===
Xerxes offered a reward to anyone who could invent him a fresh pleasure—the multitude of the Derby Day and Brighton beach should do the same. But indeed they do, for an immense fortune would certainly be the reward of such a discoverer. One gets tired of pitching sticks at coconuts all one's time.

===Chinese===
薛西斯曾悬赏，谁若能发明一种新奇的享乐便赏他——德比赛日和布莱顿海滩上那黑压压的人群也该这么干才对。不过他们确实也在做了，因为任何此类发明者肯定会得到一笔巨赏。人一辈子总不能老扔棍子打椰子吧。

===Original===
However, at Woolhorton nobody but the very rawest and crudest folk cared for the shows, all they did care was to alternately stand stock still and then shove. First they shoved as far as the "Lion" and had some beer, then they shoved back to the "Lamb" and had some beer, then they stood stock still in the street and blocked those who were shoving. Several thousand people were thus happily occupied, and the Lion and the Lamb laid down together peacefully that day.

===Chinese===
然而在伍尔霍顿（Woolhorton），除了那些最粗鄙、最没见过世面的乡下人，没人对这些表演感兴趣，他们在意的不过是先呆站着，再推搡一阵。先是一路挤到"狮子酒馆"喝上几杯，然后又挤回"羔羊酒馆"再喝几杯，接着又在街上站定不动，挡住了还在挤的人。几千人就这样乐此不疲地消磨着时光，那一天"狮子"和"羔羊"倒是和平共处了。

===Original===
Amaryllis and old Iden had in like manner to shove, for there was no other way to get through, no one thought of moving, or giving any passage, if you wanted to progress you must shoulder them aside. As Grandfather Iden could not shove very hard they were frequently compelled to wait till the groups opened, and thus it happened that Amaryllis found herself once face to face with Jack Duck.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯和老艾登也不得不这样挤着走，因为除此之外别无他法。没人想到要挪个位置、让出条路来，你想往前走，就得用肩膀把他们拨开。由于艾登爷爷挤不太动，他们时常被迫停下等人群散开。也就是这样，阿玛丽莉斯才迎面碰上了杰克·达克（Jack Duck）。

===Original===
He kind of sniggered in a foolish way at Amaryllis, and touched his hat to Iden. "You ain't a been over to Coombe lately, Mr. Iden," he said.

===Chinese===
他冲着阿玛丽莉斯傻笑了一下，又朝艾登碰了碰帽檐。"您有阵子没去库姆（Coombe）了吧，艾登先生。"他说。

===Original===
"No," replied the old man sharply, and went on.

===Chinese===
"没有。"老头子硬邦邦地答了一声，继续往前走。

===Original===
Jack could hardly have struck a note more discordant to Amaryllis. The father had not been to visit his son for more than a year—she did not want unpleasant memories stirred up.

===Chinese===
杰克这话在阿玛丽莉斯听来简直再刺耳不过了。父亲已经一年多没去看望他的儿子了——她不想让人勾起那些不愉快的往事。

===Original===
Again in another group a sturdy labourer touched his hat and asked her if her father was at fair, as he was looking out for a job. Old Iden started and grunted like a snorting horse.

===Chinese===
在另一堆人里，又有一个壮实的劳工碰了碰帽檐，问她父亲是不是也来赶集了，他正想找份活儿。老艾登猛地一惊，像匹打响鼻的马一样哼了一声。

===Original===
Amaryllis, though put out, stayed to speak kindly to him, for she knew he was always in difficulties. Bill Nye was that contradiction a strong man without work. He wanted to engage for mowing. Bill Nye was a mower at Coombe, and his father, Bill Nye, before him, many a long year before he was discovered in California.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯虽然心里不痛快，还是停下脚步和颜悦色地跟他说话，因为她知道他一直日子不好过。比尔·奈（Bill Nye）是个矛盾的人——身强力壮却找不到活儿干。他想找个割草的差事。比尔·奈在库姆是割草的好手，他父亲也叫比尔·奈，在他之前干了很多很多年，直到被发现去了加利福尼亚。

===Original===
When she overtook Iden he was struggling to pass the stream of the Orinoco, which set strongly at that moment out of the "Lamb" towards the "Lion." Strong men pushed out from the "Lamb" archway like a river into the sea, thrusting their way into the general crowd, and this mighty current cast back the tottering figure of old Iden as the swollen Orinoco swung the crank old Spanish caravels that tried to breast it.

===Chinese===
等她追上艾登时，他正挣扎着要渡过奥里诺科河（Orinoco）般的急流——此刻"羔羊酒馆"那边的人正大股大股地涌向"狮子酒馆"。壮汉们从"羔羊酒馆"的拱门里冲出来，如同河流奔入大海，涌入一般的人潮，这股强大的洪流把老艾登摇摇晃晃的身子推了回来，就像涨水的奥里诺科河摇晃着那些试图逆流而上的破旧西班牙帆船一样。

===Original===
It was as much as Amaryllis and he together could do to hold their ground at the edge of the current. While they were thus battling she chanced to look up.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯和他两人合力，才勉强在洪流边上站稳脚跟。就在他们这样挣扎的时候，她偶然抬起了头。

===Original===
A large window was open over the archway, and at this window a fellow was staring down at her. He stood in his shirtsleeves with a billiard-cue in his hand waiting his turn to play. It was the same young fellow, gentleman if you like, whose pale face had so displeased her that morning as he rode under when she watched the folk go by to fair. He was certainly the most advanced in civilization of all who had passed Plum Corner, and yet there was something in that pale and rather delicate face which was not in the coarse lineaments of the "varmers" and "drauvers" and "pig-dealers" who had gone by under the wall. Something that insulted her.

===Chinese===
拱门上方开着一扇大窗户，窗口有个家伙正居高临下地盯着她看。他只穿了件衬衫，手里攥着一根台球杆，等着轮到自己上场。正是那天早上她看着赶集的人群经过梅子角（Plum Corner）时骑马而过的那个面色苍白的年轻人——你大可以叫他绅士。他无疑是所有经过梅子角的人中最文明的一个，但那苍白而略显精致的脸庞上，有种东西是那些从墙下经过的"庄稼汉"、"赶车人"和"猪贩子"粗犷的五官里所没有的。那是一种冒犯她的东西。

===Original===
The face at the window was appraising her.

===Chinese===
窗口那张脸正在品评她。

===Original===
It was reckoning her up—so much for eyes, so much for hair, so much for figure, and as this went on the fingers were filling a pipe from an elastic tobacco-pouch. There was no romance, no poetry in that calculation—no rapture or pure admiration of beauty; there was a billiard-cue and a tobacco-pouch, and a glass of spirits and water, and an atmosphere of smoke, and a sound of clicking ivory balls at the back of the thought. His thumb was white where he had chalked it to make a better bridge for the cue. His face was white; for he had chalked it with dissipation. His physical body was whitened—chalked—a whited sepulchre; his moral nature likewise chalked.

===Chinese===
他正在心里盘算她——眼睛值多少，头发值多少，身段值多少，盘算之间手指还从一个弹性烟草袋里往烟斗里塞烟丝。这番盘算里没有浪漫，没有诗意——没有对美的狂喜或纯粹的倾慕；他脑子里想的是台球杆和烟草袋，一杯掺水的烈酒，一股烟雾弥漫的气息，以及象牙球撞击的咔嗒声。他拇指上沾了白粉，那是为了架杆时滑得更顺。他的脸也是白的——那是纵欲生活抹上的白粉。他的肉身是粉白的——像涂了白灰的坟墓；他的道德本性也同样涂满了白灰。

===Original===
At the back of his thought lay not the high esteem of the poet-thinker for beauty, but the cynical blackguardism of the XIX century.

===Chinese===
他心思深处流淌的，不是诗人思想家对美的敬仰，而是十九世纪那种冷嘲热讽的无赖哲学。

===Original===
The cynicism that deliberately reckons up things a Shakespeare would admire at their lowest possible sale value. A slow whiff of smoke from a corner of the sneering mouth, an air of intense knowingness, as much as to say, "You may depend upon me—I've been behind the scenes. All this is got up, you know; stage effect in front, pasteboard at the rear; nothing in it."

===Chinese===
那种故意把莎士比亚都会赞叹的事物贬到最低贱的出售价格的犬儒主义。嘲笑的嘴角慢悠悠地吐出一口烟，一脸故作高深的模样，仿佛在说："你可以信我——我可是后台的人。这一切都是摆出来的，懂吗？前面是舞台效果，后面全是纸板——没什么真东西。"

===Original===
In the sensuality of Nero there may still be found some trace of a higher nature; "What an artist the world has lost!" he exclaimed, dying.

===Chinese===
尼禄（Nero）的纵欲之中，尚且能寻到几分高贵天性的遗迹；临终时他还感叹道："这世界失去了一位多伟大的艺术家啊！"

===Original===
The empress Theodora craved for the applause of the theatre to which she exposed her beauty.

===Chinese===
皇后狄奥多拉（Theodora）渴望着剧场的掌声，为此不惜将她的美貌示于人前。

===Original===
This low, cynical nineteenth century blackguardism thinks of nothing but lowness, and has no ideal. The milliner even has an ideal, she looks to colour, shape, effect; though but in dress, yet it is an ideal. There was no ideal in Ned Marks.

===Chinese===
这种卑劣、冷嘲热讽的十九世纪流氓哲学只知卑鄙，毫无理想可言。就连做女帽的女工也有个理想，她追求的是色彩、形状、效果；虽只在穿戴一道，到底也算一种理想。而内德·马克斯（Ned Marks）心中却毫无理想。

===Original===
They called him from within to take his turn with the cue; he did not answer, he was so absorbed in his calculations. He was clever—in a way; he had quite sufficient penetration to see that this was no common girl. She was not beautiful—yet, she was not even pretty, and so plainly dressed; still there was something marked in her features. And she was with old Iden.

===Chinese===
里面有人喊他上场打球；他没有应声，他正沉浸在自己的盘算中不可自拔。他聪明——从某种意义上说；他的洞察力足以看出这不是个寻常姑娘。她算不上漂亮——甚至说不上标致，衣着又是那样朴素；但她面容上确实有种惹眼的东西。而且她跟老艾登在一起。

===Original===
Amaryllis did not understand the meaning of his glance, but she felt that it was an insult. She looked down quickly, seized her grandfather's arm, and drew him out from the pavement into the street, yielding a little to the current and so hoping to presently pass it.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯并不明白他那目光的含义，但她觉得那是一种侮辱。她迅速低下头，一把抓住祖父的胳膊，将他从人行道拉到街面上，稍稍顺着人流让了一步，指望这样就能很快挤过去。

===Original===
By this time, as Ned Marks did not answer, his companions had come to the window to discover what he was staring at. "Oho!" they laughed. "It's Miss Iden. Twenty thousand guineas in the iron box!"

===Chinese===
内德·马克斯没有应答，他的同伴们此时都凑到窗前来看他到底在看什么。"嗬！"他们哄笑起来，"是艾登小姐。铁箱子里装着两万几尼（guineas）呢！"

===Original===
Iden's great white hat, which always seemed to sit loosely on his head, was knocked aside by the elbow of a burly butcher struggling in the throng; Amaryllis replaced it upright, and leading him this way, and pushing him that, got at last to the opposite pavement, and so behind the row of booths, between them and the houses where there was less crush. Taking care of him, she forgot to look to her feet and stepped in the gutter where there was a puddle. The cold water came through the crack in her boot.

===Chinese===
艾登那顶大白帽子，戴在头上向来松松垮垮的，被人群中一个壮硕屠夫的胳膊肘碰歪了；阿玛丽莉斯替他扶正了帽子，领着他这边走、那边推，总算到了对面人行道，又绕到摊位后面，夹在摊位和房屋之间，这里人没那么挤了。她一心照顾着他，忘了留神脚下，一脚踩进了排水沟的水坑里。冰冷的水从靴子的裂缝里渗了进来。

===Original===
While these incidents were still further irritating her, the old Pacha kept mumbling and muttering to himself, nodding his head and smiling at each fresh mark of attention, for though he was so independent and fearless still he appreciated the trouble she took. The mumbling in his mouth was a sort of purring. Her dutiful spirit had stroked him up to a pleasant state of electric glow; she felt like a hound in a leash, ready to burst the bond that held her to his hand. Side by side, and arm in arm, neither of them understood the other; ninety and sixteen, a strange couple in the jostling fair.

===Chinese===
这些事还在令她愈发恼火的时候，那位老帕夏（Pasha）却一直嘟嘟囔囔地自言自语，每受到一次新的关照便点着头微笑——虽然他那么独立无畏，却也知道感念她的一番辛苦。他嘴里那含混不清的嘟囔，倒像猫儿打呼噜。她那孝顺的心已经把他抚摸得心花怒放、通体舒泰了；她却觉得自己像一条拴着皮带的猎犬，随时要挣脱系在主人手上的羁绊。肩并肩、臂挽臂，两个人谁也不了解谁；九十岁与十六岁，在推推搡搡的集市上，成了多么奇特的一对。

===Original===
Iden turned down a passage near the end of the street, and in an instant the roar of the crowd which had boomed all round them was shut off by high walls up which it rose and hummed over their heads in the air. They walked on broad stone flags notched here and there at the edges, for the rest worn smooth by footsteps (the grave drives such a trade) like Iden's doorstep, they were in fact tombstones, and the walled passage brought them to the porch of the Abbey church.

===Chinese===
艾登拐进了街道尽头附近的一条小巷，刹那间，四面八方汹涌而来的人群喧哗声被高墙挡住了，声音只在墙头升腾，嗡嗡地盘旋在他们头顶的空中。脚下是宽阔的石板路面，边缘这里那里磕出了缺口，其余部分被脚步磨得溜光（坟墓可是好买卖啊），跟艾登家门口的台阶一模一样——它们其实就是墓碑。这条围墙小巷把他们引到了修道院教堂的门廊前。

===Original===
There he stopped, muttering and mumbling, and wiped his forehead with his vast silk handkerchief. They were no longer incommoded by a crowd, but now and then folk came by hastening to the fair; lads with favours in their coats, and blue ribbons in their hats, girls in bright dresses, chiefly crude colours, who seemed out of accord with the heavy weight as it were of the great Abbey, the ponderous walls, the quiet gloom of the narrow space, and the shadows that lurked behind the buttresses.

===Chinese===
他在那里停了下来，嘴里嘟嘟囔囔，用他那块硕大的丝绸手帕擦了擦额头。人群不再挤得他们走不动了，但时不时仍有人匆匆赶去集市；衣襟上别着赠品彩带、帽子上系着蓝丝带的小伙子们，穿着鲜亮衣裙的姑娘们——大都是些扎眼的颜色——跟大修道院那沉甸甸的庄重、那厚重的墙壁、那狭小空间里的幽暗寂静、以及扶壁背后潜伏的阴影，似乎格格不入。

===Original===
The aged man muttered and mumbled about the porch and took Amaryllis under it, making her look up at the groining, and note the spring of the arch, which formed a sort of carved crown over them. It was a fine old porch, deep and high, in some things reminding you of the porches that are to be seen in Spain; stone made to give a pleasant shade like trees, so cut and worked as to be soft to the eye.

===Chinese===
老人在门廊边嘟囔了一阵，把阿玛丽莉斯带进门廊下面，让她抬头看穹顶的肋拱，注意那拱的起势——那就像一顶雕刻的冠冕悬在他们头顶。这门廊古朴精美，深邃高大，有些地方让人联想到西班牙的门廊；石材被雕琢成能如树木般投下宜人荫翳的样子，切割加工得对眼睛十分柔和。

===Original===
He pointed out to her the touches that rendered it so dear to those who value art in stone. He knew them, every one, the history and the dates, and the three stags' heads on a shield; there were broad folios in the smoky room at home, filled with every detail, Iden himself had subscribed forty pounds to the cost of illustrating one of them. Every scholar who visited the Abbey church, called and begged to see the baker's old books.

===Chinese===
他指点给她看那些让珍视石刻艺术的人爱不释手的细节。每一处他都烂熟于心，哪段历史、哪个年份、盾徽上那三颗鹿头——全都如数家珍；家里那间烟熏火燎的房间里有好几本大开本的册子，每处细节都记录在案，艾登本人还捐了四十英镑来资助其中一本的插图绘制。凡是来参观修道院教堂的学者，都要来拜访，央求看看这位面包师傅的老书。

===Original===
Iden rubbed his old thumb in the grooves and went outside and hoisted himself, as it were, up from his crooked S position to look at the three stags' heads on the shield on the wall; dim stags' heads that to you, or at least to me, might have been fishes, or Jove's thunderbolts, or anything.

===Chinese===
艾登用老拇指在沟槽里摩挲了一番，然后走到外面，仿佛从他那弯成S形的身架中直起了腰，去看墙盾徽上那三颗鹿头；那影影绰绰的鹿头，对你——至少对我——来说，看着像鱼也行，像朱庇特的雷霆也行，像什么都行。

===Original===
Amaryllis was left standing alone a moment in the porch, the deep shadow within behind her, the curve of the arch over, a fine setting for a portrait. She stood the more upright because of the fire and temper suppressed in her.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯独自在门廊里站了一会儿，身后是门廊深处的幽暗，头顶是拱顶的弧线——真是肖像画的绝佳背景。她站得格外挺直，因为心中憋着一股怒火和倔强。

===Original===
Just outside the human letter S—crooked S—clad in sad white-grey miller's garments, its old hat almost falling backwards off its old grey head, gazed up and pointed with its oaken cudgel at the coat of arms. Seven hundred years—the weight of seven hundred years—hung over them both in that old Abbey.

===Chinese===
在门外，那个弯成人形S——歪歪扭扭的S——裹着灰白的磨坊工服的身影，旧帽子几乎从他灰白的脑袋上向后滑落，正仰头用手中的橡木棍指着盾徽。七百年——七百年的重压——悬在那座古老修道院里他们两人头顶。

===Original===
Into that Past he was soon to disappear: she came out to the Future.

===Chinese===
他即将消逝于那片往昔之中：而她则来到了未来。

===Original===
Thence he took her to an arched door, nail-studded, in the passage wall, and giving her the key, told her to open it, and stood watching her in triumph, as if it had been the door to some immense treasury. She turned the lock, and he pushed her before him hastily, as if they must snatch so grand an opportunity.

===Chinese===
他随后带她来到巷道墙上一扇钉满铁钉的拱门前，把钥匙递给她，叫她打开，自己在一旁得意洋洋地看着，仿佛那是一扇通往巨大宝藏的大门。她转动钥匙开了锁，他急急忙忙把她推到前面进去，好像非得抓住这个绝佳的时机不可。

## XIX / 第XIX章

===Original===
So there was tribulation in three houses. Next morning she scarcely dared come in to breakfast, and opened the door timidly, expecting heavy looks, and to be snapped up if she spoke. Instead of which, on taking her place, Iden carefully cut for her the most delicate slice of ham he could find, and removed the superfluous fat before putting it on her plate. Mrs. Iden had a special jug of cream ready for her﻿—Amaryllis was fond of cream﻿—and enriched the tea with it generously.

===Chinese===
三家子都闹得鸡飞狗跳。第二天早上她几乎不敢进来吃早饭，怯生生地推开门，等着挨白眼，等着一开口就被训。谁知坐下来后，艾登仔细地给她挑了最精的一片火腿，还把多余的肥肉剔掉才放到她盘里。艾登夫人专门给她备了一壶奶油——阿玛丽莉斯好喝奶油——慷慨地往茶里倒了不少。

===Original===
“And what did you see at the fair?” asked Iden in his kindest voice, lifting up his saucer﻿—from which he always drank﻿—by putting his thumb under it instead of over, so that his thick little finger projected. He always sipped his tea in this way.

===Chinese===
“在集市上看见什么了？”艾登用最温和的嗓音问道，他端茶碟——他向来喝茶用茶碟——时把拇指搁在碟底而不是碟沿，粗短的小指翘着。他喝茶向来如此。

===Original===
“You had plenty of fun, didn’t you?” said Mrs. Iden, still more kindly.

===Chinese===
“玩得可开心了吧？”艾登夫人说得更和气了。

===Original===
“I﻿—I don’t know; I did not see much of the fair,” said Amaryllis, at a loss to understand the change of manner.

===Chinese===
“我——我不知道；我没怎么看集市，”阿玛丽莉斯说，不明白他们态度怎么变了。

===Original===
Iden smiled at his wife and nodded; Mrs. Iden picked up a letter from the tea-tray and gave it to her daughter:

===Chinese===
艾登朝妻子笑了笑，点了点头；艾登夫人从茶盘上拿起一封信递给女儿。

===Original===
“Read.”

===Chinese===
“念。”

===Original===
Amaryllis read﻿—it was from Grandfather Iden, furiously upbraiding Iden for neglecting his daughter’s education; she had no reverence, no manners﻿—an undutiful, vulgar girl; she had better not show her face in his house again till she had been taught to know her position; her conduct was not fit for the kitchen; she had not the slightest idea how to behave herself in the presence of persons of quality.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯念起来——是艾登祖父（Grandfather Iden）来的信，怒斥艾登疏于女儿的教养；她没有敬畏心，没有规矩——是个不孝顺、粗鄙的丫头；她最好别再登他的门，除非先学会了认清自己的身份；她的做派连厨房都不配待；她压根儿不知道在体面人面前该如何自处。

===Original===
She put it down before she had finished the tirade of abuse; she did not look up, her face was scarlet.

===Chinese===
还没念完那通辱骂，她就把信放下了；她没抬头，脸涨得通红。

===Original===
Iden laughed.

===Chinese===
艾登笑了。

===Original===
“Horrid old wretch! Served him right!” said Mrs. Iden. “So glad you vexed him, dear!”

===Chinese===
“讨厌的老东西！活该！”艾登夫人说，“亲爱的，真高兴你气着他了！”

===Original===
Amaryllis last night a wretch was this morning a heroine. The grandfather’s letter had done this.

===Chinese===
昨夜还是个祸害，今早倒成了英雄。全凭祖父这封信。

===Original===
Iden never complained﻿—never mentioned his father﻿—but of course in his heart he bitterly felt the harsh neglect shown towards him and his wife and their child. He was a man who said the less the more he was moved; he gossiped freely with the men at the stile, or even with a hamlet old woman. Not a word ever dropped from him of his own difficulties﻿—he kept his mind to himself. His wife knew nothing of his intentions﻿—he was over-secretive, especially about money matters, in which he affected the most profound mystery, as if everyone in Coombe was not perfectly aware they could hardly get a pound of sugar on credit.

===Chinese===
艾登从不抱怨——从不提他父亲——可心里对这冷遇自然苦楚得很。他这人越是动情越说不出话；倒是跟路口台阶上闲聊的男人们，甚至跟村里的老婆婆，东拉西扯起来没完。他自己的难处，他一个字不漏——全憋在心里。他妻子对他的盘算一无所知——他藏得太深，尤其是钱的事，他故弄玄虚得不得了，好像库姆（Coombe）人还不知道他们连一磅糖都赊不来似的。

===Original===
All the more bitterly he resented the manner in which Grandfather Iden treated him, giving away half-crowns, crown-pieces, shillings, and fourpenny bits to anyone who would flatter his peculiarities, leaving his own descendants to struggle daily with debt and insult.

===Chinese===
艾登祖父对他的态度，他越发愤恨不平——那老头子动不动就把半克朗、克朗、先令和四便士赏给那些奉承他怪脾气的人，却让自家子孙天天在债务和屈辱里挣扎。

===Original===
Iden was in reality a very proud man, and the insults of his petty creditors fretted him.

===Chinese===
艾登骨子里是个很骄傲的人，那些小债主的欺辱让他窝火。

===Original===
He would have been glad if Amaryllis had become her grandfather’s favourite; as the grandfather had thrown savage words at the girl, so much the more was added to the score against the grandfather.

===Chinese===
要是阿玛丽莉斯能讨祖父欢心，他本是高兴的；可祖父既然对这丫头恶语相向，那笔账就更得记在老头子头上了。

===Original===
Mrs. Iden hated the grandfather with every drop of Flamma blood in her veins﻿—hated him above all for his pseudo-Flamma relationship, for old Iden had in his youth been connected with the Flammas in business﻿—hated him for his veneration of the aristocratic and medieval Pamments.

===Chinese===
艾登夫人恨透了祖父，血管里弗拉玛（Flamma）家族的血每一滴都在恨——尤其恨他那层虚情假意的弗拉玛关系，因为老艾登年轻时跟弗拉玛家有过生意往来——恨他对贵族气十足、中世纪派头的帕门特（Pamments）家的顶礼膜拜。

===Original===
She was always impressing upon Amaryllis the necessity of cultivating her grandfather’s goodwill, and always abusing him﻿—contradicting herself in the most natural manner.

===Chinese===
她总教导阿玛丽莉斯要讨祖父欢心的重要性，又总骂他——自相矛盾得再自然不过。

===Original===
This letter had given them such delight, because it showed how deeply Amaryllis had annoyed the old gentleman. Had he been whipped he could hardly have yelled more; he screamed through his scratchy quill. Suppose they did lose his money, he had had *one* good upset, that was something.

===Chinese===
这封信让他们大为开心，因为它说明阿玛丽莉斯把老头子气得够呛。就算挨了鞭子，他大概也叫不出这么大声；他那支刮纸的破笔简直是声嘶力竭。就算真丢了他那笔钱，好歹让他痛痛快快气了一回，也算值了。

===Original===
They were eager to hear all about it. Amaryllis was at first very shy to tell, knowing that her father was a thick Tory and an upholder of the Pamments, and fearing his displeasure. But for various reasons both father and mother grew warmer in delight at every fresh incident of her story.

===Chinese===
他们急着听她讲经过。阿玛丽莉斯起初很羞怯，不敢说，知道父亲是个死硬的托利党人（Tory）和帕门特家的拥护者，怕惹他不高兴。可出于种种原因，父母越听越高兴，她讲的每件新鲜事都让他们愈发得意。

===Original===
Mrs. Flamma Iden﻿—revolutionary Flamma﻿—detested the Pamments enthusiastically, on principle first, and next, because the grandfather paid them such court.

===Chinese===
弗拉玛·艾登夫人——革命派的弗拉玛——对帕门特家深恶痛绝，首先是出于原则，其次是因为祖父对他们那般谄媚。

===Original===
Iden was indeed an extra thick Tory, quite opaque, and had voted in the Pamment interest these thirty years, yet he had his secret reasons for disliking them personally.

===Chinese===
艾登确实是个铁杆托利党，铁得透不过气来，三十年来一直投帕门特家的票，可私下里他有不喜欢他们的理由。

===Original===
Both Mr. and Mrs. Iden agreed in their scorn of the grandfather’s pottering about the grounds and in and out the conservatories, as if that was the highest honour on earth. Yet Mrs. Iden used often to accuse her husband of a desire to do the very same thing: “You’re just as stupid,” she would say; “you’d think it wonderful to have a private key﻿—you’re every bit as silly really, only you haven’t got the chance.”

===Chinese===
艾登夫妇都鄙夷祖父在庭院里晃悠、在暖房里进进出出的做派，好像那是天大的荣耀似的。可艾登夫人又常指责丈夫也想干同样的事："你一样蠢，"她说，"你会觉得有把私用钥匙了不起——其实你跟他一样傻，只不过你没那个机会罢了。"

===Original===
However, from a variety of causes they agreed in looking on Amaryllis’ disgrace as a high triumph and glory.

===Chinese===
不过出于种种原因，他们一致认为阿玛丽莉斯的丢脸是一大胜利和荣光。

===Original===
So she was petted all the morning by both parties﻿—a rare thing﻿—and in the afternoon Iden gave her the sovereign she had brought home, to buy her some new boots, and to spend the rest as she chose on herself.

===Chinese===
于是她一上午被两边捧着疼着——难得的殊荣——下午艾登把她带回来的那枚金币给她，让她买双新靴子，剩下的随她自己花。

===Original===
Away went Amaryllis to the town, happy and yet not without regret that she had increased the disagreement between her father and grandfather. She met the vans and gipsies slowly leaving the site of the fair, the children running along with bare brown feet. She went under the archaeologically interesting gateway, and knocked at the door of Tiras Wise, shoemaker, “established 200 years.”

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯快快乐乐地进城去了，心里却并非没有一丝遗憾——她毕竟加深了父亲和祖父的嫌隙。她遇见大篷车和吉普赛人正慢慢离开集市旧址，孩子们光着褐色的小脚跑来跑去。她走过一座有考古价值的拱门，敲响了蒂拉斯·怀斯（Tiras Wise）鞋匠铺的门，门上写着"创立二百年"。

===Original===
Tiras Wise of the present generation was thin and nervous, weary of the centuries, worn out, and miserable-looking. Amaryllis, strong in the possession of a golden sovereign, attacked him sharply for his perfidious promises; her boots promised at Christmas were not mended yet.

===Chinese===
这一代的蒂拉斯·怀斯瘦骨嶙峋、神经兮兮，像是被两百年磨得精疲力竭，一副愁眉苦脸的样子。阿玛丽莉斯手握金币，底气十足，毫不客气地质问他为何食言——圣诞节答应补的靴子到现在还没补好。

===Original===
Tiras, twiddling a lady’s boot in one hand, and his foot measure in the other, very humbly and deprecatingly excused himself; there had been so much trouble with the workmen, some were so tipsy, and some would not work; they were always demanding higher wages, and just as he had a job in hand going off and leaving it half finished﻿—shoemaker’s tricks these. Sometimes, indeed, he could not get a workman, and then there was the competition of the ready-made boot from Northampton; really, it was most trying﻿—it really was.

===Chinese===
蒂拉斯一手转着一只女靴，一手握着脚尺，谦卑而惶恐地为自己辩解；工人们太难伺候了，有的喝得醉醺醺，有的干脆不肯干活；他们老嚷着要加薪，好不容易接了活儿，干到一半撂挑子走人——鞋匠的伎俩就是如此。有时候确实雇不到工人，还得跟北安普顿（Northampton）来的成品靴竞争；真的是太难了——真的。

===Original===
“Well, and when am I going to have the boots?” said Amaryllis, amused at the poor fellow’s distress. “When *are* they going to be finished?”

===Chinese===
“那我到底什么时候能拿到靴子？”阿玛丽莉斯问，被这可怜虫的狼狈样子逗乐了。“*到底*什么时候能做好？”

===Original===
“You see, Miss Iden,” said the shoemaker’s mother, coming to help her son, “the fact is, he’s just worried out of his life with his men﻿—and really﻿—”

===Chinese===
“您看，艾登小姐，”鞋匠的母亲过来帮儿子说话，“实在是，他被那些工人愁得要死——真的——”

===Original===
“You don’t seem to get on very well with your shoemaking, Mr. Wise,” said the customer, smiling.

===Chinese===
“怀斯先生，你这鞋匠生意似乎不太顺当啊，”顾客笑着说。

===Original===
“The fact is,” said poor Wise, in his most melancholy manner, with a deep sigh, “the fact is, the men don’t know their work as they used to, they spoil the leather and cut it wrong, and leave jobs half done, and they’re always drinking; the leather isn’t so good as it used to be; the fact is,” with a still deeper sigh, “*we can’t make a boot*.”

===Chinese===
“事实是，”可怜的怀斯一脸愁苦，深深叹了口气，“事实是，工人们手艺不如从前了，他们糟蹋皮料，裁剪错误，活儿干一半丢下，而且天天喝酒；皮料也不如从前了；事实是，”又一声更深的叹息，"*我们连一双靴子都做不出来*。"

===Original===
At which Amaryllis laughed outright, to think that people should have been in business two hundred years as shoemakers, and yet could not make a boot!

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯听了哈哈大笑，想想这些人做鞋做了两百年，竟然做不出一双靴子！

===Original===
Her experience of life as yet was short, and she saw things in their first aspect; it is not till much later we observe that the longer people do one thing, the worse they do it, till in the end they cannot do it at all.

===Chinese===
她阅历尚浅，看事物只看表面；要到很久以后我们才会发现，人们做一件事做得越久，就做得越差，到头来干脆做不了。

===Original===
She presently selected a pair for herself, 9s., and another pair for her mother, 10s. 6d., leaving sixpence over; add sixpence discount for ready-money, and she was still rich with a shilling. Carrying the parcel, she went up the street and passed old Iden’s door on elate instep, happy that she had not got to cross his threshold that day, happy to think she had the boots for her mother. Looking in at two or three dingy little shops, she fixed at last on one, and bought half-a-dozen of the very finest mild bloaters, of which Mrs. Iden was so fond. This finished the savings, and she turned quickly for home. The bloaters being merely bound round with one thin sheet of newspaper, soon imparted their odour to her hand.

===Chinese===
她很快给自己挑了一双，九先令，又给母亲挑了一双，十先令六便士，还剩六便士；再加上现金付账的六便士折扣，她还剩一先令，算是富余了。她拎着包裹走上街，趾高气扬地从老艾登家门口走过——庆幸那天不用跨他的门槛，庆幸想着给母亲买了靴子。逛了两三家昏暗的小铺子，她最终选定一家，买了半打顶好的温和熏鲱鱼——艾登夫人最爱这个。这下积蓄花光了，她赶紧转身回家。熏鲱鱼只用一张薄报纸裹着，很快就染得她满手腥味。

===Original===
A lady whose hand smells of bloaters is not, I hope, too ideal; I hope you will see now that I am not imaginative, or given to the heroinesque. Amaryllis, I can tell you, was quite absorbed in the bloaters and the boots; a very sweet, true, and loving hand it was, in spite of the bloaters﻿—one to kiss fervently.

===Chinese===
一位手上有熏鲱鱼味的小姐，我想还不至于太理想化；我希望你现在看得出来，我不是个耽于想象的人，也不爱塑造什么女英雄。阿玛丽莉斯，我告诉你，当时满脑子想的都是熏鲱鱼和靴子；尽管沾着鱼腥味，那只手却是极甜美、极真切、极温柔的——一只值得热切亲吻的手。

===Original===
They soon had the bloaters on over a clear fire of wood-coals, and while they cooked the mother tried her new boots, naturally not a little pleased with the thoughtful present. The Flamma blood surged with gratitude; she would have given her girl the world at that moment. That she should have remembered her mother showed such a good disposition; there was no one like Amaryllis.

===Chinese===
她们很快把熏鲱鱼架在明净的木炭火上烤，母亲趁烤鱼的工夫试新靴子，对女儿这份体贴的礼物自然满心欢喜。弗拉玛家族的血液涌动着感激；那一刻她愿意把全世界都给女儿。她能惦记着母亲，可见心地多好；没人比得上阿玛丽莉斯。

===Original===
“Pah!” said Iden, just then entering, “pah!” with a gasp; and holding his handkerchief to his nose, he rushed out faster than he came in, for the smell of bloaters was the pestilence to him.

===Chinese===
“呸！”艾登这时走进来，"呸！"地倒抽一口气；他拿手帕捂住鼻子，冲出去比进来时还快，因为熏鲱鱼的气味在他看来简直是瘟疫。

===Original===
They only laughed all the merrier over their supper.

===Chinese===
她们晚饭时笑得更欢了。

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===
Within there was a gravel path, and glimpses between trees of wide pleasure-grounds. Amaryllis hesitated, and looked back; Iden drew her forward, not noticing her evident disinclination to proceed. If he had, he would have put it down to awe, instead of which it was dislike.
For she guessed they were entering the lawns in front of the Hon. Raleigh Pamment's mansion. He was the largest owner of town and country; the streets, the marketplace, the open spaces, in which the fair was being held, belonged to him; so did most of the farms and hamlets out of which the people had come. The Pamments were Tories; very important Tories indeed.
The Idens, in their little way, were Tories, too, right to the centre of the cerebellum; the Flammas were hot Republicans. Now Amaryllis, being a girl, naturally loved her father most, yet she was a wilful and rebellious revolutionist. Amaryllis, who would not be a Flamma, had imbibed all the Flamma hatred of authority from her mother.

===Chinese===
里头是一条碎石小径，树木掩映间依稀可见宽阔的游乐花园。阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）犹豫了，回头望了一眼；艾登（Iden）拉着她往前走，并未注意到她脸上明显的不情愿。即便注意到了，他也会以为那是敬畏，殊不知那是厌恶。
她猜出他们正走向尊敬的拉利·帕门特（Raleigh Pamment）宅邸前的草坪。拉利是这座城乡最大的地主；街道、集市、举办集市的开阔场地，全都属于他；人们赶来的那些农场和小村落，也多半是他的产业。帕门特家族是托利党人；地位举足轻重的托利党人。
艾登家论起自己的那点门第，也是根正苗红的托利党人，从头到脚、骨子里都是；弗拉玛家（Flammas）则是狂热的共和党人。阿玛丽莉斯虽是个女孩子，自然最爱自己的父亲，可她偏偏是个任性的、叛逆的革命者。阿玛丽莉斯不愿做弗拉玛家的人，却从母亲那里继承了弗拉玛家对一切权威的仇恨。

===Original===
To her the Pamments were the incarnation of everything detestable, of oppression, obstruction, and medieval darkness. She knew nothing of politics; at sixteen you do not need to know to feel vehemently, you feel vehemently without knowing. Still, she had heard a good deal about the Pamments.
She resented being brought there to admire the pleasure grounds and mansion, and to kowtow to the grandeur of these medieval tyrants.

===Chinese===
在她看来，帕门特家就是一切可憎之物的化身——压迫、阻挠、中世纪的黑暗。她对政治一窍不通；十六岁的人不需要懂什么就能感受到强烈的愤慨，你没来由地就愤愤不平了。不过，关于帕门特家的事，她倒是听过不少。
她恼恨被带到这儿来，看什么花园和宅邸，向这些中世纪暴君的威势卑躬屈膝。

===Original===
Old Iden led her on till they came to the smooth lawn before the front windows; three centuries of mowing had made it as smooth as the top of his own head, where the years had mown away merrily.
There was not so much as a shrub—not a daisy—between them and the great windows of the house. They stood in full view.
Amaryllis could scarcely endure herself, so keen was her vexation; her cheeks reddened. She was obliged to face the house, but her glance was downwards; she would not look at it.

===Chinese===
老艾登领着她往前走，直到他们来到正前方大窗前那片平整的草坪上；三百年不断的修剪，把这片草地弄得跟他自己的头顶一样光滑——岁月也在那儿欢快地薅去了不少。
他们与宅邸巨大的窗户之间，连一丛灌木都没有，连一朵雏菊都没有。他们完完全全暴露在窗前。
阿玛丽莉斯几乎受不了自己了，恼怒来得如此猛烈；她的双颊涨红了。她不得不面朝宅邸，目光却朝下看；她偏不看它。

===Original===
Grandfather Iden was in the height of his glory. In all Woolhorton town there was not another man who could do as he was doing at that moment.
The Pamments were very exclusive people, exceptionally exclusive even for high class Tories. Their gardens, and lawns, and grounds were jealously surrounded with walls higher than the old-fashioned houses of the street beneath them. No one dared to so much as peer through a crevice of the mighty gates. Their persons were encircled with the "divinity" that hedges the omnipotent landed proprietor. No one dared speak to a Pamment. They acknowledged no one in the town, not even the solicitors, not even the clergyman of the Abbey church; that was on account of ritual differences.

===Chinese===
艾登爷爷此刻正处于他一生荣耀的顶峰。整个伍尔霍顿镇（Woolhorton），没有第二个人能像他此刻这般行事。
帕门特一家是极其排外的人，即便对于上层托利党来说也算格外排外。他们的花园、草坪和地界，被高墙严严实实地围了起来，那墙比街面下方那些老式房屋还要高。谁也不敢透过那巍峨大门的缝隙窥探一眼。他们身上笼罩着"神圣光环"——那是全能的大地主头上的光晕。谁也不敢跟帕门特家的人说话。他们在镇上不认任何人，连律师都不认，连修道院教堂的牧师也不认；那是因为礼拜仪式上的分歧。

===Original===
It was, indeed, whispered—high treason must always be whispered—that young Pamment, the son and heir, was by no means so exclusive, and had been known to be effusive towards ladies of low birth—and manners.
The great leaders of Greece—Alcibiades, Aristides, and so on—threw open their orchards to the people. Everyone walked in and did as he chose. These great leaders of England—the Pamments—shut up their lawns and pleasure-grounds, sealed them hermetically, you could hardly throw a stone over the walls if you tried.
But Grandfather Iden walked through those walls as if there were none; he alone of all Woolhorton town and country.

===Chinese===
事实上，人们私下里嘀咕——大逆不道的话总得悄悄说——小帕门特，也就是那个继承人、少东家，可没他父母那么排外；据说他对出身低微的女士们——和她们的举止——倒相当热情。
古希腊的伟大领袖——阿尔西比亚德斯（Alcibiades）、阿里斯提德斯（Aristides）之流——向百姓敞开果园。人人可以自由进出，随心所欲。而英格兰的伟大领袖们——帕门特家——把草坪和游乐花园关得严严实实，封得铁桶一般，你就是想扔块石头过墙都扔不过去。
可是艾登爷爷穿过那些高墙，就跟没墙似的；整个伍尔霍顿城乡，唯独他一人如此。

===Original===
In that gossipy little town, of course, there were endless surmises as to the why and wherefore of that private key. Shrewd people said—"Ah! you may depend they be getting summat out of him. Lent 'em some of his guineas, a' reckon. They be getting summat out of him. Hoss-leeches, they gentlefolks."
Grandfather Iden alone entered when he listed: he wandered about the lawns, he looked in at the conservatories, he took a bunch of grapes if it pleased him, or a bouquet of flowers; he actually stepped indoors occasionally and sat down on the carved old chairs, or pottered about the picture gallery. He had a private key to the nail-studded door in the wall by the Abbey church, and he looked upon that key very much as if it had been the key of Paradise.

===Chinese===
在那个爱嚼舌根的小镇上，关于那把私人钥匙的来龙去脉，自然有无穷无尽的猜测。精明人说——"呵！你瞧好了，他们准是从他那儿捞了什么好处。我估摸着，借了他几块金几尼吧。他们从他那儿捞了好处。那些上等人，全是吸血马蟥。"
