This woke up Iden for the moment. First there was a rummaging about in his old bureau, and a laborious writing of letters, or adding up of figures. Next there was a great personal getting up, a bath, clean linen, shaving, and donning of clothes packed away these years past. In two hours or so Iden came down another man, astonishingly changed, quite a gentleman in every respect, and so handsome in Amaryllis's eyes. Indeed, he was really handsome still, and to her, of course, wonderfully so. If only he would always dress like that!

===Chinese===
这一下倒是把艾登暂时惊醒了。先是翻箱倒柜地翻他那张旧写字台，费力地写信、算账。接着是一番大张旗鼓的梳洗打扮——洗澡、换上干净的衬衣、刮胡子、穿上压在箱底好几年的衣服。大约两个小时后，艾登下楼时判若两人，变化之大令人惊叹，方方面面都像个绅士，在阿玛丽莉斯眼中简直英俊极了。确实，他本来就很英俊，在她眼中当然更是如此。要是他总能这样穿戴该多好！

===Original===
Iden walked into Woolhorton, but all these preparations had so consumed the time that the bank was shut, the solicitor's offices closed, and there was no means of raising any money that evening. The son passed the father's doorstep—the worn stone step, ground by the generations of customers—he saw the light behind the blind in the little room where Grandfather Iden sat—he might, had he paused and listened, have heard the old man poke the fire, the twenty-thousand-guinea-man—the son passed on, and continued his lonely walk home, the home that held a bailiff.

===Chinese===
艾登步行去了伍尔霍顿，但这一番准备耗尽了时间，银行已经关门，律师事务所也下班了，当晚再无任何途径筹到钱。他经过父亲家门口——那块被几代顾客的脚磨凹了的石阶——看见小屋里百叶窗后透出灯光，艾登爷爷（Grandfather Iden）正坐在里面——他若是停下脚步细听，也许能听见那位坐拥两万基尼的老人拨弄炉火的声音——但他继续往前走了，继续他孤独的归途，回到那个驻着法警的家。

===Original===
A makeshift bed had to be made up for the bailiff in the kitchen, and there he remained the night, and was up and had lit the fire for Luce the servant before she was down. The man was certainly very civil, but still there was the shock of it.

===Chinese===
厨房里不得不给法警搭了一张临时床铺，他在那里过了一夜，第二天女仆露丝（Luce）还没起床他就起来了，替她生好了炉火。这人确实很客气，但那冲击毕竟还在。

===Original===
Early in the morning Iden went into town again, saw his solicitor, and got a cheque—it was only five-and-twenty or thirty pounds, and the bailiff left.

===Chinese===
一大清早，艾登又进了城，找到他的律师，拿到了一张支票——不过二三十英镑，法警便走了。

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===
But his presence did not die out of the kitchen; they always seemed to feel as if he had been there. The hearth had been stained by a foreign foot, the very poker had been touched by a foreign hand, the rude form at the side by the wall had been occupied by an intruder. Amaryllis had always been so fond of the kitchen—the oldest part of the house, two centuries at least. The wide hearth and immense chimney, up which, when the fire was out, of a winter's night you could see the stars; over which of a windy night you could imagine the witches riding by, borne on the deep howling of the blast; the great beam and the gun slung to it; the heavy oaken table, unpolished, greyish oak; the window in the thick wall, set with yellowish glass; the stone floor, and the walls from which the whitewash peeled in flakes; the rude old place was very dear to her.

===Chinese===
可那人的痕迹并没有从厨房里消失；他们总觉得他仿佛还在这里。壁炉被一双异乡的脚踩过，连火钳都被一双异乡的手碰过，墙边那张粗笨的长凳也被一个外人坐过。阿玛丽莉斯一直很喜欢这间厨房——这是宅子里最古老的部分，至少有两百年了。宽大的壁炉和巨大的烟囱，炉火熄灭的冬夜，你能透过烟囱看到天上的星星；刮风的夜里，你能想象女巫骑着扫帚从烟囱上方飞过，在狂风的怒号中穿行；粗大的横梁上挂着一支枪；沉重的橡木桌子，未经打磨，灰白色的橡木；厚墙里的窗户，嵌着泛黄的玻璃；石板地面，墙上的白灰一层层剥落；这个粗陋的老地方对她无比珍贵。

===Original===
Ofttimes they sat there in winter instead of the sitting-room, drawn by its antique homeliness. Mrs. Iden warmed elder wine, and Iden his great cup of Goliath ale, and they roasted chestnuts and apples, while the potatoes—large potatoes—Iden's selected specialities—were baking buried in the ashes. Looking over her shoulder Amaryllis could see the white drift of snow against the window, which was on a level with the ground outside, and so got Iden to tell her stories of the deep snow in the United States, and the thick ice, sawn with saws, or, his fancy roaming on, of the broad and beautiful Hudson River, the river he had so admired in his youth, the river the poets will sing some day; or of his clinging aloft at night in the gale on the banks of Newfoundland, for he had done duty as a sailor. A bold and adventurous man in his youth, why did he gossip at the stile now in his full and prime of manhood?

===Chinese===
冬天他们常在这里而不是在客厅里坐坐，被它古旧而温馨的气息吸引。艾登太太温着接骨木酒，艾登端着他的大号哥利亚（Goliath）麦芽酒杯，他们烤栗子和苹果，而那些大个儿土豆——艾登精心挑选的上等品种——埋在灰烬里烘烤。阿玛丽莉斯回头就能看见窗外堆积的白雪——窗户与室外的地面齐平——于是她就缠着艾登讲美国的深雪、厚冰，用锯子锯冰的故事，或者他思绪飞扬时，就讲宽阔美丽的哈德逊河（Hudson River），那条他年轻时如此倾慕的河，那条终有一天诗人会为之歌唱的河；或者讲他年轻时在纽芬兰（Newfoundland）海岸的暴风雨之夜悬在半空中的经历，因为他曾经当过水手。年轻时一个勇敢而富有冒险精神的人，为什么在他盛年鼎盛之时，却只能在路阶旁闲聊打发日子？

===Original===
It would be a long, long tale to tell, and even then only those who have lived in the country and had practical experience could fully comprehend the hopelessness of working a small farm, unless you are of a wholly sordid nature. Iden's nature was not sordid; the very reverse. The beginning, or one of the beginnings, of the quarrel between father and son arose because of this; Grandfather Iden could not forgive his son for making the place beautiful with trees and flowers.

===Chinese===
这话说来就长了，而且即便说了，也只有那些在乡下生活过、有实际经验的人才能真正理解经营一个小农场的绝望——除非你是那种完全唯利是图的人。艾登的天性并不唯利是图；恰恰相反。父子之间那场争吵的起因——或者说起因之一——就是因为这个；艾登爷爷无法原谅儿子用树木和花草把这地方弄得那么漂亮。

===Original===
By-and-by the baked potatoes were done, and they had supper on the old and clumsy table, village made and unpolished, except in so far as the stains of cooking operations had varnished it, the same table at which "Jearje," the fogger, sat every morning to eat his breakfast, and every evening to take his supper. What matter? George worked hard and honestly all day, his great arms on the table, spread abroad as he ate, did not injure it.
Great mealy potatoes, cracked open, white as the snow without, floury and smoking; dabs of Mrs. Iden's delicious butter, a little salt and pepper, and there was a dish for a king. The very skins were pleasant—just a taste.

===Chinese===
烤土豆渐渐好了，他们在那张又旧又笨的桌子上吃晚饭——村里做的，没有抛光，只有做饭留下的油渍权当给它刷了一层漆。每天早上"杰吉"（Jearje）——就是那个巡夜工——都在这张桌旁吃早饭，每天晚上也在这儿吃晚饭。有什么关系？乔治（George）一整天勤勤恳恳地干活，他那双粗壮的大臂搁在桌上，吃饭时摊得老开，也不会伤到它。
粉质的大土豆掰开来，白得像外面的雪，松软粉糯、热气腾腾；抹上一点艾登太太的美味黄油，撒点盐和胡椒，那就是一道帝王级的佳肴。就连土豆皮都好吃——尝一口就知道。

===Original===
They were not always alone at these kitchen-feasts, sometimes a Flamma from London, sometimes an Iden from over the hill, or others were there. Iden was very hospitable—though most of his guests (family connections) were idle folk, no good to themselves or anybody, still they were made cordially welcome. But others, very high folk, socially speaking (for they had good connections, too, these poor Idens), who had dined at grand London tables, seemed to enjoy themselves most thoroughly on the rude Homeric fare.
For it was genuine, and there was a breadth, an openhanded generosity, a sense of reality about it; something really to eat, though no finger-glasses; Homeric straightforwardness of purpose.

===Chinese===
厨房里的盛宴并非总是他们一家独享，有时从伦敦来的弗拉玛家的人，有时从山那边来的艾登家的人，或别的客人也在场。艾登非常好客——尽管他的客人大多（亲戚关系）都是些游手好闲的人，对自己对别人都没用，但还是受到热情款待。还有一些在社交意义上身份很高的人（这些穷艾登家也有体面的人脉），他们在伦敦的大宴席上用过餐，却似乎在这粗犷的荷马式饭菜面前吃得最为尽兴。
因为这是真正实在的东西，有一股坦荡的慷慨气度，一种实实在在的感觉；有真正可吃的东西，虽然没有洗指碗；荷马式的直率和纯粹。

===Original===
Amaryllis was very fond of the old kitchen; it was the very centre of home. This strange man, this intruding bailiff, trod heavily on her dearest emotions. His shadow remained on the wall though he had gone.
They all felt it, but Amaryllis most of all, and it was weeks before the kitchen seemed to resume its former appearance. Jearje was the one who restored it. He ate so heartily, and spoke so cheerily at breakfast and at supper, it almost made them forget their troubles to see anyone so grateful and pleased with all they did for him. "Thank you, ma'am; dest about a good bit a' bacon, this yer"—locally the *d* and *j* were often interchangable, dest for jest, or just—"That'll be a' plenty for I, ma'am, doan't want more'n I can yet"—don't want more than I can eat, don't want to be greedy—"Thank you, miss; dest about some ripping good ale, this yer; that it be."

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯一直非常喜欢这间旧厨房；它是家的中心。这个陌生的男人，这个闯进来的管家，沉重地踩在了她最珍贵的感情上。他虽然走了，他的影子却还留在墙上。
他们都感受到了，但阿玛丽莉斯感受最深，过了好几个星期厨房才似乎恢复了旧日的模样。把它恢复原样的人是杰吉。他吃饭那么香，说话那么爽快，早饭晚饭都是如此，看到有人对他们为他做的一切如此感激和满足，几乎让他们忘了自己的烦恼。"多谢了，太太；这块熏肉挺好，正合适"——在当地，*d*和*j*经常互换，"dest"就是"jest"（just）的意思——"这就够我吃的了，太太，不想贪多"——不想吃得比需要多，不想贪心——"多谢了，小姐；这啤酒真是够劲儿，没错。"

===Original===
He so thoroughly enjoyed and appreciated the bacon, and the cheese, and the ale; he was like a great, big human dog; you know how we like to see a big dog wag his tail at his food, or put his paws on our knees and laugh, as it were, with his eyes in our face. They petted him, these two women, exactly as if he had been a dog, giving him all the bones, literally and metaphorically, the actual bones of the meat, and any scraps there were, to take home with him (besides his regular meals), and now and then some trifles of clothing for his aged mother. The dog most thoroughly appreciated this treatment; he rolled in it, revelled in it, grew shiny and fat, and glistened with happiness.

===Chinese===
他如此真心实意地享受和赞美熏肉、奶酪和麦芽酒；他就像一条大大的、憨厚的人形大狗；你知道我们多喜欢看一条大狗在食物面前摇尾巴，或者把爪子搭在我们膝盖上，冲着我们的脸咧嘴笑，仿佛在用眼睛跟我们说话一样。这两个女人宠他，就像宠一条狗一样，把所有的骨头——名副其实和比喻意义上的骨头——真正的肉骨头和所有剩菜剩饭——都给他带回家（除了他的正餐之外），还不时给他年迈的母亲带些零碎衣物。这条"狗"极其领情；他在这种宠爱里打滚、沉醉，长得油光水滑、肥肥胖胖，浑身洋溢着幸福。

===Original===
Iden petted him, too, to some degree, out of doors, and for much the same reason; his cheery content and willingness, and the absence of the usual selfish niggardliness of effort. George worked willingly and fairly, and, if occasion needed, stayed another hour, or put his shoulder to the wheel of his own accord, and so, having a good employer, and not one minded to take advantage of him, was rewarded in many ways. Iden did not reduce his wages by a shilling or eighteenpence in winter, and gave him wood for firing, half a sack of potatoes, garden produce, or apples, and various other things from time to time.
Living partly indoors, and being of this disposition, Jearje was more like a retainer than a servant, or labourer; a humble member of the family.

===Chinese===
艾登在户外也一定程度上宠他，原因大同小异；因为他那种快活的满足和勤快，以及平日里少见的毫不自私的卖力。乔治干活痛快又公道，需要的时候，他会自己主动多干一小时，或者主动把肩膀顶上车轮——有了这么个好雇主，又不是那种想占他便宜的人，他在很多方面都得到了回报。艾登冬天从不少他一先令或十八便士的工资，还给他柴火、半袋土豆、菜园里的蔬菜或苹果，不时还送些别的东西。
由于部分时间住在主人家里，加上这种性格，杰吉与其说是个仆人或雇工，不如说更像一个家臣；一个卑微的家庭成员。

===Original===
It was a sight to see him eat. Amaryllis and Mrs. Iden used often to watch him covertly, just for the amusement it gave them. He went about it as steadily and deliberately as the horses go to plough; no attempt to caracole in the furrow, ready to stand still as long as you like.
Bacon three inches thick with fat: the fat of beef; fat of mutton—anything they could not finish in the sitting-room; the overplus of cabbage or potatoes, savoury or unsavoury; vast slices of bread and cheese; ale, and any number of slop-basins full of tea—the cups were not large enough—and pudding, cold dumpling, hard as wood, no matter what, Jearje ate steadily through it.

===Chinese===
看他吃饭是一种享受。阿玛丽莉斯和艾登太太常常偷偷地看他，光是为了那份乐趣。他吃饭的样子稳稳当当、不慌不忙，就像马儿犁地一样；绝不试图在犁沟里蹦跶，你让他停多久他就停多久。
三英寸厚的肥膘熏肉：牛肉的脂肪；羊肉的脂肪——客厅里吃剩的任何东西；多出来的卷心菜或土豆，好吃的难吃的都行；大块的面包和奶酪；麦芽酒，还有数不清的满满一盆又一盆的茶——杯子太小不够装——布丁、冷饺子，硬得像木头，管它是什么，杰吉都稳稳当当地吃下去。

===Original===
A more willing fellow never lived; if Mrs. Iden happened to want anything from the town ever so late, though George had worked hard the long day through from half-past five in the morning, off he would start, without sign of demur, five miles there and back, and come in singing with his burden.
There are such, as George still among the labourer class, in despite of the change of circumstance and sentiment, men who would be as faithful as the faithfullest retainer who ever accompanied a knight of the old time to the Crusade. But, observe, for a good man there must be a good master. Proud Iden was a good master, who never forgot that his man was not a piece of mechanism, but flesh and blood and feelings.

===Chinese===
这样痛快的人世间再没有第二个了；就算艾登太太深夜才想起要从镇上买什么，尽管乔治从早上五点半就开始干了一整天的重活，他也会二话不说立刻出发，来回五英里，背着东西回来时还唱着歌。
像乔治这样的人，如今在雇工阶层中依然存在，尽管环境和风气都变了，他们仍然像古代跟随骑士参加十字军东征的最忠实的家臣一样忠诚。但是，记住，好雇工必须有好主人。骄傲的艾登是个好主人，他从不忘记他的人不是一台机器，而是有血有肉、有感情的人。

===Original===
Now this great human dog, sprawling his strong arms abroad on the oaken table, warming his heavily-booted feet at the hearth, always with a cheery word and smile, by his constant presence there slowly wore away the impression of the bailiff, and the dear old kitchen came to be itself again.

===Chinese===
就这样，这条大大的人形大狗，粗壮的双臂摊在橡木桌上，穿着厚重靴子的双脚在壁炉前烤着火，始终带着快活的话语和笑容，日复一日地守在那里，慢慢磨去了那个管家留下的印象，亲爱的旧厨房终于又恢复了它本来的模样。

## XXIV / 第二十四章

===Original===
But all these shocks and worries and trampling upon her emotions made the pencil tremble in the artist's hand as she worked in the gaunt garret.

===Chinese===
然而这一切打击、忧虑与情感上的践踏，使得那位在寒碜的阁楼里作画的女画师握笔的手直打颤。

===Original===
One day, as she was returning from Woolhorton, Iden's solicitor, from whom he had borrowed money, overtook her, walked his horse, and began to talk to her in his perky, affected, silly way. Of all the fools in Woolhorton town there was none equal in pure idiotcy to this namby-pamby fellow—it was wonderful how a man of Iden's intelligence could trust his affairs to such a man, the more so as there was at least one good lawyer in the place. This is very characteristic of the farming race; they will work like negroes in the field, and practise the utmost penury to save a little, and be as cautious over a groat as the keenest miser, and then go and trust their most important affairs to some perfect fool of a solicitor. His father, perhaps, or his uncle, or somebody connected with the firm, had a reputation about the era of Waterloo, and upon this tradition they carry their business to a man whom they admit themselves "doan't seem up to much, yon." In the same way, or worse, for there is no tradition even in this case, they will consign a hundred pounds' worth of milk to London on the mere word of a milkman's agent, a man of straw for all they know, and never so much as go up to town to see if there is such a milk business in existence.

===Chinese===
一天，阿玛丽莉斯从伍尔霍顿往回走，艾登的律师——他就是从这位律师那儿借的钱——骑马追上了她，放慢马速，用他那副轻浮、做作、愚蠢的腔调跟她攀谈起来。伍尔霍顿镇上所有蠢人之中，论纯粹的白痴程度，没有一个比得上这个扭捏作态的家伙。令人难以置信的是，以艾登那样的精明人，竟会把事务托付给这样的人，况且本地至少还有一位好律师。这就是庄稼人的典型做派：他们在地里像黑奴一样卖力干活，省吃俭用一文钱掰成两半花，对一枚四便士银币精打细算堪比最抠门的守财奴，然后转头就把最要紧的事务交给一个十足的傻瓜律师去办。或许他父亲，或者他叔叔，或者什么跟那事务所有渊源的人，在滑铁卢战役那会儿有点名气，他们就凭这层老关系把生意送到一个连他们自己都承认"那家伙看起来不太行"的人手上。同样地，或者更糟——因为这回连老关系都没有了——他们仅凭牛奶代理人的一句话，就把价值一百英镑的牛奶托付运往伦敦，那人搞不好就是个空架子，他们连伦敦是否有这家牛奶行都没去核实过。

===Original===
This jackanapes began to talk to Amaryllis about her father. "Now, don't you think, Miss Iden, you could speak to your father about these money matters; you know he's getting into a pound, he really is (the jackanapes pretended to hunt); he'll be pounded. Now, don't you think you could talk to him, and persuade him to be more practical?"

===Chinese===
这个小丑开始跟阿玛丽莉斯谈论起她父亲来。"我说，艾登小姐，您看是不是跟您父亲谈谈这些钱财的事儿；您知道他正往困境里钻呢，真的（这小丑装模作样地打猎）；他会被困住的。您看是不是跟他说说，劝他实际一点？"

===Original===
The chattering of this tomtit upset Amaryllis more than the rudeness of the gruff baker who forced his way in, and would not go. That such a contemptible nincompoop should dare to advise her father to be practical! The cleverest man in the world—advise him to be practical; as if, indeed, he was not practical and hardworking to the very utmost.

===Chinese===
这只山雀的聒噪，比那个粗暴的面包匠硬闯进来赖着不走的无礼行径更让阿玛丽莉斯心烦。这样一个可鄙的蠢货居然敢建议她父亲"实际一点"！世界上最聪明的人——劝他实际一点；好像他还不够实际、不够拼命似的。

===Original===
To her it was a bitter insult. The pencil trembled in her hand.

===Chinese===
对她来说，这是一种刻骨的侮辱。她手中的画笔不住颤抖。

===Original===
But what shook it most of all was anxiety about her mother. Ever since the bailiff's intrusion Mrs. Iden had seemed so unsettled. Sometimes she would come downstairs after the rest had retired, and sit by the dying fire for hours alone, till Iden chanced to wake, and go down for her.

===Chinese===
但最令她手抖的，还是对母亲的忧虑。自从法警闯入以来，艾登太太（Mrs. Iden）一直显得心神不宁。有时别人都歇下了，她却下楼来，在将灭的炉火旁独自坐上好几个钟头，直到艾登碰巧醒了，下来接她。

===Original===
Once she went out of doors very late, leaving the front door wide open, and Amaryllis found her at midnight wandering in an aimless way among the ricks.

===Chinese===
有一次，她深夜出了门，连前门都大敞着没关，阿玛丽莉斯在半夜时分发现她正在草垛之间漫无目的地游荡。

===Original===
At such times she had a glazed look in her eyes, and did not seem to see what she gazed at. At others she would begin to cry without cause, and gave indications of hysteria. The nervous Flamma family were liable to certain affections of that kind, and Amaryllis feared lest her mother's system had been overstrained by these continual worries.

===Chinese===
在那些时候，她的双眼呆滞无神，似乎看不见自己凝视的东西。有时她会毫无来由地哭泣，显出歇斯底里的迹象。弗拉玛（Flamma）家族的人天生神经敏感，容易染上这类病症，阿玛丽莉斯担心母亲的身体在这些接二连三的操劳忧虑中已被折腾得吃不消了。

===Original===
Poor woman! she had, indeed, been worried enough to have shaken the strongest; and, having nothing stolid in her nature, it pressed upon her.

===Chinese===
可怜的女人！她确实被折腾得够呛，足以压垮最强壮的人；而她天性里毫无迟钝之处，这些重压便全落在了她身上。

===Original===
After awhile these attacks seemed to diminish, and Amaryllis hoped that nothing would come of it, but it left her in a state of extreme anxiety lest some fresh trouble should happen to renew the strain.

===Chinese===
过了一阵子，这些发作似乎有所减轻，阿玛丽莉斯心存侥幸，希望不会有什么事了，但这使她陷入了一种极度的焦虑之中，唯恐再出什么新的麻烦，让母亲的神经再次崩溃。

===Original===
When she thought of her mother she could not draw—the sound of her shuffling, nervous footstep on the landing or the path outside under the window stopped her at once. These things disheartened her a thousand times more than the returned sketches the postman was always bringing.

===Chinese===
她一想到母亲就画不下去——母亲在楼梯平台上或者窗外小径上那迟疑的、神经质的脚步声会立刻打断她。这些事让她灰心丧气的程度，比邮差不断送回来的退稿要厉害一千倍。

===Original===
On butter-making mornings, once a week, there was always a great to-do; Mrs. Iden, like nervous people, was cross and peevish when she was exceptionally busy, and clapperclawed Iden to some purpose. It chanced that Amaryllis one day was just opening an envelope and taking out a returned drawing, when Iden entered, angry and fresh from Mrs. Iden's tongue, and, seeing the letter, began to growl:—

===Chinese===
每周一次做黄油的早晨，总是闹得鸡飞狗跳。艾登太太像许多神经质的人一样，格外忙碌的时候脾气就特别暴躁，没少给艾登一顿好骂。碰巧有一天阿玛丽莉斯刚拆开信封，取出一幅被退回的画稿，艾登就进来了，一脸怒容，刚被太太数落完，一看到那封信便开始嘟囔起来：

===Original===
"Better drow that there fool stuff in the vire, and zee if you can't help your mother. Better do zummat to be some use on. Pity as you wasn't a boy chap to go out and yarn summat. Humph! humph!" growl, mutter, growl. "Drow" was local for throw, "summat" for something, "yarn" for earn. Unless I give you a vocabulary you may not be able to follow him.

===Chinese===
"莫不如把那些个傻东西扔进火里，看看你能不能帮帮你妈。干点有用的事强些。可惜你不是个男娃子，出去挣几个钱。哼！哼！"咕哝着，嘟囔着，又咕哝。"drow"是方言里的"扔"，"summat"是"什么"，"yarn"是"挣"。不给你列个词汇表，你恐怕听不懂他在说什么。

===Original===
The contemptuous allusion to her sketches as fool stuff, contrasted with the benefit and advantage of earning something—something real and solid—hit the artist very hard. That was the thought that troubled her so much, and paralysed her imagination. They were unsaleable—she saw the worthlessness of them far more than Iden. They were less in value than the paper on which they were traced; fool stuff, fit for the fire only.

===Chinese===
那番轻蔑的言辞，把她的画稿贬为"傻东西"，与"挣点钱——实实在在的东西"形成鲜明对比，重重地击中了这位画师。这正是最令她困扰、最令她想象力瘫痪的那个念头。它们卖不出去——她比艾登更清楚它们一文不值。它们连画在上面的纸都不如；不过是傻东西，只配扔进火里。

===Original===
That was the very thought that troubled her so, and Iden hit the nail home with his rude speech. That was the material view; unless a thing be material, or will fetch something material, it is good for the fire only.

===Chinese===
这正是那个令她备受折磨的念头，艾登用粗鲁的言语一针见血地戳破了它。这就是唯物质论的观点：一样东西若是非实物的，或者换不来实物，那就只配烧火。

===Original===
So it came about that the portfolio was pushed aside, and dust gathered on it, and on the pencils, and the india-rubber, and in the little box of vine charcoal. Amaryllis having arranged her violets in the tumbler of water in the window niche, sat down at the table and leant her head on her hand, and tried to think what she could do, as she had thought these many, many days.

===Chinese===
于是画夹被推到了一边，上面落满了灰，铅笔、橡皮和装藤炭的小盒里也都积了尘。阿玛丽莉斯把紫罗兰插进窗台壁龛里的水杯摆好，在桌边坐下，手托着头，试着想想自己能做些什么——就像这些天来日日夜夜想的那样。

===Original===
The drawings were so unreal, and a sovereign so real. Nothing in all the world at these moments seemed to her to be so good and precious as the round disk of gold which rules everything. The good that she could do with it—with just one of those golden disks!

===Chinese===
画稿是那样虚幻，而一镑金币是那样真实。此刻她觉得这世上没有什么比那枚统驭万物的金色圆盘更可贵、更美好了。用它能做多少好事啊——哪怕只有一枚那样的金币！

===Original===
Did you ever read Al Hariri? That accomplished scholar, the late Mr. Chenery (of The Times), translated twenty-six of his poems from the Arabic, and added most interesting notes. This curious book is a fusion of the Arabian Nights, Ecclesiastes, and Rabelais. There is the magical unexpectedness of the Arabian Nights, the vanity of vanities, all is vanity, of the Preacher, and the humour of the French satificer. Wisdom is scattered about it; at one moment you acknowledge a great thought, the next you are reproached for a folly, and presently laugh at a deep jest.

===Chinese===
你可曾读过哈里里（Al Hariri）？已故的钱纳里先生（Chenery）——《泰晤士报》的那位才子——从阿拉伯语翻译了他的二十六首诗，并附上了极为有趣的注释。这部奇特的书是《一千零一夜》、《传道书》和拉伯雷（Rabelais）的融合。其中有《一千零一夜》般的奇幻出人意料，有传道者"虚空的虚空，凡事皆虚空"的感叹，还有那位法国讽刺作家的幽默。智慧散落其间；上一刻你还在颔首赞叹一个伟大的思想，下一刻便被嘲弄了一番愚行，转眼又为一句深妙的笑话忍俊不禁。

===Original===
Al Hariri has a bearing upon Amaryllis, because he sang of the dinar, the Arabian sovereign, the double-faced dinar, the reverse and the obverse, head and tail, one side giving everything good, and the other causing all evil. For the golden disk has two sides, and two Fates belong to it. First he chants its praises:—

===Chinese===
哈里里与阿玛丽莉斯有某种关联，因为他歌咏了迪纳尔（dinar），那阿拉伯的金币，那双面的迪纳尔——正面与反面，正面与反面——一面赐予一切美好，另一面招致一切灾祸。因为金色的圆盘有两面，两重命运附于其上。他先吟咏了它的赞歌：

===Original===
> How noble is that yellow one, whose yellowness is pure,

===Chinese===
> 那金黄之物何其高贵，其金色纯净无瑕，

===Original===
> Which traverses the regions, and whose journeying is afar.

===Chinese===
> 它穿越万里疆域，远行天涯海角。

===Original===
> Told abroad are its fame and repute:

===Chinese===
> 它的盛名与声誉远播四方：

===Original===
> Its lines are set as the secret sign of wealth;

===Chinese===
> 上面的纹路刻作财富的秘符；

===Original===
> Its march is coupled with the success of endeavours;

===Chinese===
> 它的行程与一切努力的成功相伴相随；

===Original===
> Its bright look is loved by mankind,

===Chinese===
> 它明亮的容颜为世人所爱慕，

===Original===
> As though it had been molten of their hearts.

===Chinese===
> 仿佛是从他们的心头熔铸而出。

===Original===
> By its aid whoever has got it in his purse assails boldly,

===Chinese===
> 仰仗它的人，腰包里有了它便勇往直前，

===Original===
> Though kindred be perished or tardy to help.

===Chinese===
> 纵然亲族凋零或迟迟不施援手。

===Original===
> Oh! charming are its purity and brightness;

===Chinese===
> 啊！它的纯净与光华何其迷人；

===Original===
> Charming are its sufficiency and help.

===Chinese===
> 它的充裕与救助何其迷人。

===Original===
> How many a ruler is there whose rule has been perfected by it!

===Chinese===
> 有多少君王因它而将统治臻于完善！

===Original===
> How many a sumptuous one is there whose grief, but for it, would be endless!

===Chinese===
> 有多少锦衣玉食者，若无它，其悲痛将绵绵无尽！

===Original===
> How many a host of cares has one charge of it put to flight!

===Chinese===
> 有多少满腹忧愁，因它一袋入账便烟消云散！

===Original===
> How many a full moon has a sum of it brought down!

===Chinese===
> 有多少满月般的容颜，因它一笔巨款而低垂下来！

===Original===
> How many a one, burning with rage, whose coal is flaming,

===Chinese===
> 有多少人怒火中烧，如烧红的煤块烈焰熊熊，

===Original===
> Has it been secretly whispered to and then his anger has softened.

===Chinese===
> 只因有人悄悄在他耳边低语了一句（金币的数目），他的怒气便消了。

===Original===
> How many a prisoner, whom his kin had yielded,

===Chinese===
> 有多少囚徒，其亲族已弃之不顾，

===Original===
> Has it delivered, so that his gladness has been unmingled.

===Chinese===
> 却因它而获释，其欢喜从此纯粹无杂。

===Original===
> Now by the Truth of the Lord whose creation brought it forth,

===Chinese===
> 以创造它的主的真理起誓，

===Original===
> Were it not for His fear, I should say its power is supreme.

===Chinese===
> 若非敬畏祂，我当说它的力量至高无上。

===Original===
The sovereign, our dinar, does it not answer exactly to this poem of the Arabian written in the days of the Crusades! It is yellow, it is pure, it travels vast distances, and is as valuable in India as here, it is famous and has a reputation, the inscription on it is the mark of its worth, it is the sinew of war, the world loves its brightness as if it was coined from their hearts, those who have it in their purses are bold, it helps everyone who has it, it banishes all cares, and one might say, were it not for fear of the Lord, that the sovereign was all mighty.

===Chinese===
我们的金币，我们的迪纳尔，它岂不正与这首十字军时代的阿拉伯诗篇完全吻合！它是金黄的，它是纯净的，它远行万里，在印度和此地一样值钱，它声名赫赫、誉满天下，上面的铭文就是它价值的印记，它是战争的筋骨，世人爱它的光华如同从自己的心头铸出，腰包里有它的人便无所畏惧，它帮助每一个拥有它的人，它驱散一切忧愁，可以说，若非敬畏上帝，那金币便是全能的。

===Original===
All mighty for good as it seemed to Amaryllis thinking in her garret, leaning her head on her hand, and gazing at her violets; all mighty for good—if only she could get the real solid, golden sovereign!

===Chinese===
在阿玛丽莉斯于阁楼中手托着头凝视紫罗兰时看来，它在为善方面无所不能——在为善方面无所不能——只要她能得到那枚实实在在的、金灿灿的金币！

===Original===
But the golden coin has another side—the obverse—another Fate, for evil, clinging to it, and the poet, changing his tone, thunders:—

===Chinese===
但金币还有另一面——反面——另一种命运，恶纠缠其上，诗人话锋一转，雷霆万钧地吟道：

===Original===
> Ruin on it for a deceiver and insincere,

===Chinese===
> 诅咒它这个骗子和伪善者，

===Original===
> The yellow one with two faces like a hypocire!

===Chinese===
> 这张有两副面孔的金黄之物，如同伪君子！

===Original===
> It shows forth with two qualities to the eye of him that looks on it,

===Chinese===
> 它在注视者的眼中显出两种品性，

===Original===
> The adornment of the loved one, the colour of the lover.

===Chinese===
> 恋人的妆饰，爱慕者的肤色。

===Original===
> Affection for it, think they who judge truly,

===Chinese===
> 对它的深情——那些目光清醒者如是想——

===Original===
> Tempts men to commit that which shall anger their Maker.

===Chinese===
> 诱使人们犯下触怒造物主的罪过。

===Original===
> But for it no thief's right hand were cut off;

===Chinese===
> 若非因它，贼人的右手便不会被砍断；

===Original===
> Nor would tyranny be displayed by the impious;

===Chinese===
> 不敬神之人也不会施展暴政；

===Original===
> Nor would the niggardly shrink from the night-farer;

===Chinese===
> 吝啬之人也不会对夜行者避而不见；

===Original===
> Nor would the delayed claimant mourn the delay of him that withholds;

===Chinese===
> 迟迟讨不回债的债主也不会为拖欠者的拖延而哀叹；

===Original===
> Nor would men call to God from the envious who casts at them.

===Chinese===
> 人们也不会因嫉妒者的投掷而向天呼告。

===Original===
> Moreover the worst quality that it possesses

===Chinese===
> 再者，它所具有的最坏品质

===Original===
> Is that it helps thee not in straits,

===Chinese===
> 便是它在你困窘时不会帮你，

===Original===
> Save by fleeing from thee like a runaway slave.

===Chinese===
> 除非像逃奴一样从你身边溜走。

===Original===
> Well done he who casts it away from a hilltop,

===Chinese===
> 将它从山顶抛下的人做得好，

===Original===
> And who, when it whispers to him with the whispering of a lover,

===Chinese===
> 当它像情人般在他耳边低语时，

===Original===
> Says to it in the words of the truth-speaking, the veracious,

===Chinese===
> 他以真实者和诚实者的言语对它说：

===Original===
> "I have no mind for intimacy with thee—begone!"

===Chinese===
> "我不愿与你亲近——滚开！"

===Original===
"The worst quality that it possesses" remains to this day, and could Amaryllis have obtained the sovereign, still it would only have helped her by passing from her, from her hand to that of the creditor's, fleeing like a runaway slave.

===Chinese===
"它所具有的最坏品质"至今未变，即便阿玛丽莉斯能得到那枚金币，它也只是从她手中转到债权人手里，像逃奴一样从她身边溜走——这就是它帮助她的唯一方式。

===Original===
But Amaryllis surrounded with the troubles of her father and mother, saw only the good side of the golden sovereign, only that it was all powerful to bless.

===Chinese===
但被父母的忧患包围的阿玛丽莉斯，只看到金色金币美好的一面，只看到它在赐福方面无所不能。

===Original===
How unnatural it seems that a girl like this, that young and fresh and full of generous feelings as she was, her whole mind should perforce be taken up with the question of money; an unnatural and evil state of things.

===Chinese===
一个如此年轻鲜活、满心慷慨情愫的姑娘，整副心神竟被金钱的问题所占据，这似乎多么不自然；一种不自然而邪恶的境况。

===Original===
It seems to me very wicked that it should be so.

===Chinese===
在我看来，事情弄到这步田地，实在非常邪恶。

## XXIX

===Original===
Something in Fleet Street holds tight those who once come within its influence. The cerebellum of the world, the "grey matter" of the world's brain, lies somewhere thereabouts. The thoughts of our time issue thence, like the radiating spokes of a wheel, to all places of the earth. There you have touch of the throbbing pulse of the vast multitudes that live and breathe. Their ideas come from Fleet Street.

From the printing-press and the engraver's wood-block, the lithographic-stone, the etcher's plate, from book and magazine, periodical and pamphlet, from world-read newspaper.

From Fleet Street, the centre whence ideas flow outwards.

It is joyous to be in the flower-grown meads; it is sweet to be on the hilltop; delicious to feel the swell and the long roll of the hexameter of the seas; doubtless there is a wild rapture on the summit of the Himalayas; triumph in the heart of the African explorer at the river's source. But if once the mind has been dipped in Fleet Street, let the meads be never so sweet, the mountaintop never so exalted, still to Fleet Street the mind will return, because there is that other Mind, without whose sympathy even success is nothing﻿—the Mind of the world.

I am, of course, thinking not only of the thoroughfare, Fleet Street, but of all that the printing-press means.

===Chinese===
舰队街（Fleet Street）有某种东西能牢牢抓住每一个踏入它影响范围的人。世界的小脑，世界大脑的"灰质"，就位于那一带。我们这个时代的种种思想从那里发出，如车轮的辐条般辐射到地球的每一个角落。在那里，你能触摸到芸芸众生跳动的脉搏。他们的思想来自舰队街。

来自印刷机和雕刻师的木版，来自石版和蚀刻版，来自书籍和杂志、期刊和小册子，来自全世界都在读的报纸。

来自舰队街——思想从那里向外流淌的中心。

置身繁花盛开的草地令人欣喜；立于山巅令人陶醉；感受大海六步格般的涌动与绵长翻滚令人心旷神怡；毫无疑问，喜马拉雅山巅有着狂喜；非洲探险家在河流源头有着胜利的欢欣。但一旦心灵浸泡过舰队街的气息，任凭草地多么甜美、山巅多么崇高，心灵仍将回归舰队街，因为那里有另一个心灵，没有它的共鸣，就连成功也一文不值——那就是世界的心灵。

我当然不仅在想舰队街这条大道，还在想印刷机所代表的一切。

===Original===
Alere was no leader of thought, but it was necessary to him to live and breathe in the atmosphere of thought﻿—to feel the throb and swell around him﻿—to be near the "grey matter" of the world's brain.

Once a man gets into Fleet Street he cannot get out. Flamma would not leave it for months of gilded idleness in any nobleman's mansion.

The flame must be fed. His name had some connection with the design of the Roman lamp on the splendid bindings of the books tooled in the House of Flamma. *Alere Flammam*﻿—feed the flame. The flame of the mind must be fed.

Sad things happen on the stones of Fleet Street; if I could but get at it all to write the inside life of it, it would, indeed, be a book. Stone-cold poverty hovers about. The rich, living in the fool's paradise of money, think they know life, but they do not, for, as was said of the sea﻿

> Only those who share its dangers
>  
> Comprehend its mystery.

Only those who have shared the struggle literally for bread﻿—for a real, actual loaf﻿—understand the dread realities of man's existence.

===Chinese===
阿莱尔并非思想的领袖，但他必须在思想的氛围中生活和呼吸——感受周围的脉动与涌动——靠近世界大脑的"灰质"。

一个人一旦走进舰队街，就再也走不出去了。弗拉玛不会为几个月在贵族府邸里的镀金闲暇而离开它。

火焰必须被喂养。他的名字与弗拉玛家族印刷室里那些精美装帧书籍上的罗马油灯图案有着某种关联。*Alere Flammam*——喂养火焰。心灵的火焰必须被喂养。

舰队街的石板路上发生着悲惨的事；倘若我能将这一切都写出来，写下它内部的生活，那确实会是一本书。冰冷彻骨的贫困在周围游荡。富人生活在金钱的愚人天堂里，以为自己懂得生活，但他们不懂，因为正如人们评价大海的那样——

> 只有那些与它共担危险的人
> 才能理解它的奥秘。

只有那些真正为面包——一块实实在在的面包——挣扎过的人，才能理解人类存在的可怕现实。

===Original===
Let but a morsel of wood﻿—a little splinter of deal, a curl of carpenter's shaving﻿—lie in Fleet Street, and it draws to it the wretched human beasts as surely as the offal draws the beast of the desert to the camp. A morsel of wood in the streets that are paved with gold!

It is so valuable. Women snatch it up and roll it in their aprons, clasping it tightly, lest it should somehow disappear. Prowling about from street to street, mile after mile, they fill their aprons with these precious splinters of deal, for to those who are poor fuel is as life itself.

Even the wealthy, if they have once been ill, especially of blood-thinning diseases (as rheumatism), sometimes say they would rather go without food than coal. Rather emptiness than chill.

These women know where there are hoardings erected by builders, where shopfronts are being rebuilt, where fires have taken place, where alterations are proceeding; they know them as the birds know the places where they are likely to find food, and visit them day by day for the scraps of wood and splinters that drop on the pavement.

Or they send their children, ragged urchins, battling for a knot of pinewood.

===Chinese===
只需有一小片木头——一小片松木屑、一卷刨花——丢在舰队街，它就一定会吸引那些可怜的人，就像腐肉吸引荒漠中的野兽一样。在铺满黄金的街道上，竟然有一小片木头！

它如此珍贵。女人们抢起它来裹进围裙，紧紧抱住，生怕它不知怎么就消失了。她们一条街一条街地游荡，一英里又一英里，用这些珍贵的松木屑塞满围裙，因为对穷人来说，燃料就等于生命本身。

就连富人，如果曾经生过病，尤其是血液稀薄的疾病（如风湿病），有时也会说宁愿没有食物也不能没有煤。宁可挨饿也不能受冻。

这些女人知道哪里有建筑商搭的围挡，哪里在重建店面，哪里发生过火灾，哪里在进行改造；她们就像鸟儿知道哪里能找到食物一样了解这些地方，日复一日地去拾取掉落在人行道上的碎木片和刨花。

或者她们打发自己的孩子——衣衫褴褛的小顽童——去争夺一小块松木。

===Original===
The terror of frost to these creatures is great indeed. Frost is the King of Terrors to them﻿—not Death; they sleep and live with death constantly, the dead frequently in the room with the living, and with the unborn that is near birth.

Alere's ten pounds helped them. The drunkard's wife knew that Flamma, the drinker, would certainly give her the silver in his pocket.

The ragged urchins, battling for a knot of pinewood, knew that they could charm the pennies and the threepenny bits out of his waistcoat; the baked potatoes and the roasted chestnuts looked so nice on the street stove.

Wretched girls whose power of tempting had gone, and with it their means of subsistence, begged, and not in vain, of shaky Alere Flamma. There are many of these wretches in Fleet Street. There is no romance about them to attract the charity of the world.

Once a flower-girl, selling flowers without a licence in the street, was charged by the police. How this harshness to the flower-girl﻿—the human representation of Flora﻿—roused up sentiment in her behalf!

But not every starving girl has the fortune to rouse up sentiment and to be fed. Their faces disfigured with eruptions, their thin shoulders, their dry, disordered hair﻿—hair never looks nice unless soft with its natural oil﻿—their dingy complexions, their threadbare shawls, tempt no one. They cannot please, therefore they must starve.

The good turn from them with horror﻿—Are they not sin made manifest? The trembling hand of Alere fed them.

===Chinese===
严寒对这些人的恐惧实在太大了。霜冻是他们的恐怖之王——不是死亡；他们与死亡同睡同住，死者常常就在活人旁边，还有即将出生的未生者。

阿莱尔的十英镑帮助了他们。酒鬼的妻子知道弗拉玛这个酒徒一定会把口袋里的银币给她。

那些为一小块松木争抢的衣衫褴褛的小顽童知道，他们能从阿莱尔的马甲里骗出便士和三便士硬币；街边炉子上烤着的土豆和栗子看起来多诱人啊。

那些容颜已逝、魅力不再的可怜姑娘们，连同她们赖以维生的手段一并丧失，向摇摇晃晃的阿莱尔·弗拉玛乞讨，而且并非空手而归。舰队街上有许多这样的可怜人。她们身上没有任何浪漫之处能引起世间的怜悯。

有一次，一个卖花女在街上无证卖花，被警察指控了。对这位卖花女——芙罗拉（Flora）的人间化身——的苛待，激起了多少人对她的同情！

但并非每个挨饿的姑娘都有幸激起同情并得到施舍。她们的脸因疮疤而变形，她们瘦削的肩膀，她们干枯凌乱的头发——头发只有柔软且带有天然油脂时才好看——她们暗沉的肤色，她们破旧的披肩，引不起任何人的兴趣。她们无法讨人喜欢，因此只能饿死。

善良的人们带着恐惧转身离去——她们不就是罪孽的明证吗？阿莱尔颤抖的手喂养了她们。

===Original===
Because the boys bawl do you suppose they are happy? It is curious that people should associate noise with a full stomach. The shoeblack boys, the boys that are gathered into institutions and training ships, are expected to bawl and shout their loudest at the annual fêtes when visitors are present. Your bishops and deans forthwith feel assured that their lives are consequently joyous.

Why then do they set fire to training ships? Why do they break out of reformatory institutions? Bawling is not necessarily happiness. Yet fatuous fools are content if only they can hear a good uproar of bawling.

I have never walked up Fleet Street and the Strand yet without seeing a starving woman and child. The children are indeed dreadful; they run unguarded and unwatched out of the side courts into the broader and more lively Strand﻿—the ceaseless world pushes past﻿—they play on the pavement unregarded. Hatless, shoeless, bound about with rags, their faces white and scarred with nameless disease, their eyes bleared, their hair dirty; little things, such as in happy homes are sometimes set on the table to see how they look.

How *can* people pass without seeing them?

Alere saw them, and his hand went to his waistcoat pocket.

===Chinese===
就因为那些男孩在大声嚷嚷，你就以为他们快乐吗？奇怪的是，人们总把喧闹和饱腹联系在一起。擦鞋童、被收容进机构和训练船的男孩们，被期望在年度庆典上当着来宾的面拼命叫喊。你们的主教和教长立刻就确信他们的生活因此而幸福。

那他们为什么放火烧训练船？为什么从感化院逃跑？大声嚷嚷并不等于幸福。然而愚昧的蠢人只要能听到一阵响亮的喧闹就心满意足了。

我每次走过舰队街和斯特兰德大街（Strand），总能看到挨饿的妇女和儿童。那些孩子实在令人痛心；他们无人看管地从侧巷跑到更宽阔、更热闹的斯特兰德大街——永不停息的世界从他们身边涌过——他们在人行道上玩耍，无人理会。没有帽子，没有鞋子，裹着破布，脸因无名的疾病而苍白、布满疤痕，眼睛浑浊，头发肮脏；这些小东西，在幸福的家庭里有时会被摆到桌上看他们长什么样。

人们*怎么能*视而不见地走过？

阿莱尔看见了他们，手伸向了马甲的口袋。

===Original===
The rich folk round about this great Babylon of Misery, where cruel Want sits on the Seven Hills﻿—make a cartoon of that!﻿—the rich folk who receive hundreds on the turn of a stock, who go to the Bank of England on dividend days﻿—how easily the well-oiled doors swing open for them!﻿—who dwell in ease and luxury at Sydenham, at Norwood, at Surbiton, at Streatham, at Brighton, at Seven-oaks, wherever there is pure air, have distinguished themselves lately in the giving of alms, ordained by the Lord whom they kneel before each Sunday, dressed in silk, scarlet, and fine linen, in their cushioned pews.

They have established Homes for Lost Dogs and Homes for Lost Cats, neither of which are such nuisances as human beings.

In the dog institution they have set up an apparatus specially designed by one of the leading scientific men of the age. The dogs that are not claimed in a certain time, or that have become diseased﻿—like the human nuisances﻿—are put into this apparatus, into a comfortable sort of chamber, to gnaw their last bone. By-and-by, a scientific vapour enters the chamber, and breathing this, the animal falls calmly to death, painlessly poisoned in peace.

Seven thousand dogs were thus happily chloroformed "into eternity" in one season. Jubilant congratulations were exchanged at the success of the apparatus. Better than shooting, drowning, hanging, vivisection, or starvation!

===Chinese===
在这座苦难的巨大巴比伦周围，在这个"贫乏"端坐七丘之上——画一幅漫画来表现这个！——的富人们，那些靠股票翻转就收入数百英镑的人，那些在分红日去英格兰银行的人——上过油的门为他们敞开得多轻松啊！——那些在锡德纳姆（Sydenham）、诺伍德（Norwood）、瑟比顿（Surbiton）、斯特里汉姆（Streatham）、布莱顿（布莱顿）、七橡树（Seven-oaks）以及所有空气清新的地方安居享乐的富人们，近来在施舍方面大放异彩，这是他们的主所吩咐的——他们每个星期天都跪在这位主面前，穿着丝绸、猩红和细麻布，在铺着软垫的教堂长椅上。

他们建立了走失犬之家和走失猫之家，这两样东西都不像人类那样惹人厌烦。

在犬类收容所里，他们安装了一台由这个时代一位顶尖科学家专门设计的仪器。那些在一定时间内无人认领的狗，或者已经生病的狗——就像那些惹人厌烦的人类一样——被放进这台仪器，放进一个舒适的腔室里，去啃它们最后一根骨头。过一会儿，一种科学气体进入腔室，吸入这种气体后，动物平静地死去，无痛地被和平毒死。

一个季节就有七千条狗被这样愉快地氯仿"送入永恒"。仪器的成功令人欢欣鼓舞，人们互相祝贺。比枪杀、溺毙、绞死、活体解剖或饿死好多了！

===Original===
Let a dog die in peace. Is not this an age of humanity indeed? To sell all you have and give to the poor was nothing compared to this. We have progressed since Anno Domini I. We know better how to do it now.

Alere did not seem to trouble himself much about the dogs; he saw so much of the human nuisances.

What a capital idea it would be to set up an apparatus like this in the workhouses and in conjunction with the hospitals!

Do you know, thoughtless, happy maiden, singing all the day, that one out of every five people who die in London, die in the workhouse or the hospital?

Eighty-two thousand people died in London in 1882, and of these, fourteen thousand expired in the workhouses, and six thousand in hospitals!

Are not these ghastly figures? By just setting up a few Apparatuses, see what an immense amount of suffering would be saved, and consider what a multitude of human nuisances would be "moved on!"

The poor have a saying that none live long after they have been in a certain hospital. "He's been in that hospital﻿—he won't live long." They carry out such wonderful operations there﻿—human vivisections, but strictly painless, of course, under chloroform﻿—true Christian chopping-up﻿—still the folk do not live long when they come out.

Why not set up the Apparatus? But a man must not die in peace. Starvation is for human nuisances.

===Chinese===
让一条狗平静地死去吧。这难道不真是一个人道的时代吗？变卖你所有的一切分给穷人，跟这比起来简直不值一提。自公元元年以来，我们进步了。我们现在更懂得如何行事了。

阿莱尔似乎并不太在意那些狗；他看到太多惹人厌烦的人类了。

在济贫院和医院里安装这样一台仪器，那该是个多么绝妙的主意啊！

无忧无虑、终日歌唱的快乐少女啊，你知道吗？在伦敦死去的人中，每五个就有一个死在济贫院或医院里？

一八八二年伦敦有八万两千人死亡，其中一万四千人死在济贫院，六千人死在医院！

这些数字不令人毛骨悚然吗？只需安装几台仪器，就能省去多少痛苦啊，想想看又有多少惹人厌烦的人会被"打发走"！

穷人们有句话说，住过某家医院的人没有活长久的。"他住过那家医院——活不长了。"他们在那里面做些多么了不起的手术啊——人体活体解剖，当然，是在氯仿下严格无痛的——真正的基督教式切割——但人们出来后还是活不长。

为什么不安上那台仪器？但人不能平静地死。饥饿是为惹人厌烦的人准备的。

===Original===
These rich folk dwelling round about the great Babylon of Misery, where Want sits on the Seven Hills, have also distinguished themselves by yet another invention. This is the organization of alms. Charity is so holy we will not leave it to chance﻿—to the stray penny﻿—we will organize it. The system is very simple: it is done by ticket. First you subscribe a few shillings to some organization, with its secretary, its clerks, its offices, boardroom, and "machinery." For this you receive tickets.

If a disagreeable woman with a baby in her arms, or a ragged boy, or a maimed man asks you for a "copper," you hand him a ticket. This saves trouble and responsibility.

The beggar can take the ticket to the "office" and get his case "investigated." After an inquiry, and an adjournment for a week; another inquiry, and another adjournment for a week; a third inquiry, and a third adjournment, then, if he be of high moral character and highly recommended, he may get his dinner.

One great advantage is conspicuous in this system: by no possible means can you risk giving a penny to a man not of high moral character, though he be perishing of starvation.

If a man asks for bread, will ye give him a stone? Certainly not; give him a ticket.

They did not understand how to do things in Judea Anno Domini I.

This organization of charity saves such a lot of money: where people used to give away five pounds they now pay five shillings.

Nothing like saving money. And, besides, you walk about with a clear conscience. No matter how many maimed men, or disagreeable women, or ragged boys you see, you can stroll on comfortably and never think about them; your charity is organized.

===Chinese===
这些居住在"贫乏"端坐七丘之上的苦难巴比伦周围的富人们，还因另一项发明而大放异彩。那就是慈善的组织化。慈善如此神圣，我们不能将它交给偶然——交给一枚流浪的便士——我们要将它组织起来。这个制度非常简单：用票据操作。你先向某个组织缴纳几先令，这个组织有秘书、职员、办公室、董事会和"机器"。为此你收到一些票据。

如果一个抱着婴儿的讨厌女人，或者一个衣衫褴褛的男孩，或者一个残疾人向你要一个"铜板"，你就递给他一张票据。这省去了麻烦和责任。

乞丐可以把票据拿到"办公室"，让他的案子被"调查"。调查一次，延期一周；再调查一次，再延期一周；第三次调查，第三次延期，然后，如果他品德高尚、有人极力推荐，他或许能吃上一顿饭。

这个制度的一大显著优点是：你绝不可能冒风险把一个便士给一个品德不高尚的人，哪怕他正在饿死。

人若求饼，你会给他石头吗？当然不会；给他一张票据。

公元元年的犹太人不懂得如何做事。

这种慈善的组织化省下了大笔钱财：过去人们要施舍五英镑的地方，现在只需付五先令。

没有什么比省钱更好的了。而且，你还能心安理得地走来走去。无论你看到多少残疾人、讨厌的女人或衣衫褴褛的男孩，你都可以悠然散步，从不去想他们；你的慈善已经组织化了。

===Original===
If the German thinkers had not found out twenty years ago that there was no Devil, one would be inclined to ascribe this spurious, lying, false, and abominable mockery to the direct instigation of a Satan.

The organization of charity! The very nature of charity is spontaneousness.

You should have heard Alere lash out about this business; he called it charity suppression.

Have you ever seen London in the early winter morning, when the frost lies along the kerb, just melting as the fires are lit; cold, grey, bitter, stony London?

Whatever *can* morning seem like to the starved and chilly wretches who have slept on the floor, and wake up to frost in Fleet Street?

The pavements are covered with expectoration, indicating the chest diseases and misery that thousands are enduring. But I must not write too plainly; it would offend.

===Chinese===
要不是二十年前德国思想家们发现并不存在魔鬼，人们真会把这种虚伪、撒谎、荒谬、可恶的嘲弄归咎于撒旦的直接唆使。

慈善的组织化！慈善的本质恰恰在于自发性。

你真该听听阿莱尔对这件事的怒斥；他称之为"慈善压制"。

你见过初冬清晨的伦敦吗？霜冻沿着路缘蔓延，炉火刚点着时正在融化；寒冷、灰暗、刺骨、石板般的伦敦。

对于那些睡在地板上、在舰队街的霜冻中醒来的饥寒交迫的可怜人来说，清晨*能*是什么样子？

人行道上满是痰迹，表明成千上万的人正在忍受的胸部疾病和苦难。但我不能写得太直白；那会冒犯人。

## XXV / 第XXV章

===Original===
Though the portfolio was pushed aside and dust had gathered on the table, except where her arm touched it, Amaryllis came daily, and often twice a day, to her flowers to pray.

===Chinese===
虽然画夹已被推到一旁，桌面积了薄灰——除了她胳膊碰过的地方——阿玛丽莉斯还是每天都来，常常一天两次，对着她的花祈祷。

===Original===
From the woods she brought the delicate primrose opening on the mossy bank among the grey ash-stoles; the first tender green leaflet of hawthorn coming before the swallow; the garden crocus from the grass of the garden; the first green spikelet from the sward of the meadow; the beautiful white wild violets gathered in the sunlit April morning while the nightingale sang.

===Chinese===
她从林子里采来娇嫩的报春花——开在灰白梣树根茬间长满苔藓的岸上；燕子来之前就冒出的山楂嫩叶；园中草丛里的园艺番红花；草地草皮上最先钻出的绿芽；还有四月阳光明媚的清晨、夜莺啼鸣时采来的洁白野紫罗兰。

===Original===
With these she came to pray each day, at the window-niche. After she had sat awhile at the table that morning, thinking, she went and knelt at the window with her face in her hands; the scent of the violets filled her hair.

===Chinese===
她每天带着这些花到窗龛前祈祷。那天早晨她在桌旁坐了一会儿，想着心事，然后走到窗前跪下，双手掩面；紫罗兰的香气盈满了她的头发。

===Original===
Her prayer was deeper than words and was not put in language, but came rushing through her heart;﻿—“That her dear mother might not suffer any more, that the strain of ceaseless trouble might be removed from her mind, that peace and rest might come to her in her old age. Let her step become firm, and the nervousness depart, and her eyes shine like they used to, so clear and bright, and do not let the grey hairs show more than they do now, or increase in number. Let her smile and be happy and talk cheerfully, and take an interest in the house and all the order of household things, and also see and understand that her husband meant to please her, even in such a little thing as splitting up useful wood for the fire, that he intended to please her, and that she might not misunderstand him any more. He intended to be kind in many ways, but misfortune had blinded her, and she took things the wrong way. And give her more change and friends to ask her out from home on visits, so that she might be amused, and make them come to see her and pass the time in contentment. Give her also enough money to buy good clothes so as to look nice as she ought to do, and if possible a conveyance of some kind﻿—not a grand carriage, she did not wish for that﻿—but a conveyance to drive about now and then, because she was not so strong as she used to be, and could not walk far.

===Chinese===
她的祈祷超出了言语，不是用话语说出的，而是从心底奔涌而出；——"愿亲爱的母亲不再受苦，愿无尽烦恼带来的重压从她心中卸去，愿她在晚年得到安宁与休憩。让她的步履重新稳健，让焦虑消散，让她的眼睛重新像从前那样明亮清澈，不要让白发再增添，不要更多了。让她微笑、快乐、言语欢快，对这个家、对家中一切井井有条的事物都重新生出兴趣，也让她明白、体谅她丈夫本意是想让她高兴的，哪怕是劈些好柴火生炉子这样的小事，他本意是想讨她欢心，让她不要再误解他了。他本想在许多事上都体贴她，可不幸蒙蔽了她，她把事情想岔了。给她多些消遣，请朋友邀她出门走走，让她散散心，让他们来看她，让她舒心地消磨时光。也给她足够的钱买些好衣裳，让她打扮得漂漂亮亮，如果可能的话，弄一辆代步的车——不用什么豪华马车，她不稀罕那个——只要偶尔能坐着出去转转，因为她不像从前那么硬朗了，走不了远路。

===Original===
And let me, thought Amaryllis, let me be able to give her a watch, for other people have watches, and my mother has not got one, and it does seem so strange it should be so after all the hard work she has done. Let me, too, get her some nice things to eat, some fish and wine, for she cannot eat our plain bacon now every day, she has not got an appetite, and her teeth too are bad, and I should so like to give her a set of artificial teeth that her food might do her more good. But what I really want is that she may be happy, and be like my mother herself really is when she is herself.

===Chinese===
让我，阿玛丽莉斯想，让我能送她一块手表吧，因为别人都有手表，我母亲却没有，她做了那么多苦活，到头来竟没有一块表，实在奇怪。也让我给她弄些好吃的，弄点鱼和酒，因为她现在天天吃咱们的咸熏肉也吃不下了，没了胃口，牙齿也不好，我真想给她弄一副假牙，好让吃下去的东西多吸收些。可我真正想要的，是她能快快乐乐的，像她本来该有的样子。

===Original===
Give my father money enough to pay his creditors, for I know that though he is so quiet and says nothing, these debts are wearing him out, and I know he wishes to pay them, and does not willingly keep them waiting. He is so patient, and so good, and bears everything, I am sure no one was ever like him, and it is so dreadful to see him work, work, work, every day from five o’clock in the morning, and yet to be always worried with these debts and people that will not let him have peace one single day. Do, please, let him have less work to do, it makes me miserable to see him in the rain, and he is not young now, and sometimes carrying such heavy things, great pieces of timber and large trusses of hay, and making his back ache digging. Surely it must soon be time for him to leave off working, he has done such a lot, and I do not think he can see quite so well as he used to, because he holds the paper so close to his eyes.

===Chinese===
给我父亲足够的钱还清债，因为我知道，虽然他那么沉默寡言、一声不吭，这些债务正把他拖垮，我知道他想还钱，不愿意欠着别人。他那么有耐心，那么好，什么都扛着，我相信没有人像他那样，看着他天天从清晨五点起就干活、干活、干活，却总是被这些债务和不肯让他清静一天的人纠缠，真是叫人心碎。求求你，让他少干点活吧，看着他在雨里我难受极了，他现在不年轻了，有时还扛那么重的东西，大块的木料、大捆的干草，挖地挖得腰酸背痛。他干了这么多年，一定是时候歇歇了，我觉得他的眼神不如从前好了，看报纸都凑得那么近。求你让他早点别干了，歇一歇，换换环境，跟母亲一起出去走走，在家时除了侍弄他的花园就别操别的了，因为他那么爱花园；让他重新像从前那样爱花，多种些，最重的活儿就是从他种的树上摘果子。

===Original===
And let me get him some new books to read, because I know he is so fond of books; he has not had a new book for so long. Let him go to London and see people and things, and life, because I know he is full of ideas and thoughts though he works and digs, and that is what would do him good. Give him some money now at last, now he has worked all these years, forty years on this farm, and ever so much work before that; do give him some money at last. Do make my grandfather kinder to him and not so harsh for the rent, let him give the place to my father now, for it can be no use to him; let my father have it for his very own, and then I think he would be happy after all, he does so like to improve things and make them beautiful, and if it was his very own there is so much that he could do.

===Chinese===
让我给他弄些新书看，因为我知道他那么爱书；他好久没读新书了。让他去伦敦看看人、看看事、看看世面，因为我知道他虽然整天劳作，心里却满是想法和念头，出去走走对他有好处。给他些钱吧，最后给一次，他干了这么多年，在这个农场上干了四十年，之前还干了那么多活儿；求求你最后给他些钱。让祖父对他好些，别那么苛刻地收租子，把这地方现在就给我父亲吧，反正祖父留着也没用；让我父亲名正言顺地拥有它，我想他会快乐的，他那么喜欢把事物变得更好、更美，要是这是他自己的地方，他能做的事就太多了。

===Original===
That would be nice work and work that he would enjoy doing, and not just to get a few wretched shillings to pay other people. I am sure he would never be cross then, and he would be so kind to my mother, and kind and good to everybody. There is nobody like him, as you know, in this place; they are not clever like him, and good to the labouring men and their families like he is (and so is my mother too); they are so rough, and so unkind and stupid; I do not mean anything against them, but they are not like he is. And if you were to help him he would soon help the poor people and give them food and more wages; you know how good he is in his heart.

===Chinese===
那才是好差事，他做着也开心，而不是为了挣那几个可怜的先令还给别人。我敢说那样他就再也不会发脾气了，对母亲也会很好，对谁都和善。您知道，这地方没人比得上他；他们不像他那么聪明，不像他对干活的人和他们的家人那么好（母亲也是这样）；他们那么粗鲁、那么刻薄、那么愚蠢；我不是要说他们坏话，但他们不像他那样。要是您帮帮他，他很快就会帮助穷人，给他们吃的、给他们涨工钱；您知道他心地多好。

===Original===
And he would do it, not because other people should praise him, but because he would like to do it; if he does not go to church his heart is very true, and it is because he likes to be true and genuine, and not make any false show. Do, please, help him, and give him some money, and do, please, let him have this place for his very own, for I do so fear lest those who set my grandfather against him, should have a will made, so that my father should not have this house and land as he ought to do, as the son. He has made it so beautiful with trees, and brought the fresh spring water up to the house, and done so many clever things, and his heart is here, and it is home to him, and no other place could be like it. I think it would kill him not to have it, and for me, I should be so﻿—I cannot tell, I should be so miserable if he did not, but I will not think of myself.

===Chinese===
他会这么做的，不是为了让别人夸他，而是因为他乐意；就算他不去教堂，他的心也是虔诚的，因为他喜欢真诚实在，不喜欢做表面功夫。求求您帮帮他，给他些钱，求求您把这地方给他名正言顺地拥有，因为我太担心那些挑唆祖父跟他作对的人，会立下遗嘱，让他本该继承的房子和土地落了空。他种了那么多树把这里弄得很美，把清新的泉水引到家里，做了那么多巧妙的事，他的心在这里，这里是他的家，别的地方再好也比不上。我觉得要是他没了这地方会活不下去的，至于我，我会——我说不出，我会很难过的，不过我不去想我自己。

===Original===
There are so many things I know he wants to do if only he was not so worried with debts, and if he could feel it was his own land; he wants to plant a copse, and to make a pond by the bro溪, and have trout in it, and to build a wall by the rickyard. Think how my dear father has worked all these years, and do help him now, and give him some money, and this place, and please do not let him grow any more grey than his hair is now, and save his eyes, for he is so fond of things that are beautiful, and please make my mother happy with him.”

===Chinese===
我知道他有好多事想做，只要他不被债务缠着，只要他能觉得这是他自己的地；他想种一片小树林，在溪边挖个池塘，养些鳟鱼，在草垛场边砌一堵墙。想想我亲爱的父亲这些年是怎么熬过来的，求您现在帮帮他，给他些钱，给他这地方，求您别让他的白发再多添一根了，保住他的眼睛，因为他那么爱美，求您让他和母亲都快快乐乐的。"

===Original===
When Amaryllis rose from her knees her face was quite white, emotion had taken away her colour, and tears were thick on her cheek. She sat a little while by the table to recover herself, still thinking, and remembered that again last night she had dreamed the same dream about fire in the thatch. Somehow there seemed to be an alarm in the night, and they ran out of doors and found the corner of the roof on fire, over the window with the wire network instead of glass. It ran up from the corner towards the chimney, where the roof was mossy by the ridge. There was no flame, but a deep red seething heat, as if the straw burned inwardly, and was glowing like molten metal. Each straw seemed to lie in the furious heat, and a light to flicker up and down, as if it breathed fire. The thatch was very thick there, she knew, and recollected it quite well in her dream; Iden himself had laid on two thick coats in his time, and it was heavy enough before then. He talked about the thatching of it, because it was an argument with him that straw had a great power of endurance, and was equal to slates for lasting. This thickness, she saw, was the reason the fire did not blaze up quickly, and why, fortunately, it was slow in moving up the roof. It had not yet eaten through, so that there was no draught﻿—once it got through, it would burn fast﻿—if only they could put it out before then all might yet be saved. In the midst of her anxiety Iden came with the largest ladder in the rickyard, and mounted up, carrying a bucket of water. She tried to follow, holding on to the rungs of the ladder with one hand, and dragging up a heavy bucket with the other﻿—the strain and effort to get up woke her.

===Chinese===
阿玛丽莉斯跪起身时脸色煞白，激动褪去了她脸上的血色，泪珠挂满了双颊。她在桌旁坐了一会儿平复心情，仍在想心事，想起昨晚又做了那个草顶着火的梦。不知怎的，夜里似乎有警报声，他们跑出门，发现屋顶一角着火了，就在那扇装着铁丝网代替玻璃的窗户上方。火从屋角朝烟囱蔓延，烟囱附近的屋脊长满了青苔。没有明火，只有一片深红的、翻滚的热浪，仿佛稻草从里面烧了起来，像熔化的金属一样发着光。每一根稻草都躺在灼人的热气里，火光忽明忽暗地跳动，像在吐着火舌。她知道那里的草顶很厚，梦里记得清清楚楚；艾登自己当年铺了两层厚草，之前就已经够厚了。他说过这草顶的铺法，因为他有个论点：稻草耐力极强，跟瓦片一样经久耐用。她看出来了，正是这厚度让火没有很快烧旺，也幸好火势在屋顶上蔓延得很慢。火还没烧穿，所以没有穿堂风——一旦烧穿，就会烧得飞快——只要在那之前把火扑灭，一切或许还能挽救。正当她焦急万分时，艾登扛着草垛场最大的梯子来了，提着一桶水往上爬。她试着跟上去，一只手抓着梯级，另一只手拖着一桶沉甸甸的水——拼命往上爬的紧张和费力把她惊醒了。

===Original===
This dream had happened to her so many times, and was so vivid and circumstantial﻿—the fire seemed to glow in the thatch﻿—that at last she began to dread lest it should come true. If it did not come true of the house itself, perhaps it would of the family, and of their affairs; perhaps it signified that the fire of debt, and poverty, and misfortune would burn them, as it were, to the ground. She tried to think whether in the dream they were getting the fire under before she woke, or whether they could not master it; it seemed dubious.

===Chinese===
这个梦她做了太多次，如此逼真、如此细致——草顶上的火光似乎还在眼前闪烁——最终她开始害怕它会成真。即便不是房子本身着火，或许也应验在家庭、应验在家事上；也许它意味着债务、穷困和不幸的烈火会将他们烧得片瓦不留。她努力回想，梦里她醒来之前火是不是已经控制住了，还是他们没能扑灭；似乎不太妙。

===Original===
She did not tell her mother of the dream, afraid lest it might excite her again; nor could she tell Iden, who would have laughed at her.

===Chinese===
她没把梦告诉母亲，怕再刺激她；也不能告诉艾登，他一定会笑话她。

===Original===
Yet, though she knew it was but a dream, and dreams have ceased to come true, she did not like it; she felt uncertain, as if some indefinable danger was threatening round about. As she sat at the table she added to her prayer the supplication that the dear old house might not be burned down.

===Chinese===
然而，虽然她知道那不过是个梦，梦早已不灵了，她还是不喜欢这个梦；她感到不安，好像某种说不清的危险正笼罩四周。坐在桌旁时，她在祈祷中又加了一句：求您保佑这可爱的老房子别被烧毁。

===Original===
Soon afterwards she went downstairs, and on the lower flight paused, to listen to voices﻿—not those of her mother and Iden﻿—creditors, doubtless, come to cry aloud, “Pay me that thou owest!”﻿—the very sum and total of religion. Her heart beat quicker﻿—the voices came again, and she thought she recognized them, and that they were not those of creditors. She entered the sitting-room, and found that two visitors, from widely separated places, had arrived; one with a portmanteau, the other with an old, many-coloured carpetbag. They were Amadis Iden, from Iden Court, over the Downs, the Court Idens, as they were called, and Alere Flamma, from London; the Flammas were carpetbag people.

===Chinese===
不久她下了楼，在最后一段楼梯上停住脚步，听声音——不是母亲和艾登的——毫无疑问是债主来了，大声叫喊着："把你所欠的还我！"——这简直就是宗教的全部要义。她心跳得更快了——声音又响起来，她觉得听出来了，不是债主的声音。她走进客厅，发现有两位客人，从相隔很远的地方赶来；一个提着手提箱，一个拎着一只旧的、花花绿绿的毛毯旅行袋。他们是从丘陵那边来的阿玛迪斯·艾登（Amadis Iden），来自艾登庄园（Iden Court），也就是所谓的"庄园艾登家"，还有从伦敦来的阿莱尔·弗拉玛（Alere Flamma）；弗拉玛家的人出门都拎毛毯旅行袋。

===Original===
Her father was making them very welcome, after his wont, and they were talking of the house the Idens of yore had built in a lonely spot, expressly in order that they might drink, drink, drink undisturbed by their unreasonable wives.

===Chinese===
父亲像往常一样热烈欢迎他们，他们在聊从前的艾登家在偏僻的地方盖的一座房子，专为不受蛮横妻子打扰、喝酒、喝酒、再喝酒。

## XXVI

===Original===
They talked on and on, these three, Iden, Amadis Iden, and Alere Flamma, with Amaryllis listening, from the end of April till near the end of May; till "a month passed away," and still they were talking. For there is nothing so good to the human heart as well agreed conversation, when you know that your companion will answer to your thought as the anvil meets the hammer, ringing sound to merry stroke; better than wine, better than sleep, like love itself—for love is agreement of thought—"God listens to those who pray to him; let us eat and drink, and think of nothing," says the Arabian proverb. So they ate and drank—very moderate the drinking—and thought of nothing, and talked, which should be added to complete felicity. Not, of course, all of them always together, sometimes all four, sometimes Alere, Amadis, and Amaryllis, sometimes only the last two.

The round summerhouse was their Parliament House whenever the east winds sank and the flowers shone forth like sunshine; as the sun shines when the clouds withdraw, so when the harsh east winds cease the May flowers immediately bloom and glow.

It was a large round house, properly builded of brick, as a summerhouse should be—put not thy faith in lath work—and therefore dry and warm; to sit in it was like sitting in a shell, warm and comfortable, with a sea of meadow-grass, smooth and coloured, stretching in front, islanded about with oak, and elm, and ash.

===Chinese===
他们三个——艾登（Iden）、阿玛迪斯·艾登（Amadis Iden）和阿莱尔·弗拉玛（Alere Flamma），以及旁听的阿玛丽莉斯（Amaryllis）——从四月底一直聊到五月底将近；"一个月过去了"，他们还在聊。因为对于人心而言，没有什么比志趣相投的交谈更好的了——你知道你的伙伴会像铁砧迎着铁锤那样回应你的思绪，清脆的回响应和着欢快的敲击；这比酒好，比睡眠好，如同爱情本身——因为爱情正是思想的契合——"真主垂听向他祈祷的人；让我们吃喝，不去想任何事"，阿拉伯谚语如是说。于是他们吃吃喝喝——酒喝得极少——什么也不想，只是聊天，这该加到圆满的幸福中去。当然不是始终四人聚在一起，有时四人全在，有时是阿莱尔、阿玛迪斯和阿玛丽莉斯，有时只有后两人。

每当东风停歇、花朵像阳光般灿烂绽放时，那座圆形凉亭便是他们的议会大厅；正如乌云散去阳光普照，当凛冽的东风止息，五月的花朵便立刻绽放发光。

那是一座宽敞的圆形砖房，凉亭本该如此建造——切莫信靠板条工事——因此干燥温暖；坐在里面如同坐在贝壳中，温暖而舒适，眼前是一片平展展、色彩柔和的牧草之海，四周点缀着橡树、榆树和白蜡树。

===Original===
The finches came to the boughs that hung over the ivy-grown thatch, and sang in the sycamore opposite the door, and in the apple-trees, whose bloom hung down almost to the ground.

These apple-trees, which Iden had planted, flung sackfuls of bloom at his feet. They poured themselves out in abandoned, open-armed, spendthrift, wasteful—perfectly prodigal—quantities of rose-tinted petal; prodigal as a river which flows full to the brim, never questioning but what there will be plenty of water to follow.

Flowers, and trees, and grass, seemed to spring up wherever Iden set down his foot: fruit and flowers fell from the air down upon him. It was his genius to make things grow—like sunshine and shower; a sort of Pan, a half-god of leaves and boughs, and reeds and streams, a sort of Nature in human shape, moving about and sowing Plenty and Beauty.

===Chinese===
雀鸟飞到常春藤覆盖的茅草屋顶上方垂挂的树枝上，对着门口对面的悬铃木歌唱，在苹果树上歌唱——苹果花低垂得几乎触到了地面。

这些苹果树是艾登亲手种下的，它们朝他脚下倾撒出一袋袋的花朵。它们恣意地、张开双臂地、挥霍地、浪费地——彻底地挥霍无度——倾泻出无数玫瑰色的花瓣；挥霍得像一条满溢的河流，从不怀疑后续还会有充沛的水源跟上。

花、树和草，仿佛在艾登落脚之处便蓬勃生长：果实和花朵从空中纷纷落到他身上。他有一种让万物生长的天赋——如同阳光和雨露；像是潘神（Pan），一个掌管枝叶、芦苇和溪流的半神，一个行走于人间的自然之灵，到处播撒丰饶与美丽。

===Original===
One side of the summerhouse was a thick holly-bush, Iden had set it there; he builded the summerhouse and set the ivy; and the pippin at the back, whose bloom was white; the copper-birch near by; the great sycamore alone had been there before him, but he set a seat under it, and got woodbine to flower there; the drooping-ash he planted, and if Amaryllis stood under it when the tree was in full leaf you could not see her, it made so complete an arbour; the Spanish oak in the corner; the box hedge along the ha-ha parapet; the red currants against the red wall; the big peony yonder; the damsons and pear; the yellow honey-bush; all these, and this was but one square, one mosaic of the garden, half of it sward, too, and besides these there was the rhubarb-patch at one corner; fruit, flowers, plants, and herbs, lavender, parsley, which has a very pleasant green, growing in a thick bunch, roses, pale sage—read Boccaccio and the sad story of the leaf of sage—ask Nature if you wish to know how many things more there were.

A place to eat and drink, and think of nothing in, listening to the goldfinches, and watching them carry up the moss, and lichen, and slender fibres for their nest in the fork of the apple; listening to the swallows as they twittered past, or stayed on the sharp, high top of the pear tree; to the vehement starlings, whistling and screeching like Mrs. Iden herself, on the chimneys; chaffinches "chink, chink," thrushes, distant blackbirds, who like oaks; "cuckoo, cuckoo," "crake, crake," buzzing and burring of bees, coo of turtledoves, now and then a neigh, to remind you that there were horses, fullness and richness of musical sound; a world of grass and leaf, humming like a hive with voices.

When the east wind ceases, and the sun shines above, and the flowers beneath, "a summer's day in lusty May," then is the time an Interlude in Heaven.

And all this, summerhouse and all, had dropped out of the pocket of Iden's ragged old coat.

===Chinese===
凉亭一侧是一丛茂密的冬青，艾登把它种在那儿的；他建造了凉亭，栽下了常春藤；后面那棵结白花的苹果树也是他种的；附近的红叶山毛榉也是；唯独那棵大悬铃木在他之前就在那里了，但他在树下安了一张长椅，让金银花攀到那儿开了花；他种了那棵垂枝白蜡树，阿玛丽莉斯若在树叶繁茂时站在树下，你根本看不见她——它形成了一座如此完美的凉亭；角落里那棵西班牙栎树；暗壕护墙沿线的黄杨篱；红墙边的红醋栗；远处那棵大牡丹；西洋李和梨树；金黄色的蜜花丛；这些全在，而这不过是花园的一个方块、一幅马赛克，其中一半还是草坪；此外角落里还有一片大黄；水果、花卉、草本和药草，薰衣草、欧芹——绿得宜人，丛生——玫瑰、淡色鼠尾草——去读薄伽丘（Boccaccio）笔下那片鼠尾草叶的悲情故事——若想知道还有多少更多之物，去问大自然吧。

一个吃吃喝喝、什么也不想的地方，听金翅雀歌唱，看它们衔着苔藓、地衣和纤细的纤维到苹果树杈上筑巢；听燕子啁啾着飞过，或停在梨树尖尖的高顶上；听激昂的椋鸟——像艾登夫人本人一样吹着口哨、尖声尖叫——在烟囱上；听苍头燕雀"叮叮"叫，听鸫鸟叫，听远处的乌鸫——它们喜欢橡树；"布谷，布谷"，"嘎嘎，嘎嘎"，蜜蜂嗡嗡嘤嘤，斑鸠咕咕低鸣，偶尔一声马嘶提醒你这里还有马匹——满耳是丰沛的乐音；一片草与叶的世界，嗡嗡作响，如蜂巢般充满声响。

当东风止息、阳光照耀、花朵绽放——"五月蓬勃的一天"，那便是天堂里的间奏。

而这一切，连同凉亭在内，全是从艾登破旧老外套的口袋里掉出来的。

===Original===
There was a magic power of healing in the influences of this place which Iden had created. Both Amadis and Alere Flamma had already changed for the better.

That morning when Amaryllis had found them, just arrived, the one with a portmanteau, and the other with a carpetbag, they were both pale to the last degree of paleness.

Three years had gone by since Amadis had stayed at Coombe Oaks before, when Amaryllis was thirteen and he eighteen; fine romps they had then, a great girl, and a great boy, rowing on the water, walking over the hills, exploring the woods; Amadis shooting and fishing, and Amaryllis going with him, a kind of gamekeeper page in petticoats. They were of the same stock of Idens, yet no relations; he was of the older branch, Amaryllis of the younger.

She had grown into a woman; Amadis Iden into a man.

Sadly, indeed, he had altered. Looking at him, she could scarce believe he was the same; so pale, so thin, so drooping, and fireless—the spark of life sunk into the very ashes. He sat at the dinner-table that morning like a ghost. He was convalescent from low fever: that dread disease which has taken the place of ague in the country. At one time it was ague; in these times it is low fever.

===Chinese===
艾登所创造的这个地方有一种神奇的疗愈之力。阿玛迪斯和阿莱尔·弗拉玛都已经好转了许多。

那天早上阿玛丽莉斯发现他们时，他们刚刚到达，一个提着旅行皮箱，一个拿着毛毯旅行袋，两人的面色都苍白到了极点。

自从阿玛迪斯上次住在库姆橡树庄（Coombe Oaks）以来，已经过去了三年——那时阿玛丽莉斯十三岁，他十八岁；那时他们玩得多开心，一个大姑娘，一个大男孩，在水面上划船，翻山越岭，探索树林；阿玛迪斯打猎钓鱼，阿玛丽莉斯跟着他去，像个穿裙子的小猎场看守。他们同属艾登家族的血脉，却没有亲戚关系；他来自较老的支系，阿玛丽莉斯来自较年轻的支系。

她已经长成了一个女人；阿玛迪斯·艾登已经长成了一个男人。

他的变化确实令人伤感。看着他，她几乎无法相信这是同一个人；如此苍白、如此瘦削、如此萎靡、了无生气——生命的火花已经沉入了灰烬深处。那天早上他坐在餐桌旁像个幽灵。他刚从低烧中恢复过来：那是取代了乡间疟疾的可怕疾病。从前是疟疾；如今是低烧。

===Original===
At Coombe Oaks they had heard of his illness in a far-off way, but had received no distinct particulars, for the news came in a roundabout way by word of mouth, country-folk never write. The distance between the two houses was less than ten miles, and might as well have been five hundred for all the communication.

So that the ghastly paleness of his face came upon her as a spectre in daylight. You could see at a glance what was wrong—the vital energy had been sapped; as a tree fades without a branch broken, or bark scored, fades and withers from the lack of the mysterious force which brings forth fresh leaves, so he drooped in his chair. The body—the tree—was there, but the life was not in it.

Alere Flamma, aged forty-nine, or nearly, was pale from other causes, and it was a different kind of paleness; not bloodlessness, like Amadis, but something lacking in the blood, a vitiated state. Too much Fleet Street, in short; too much of the Oracle—Pantagruel's Oracle of the Bottle.

His hands shook as he held his knife and fork—oddly enough, the hands of great genius often do shake; now and then when he put his glass to his lips, his teeth snapped on it, and chinked.

===Chinese===
在库姆橡树庄，他们听说过他生病的事，但只是隐隐约约的，并未收到确切详情，因为消息是辗转口耳相传的——乡下人从不写信。两座宅子相距不到十英里，可就通信而言，五百英里也不过如此。

因此他那可怕的苍白面容如同光天化日下的幽灵一般撞入她的眼帘。一眼就能看出问题所在——生命力已经耗尽；就像一棵树在没有断枝、没有伤痕的情况下枯萎了，因为缺乏催生新叶的神秘力量而枯萎凋零，他也这样瘫在椅子里。躯体——那棵树——还在，但生命已不在其中了。

阿莱尔·弗拉玛（Alere Flamma）年近四十九岁，他的苍白出于不同的原因，而且是另一种苍白；不像阿玛迪斯那样失血，而是血中有所欠缺，一种败坏的状态。说白了，就是弗利特街（Fleet Street）泡得太久；就是酒喝得太多——庞塔居尔（Pantagruel）的"瓶中神谕"。

他握刀叉时双手发抖——说来也怪，大天才的手往往都会发抖；偶尔他把杯子送到唇边时，牙齿会磕在杯沿上，发出叮当的声响。

===Original===
It seemed curious that such puffy, shaky hands could hold a pencil, and draw delicate lines without a flaw.

Many who never resort to the Oracle have hands that tremble nearly as much—the nervous constitution—and yet execute artists' work of rare excellence.

Alere's constitution, the Flamma constitution, naturally nervous, had been shaken as with dynamite by the bottle, and the glass chinked against his teeth. Every two or three years, when he felt himself toppling over like a tree half sawn through, Alere packed his carpetbag, and ran down to Coombe Oaks. When the rats began to run up the wall as he sat at work in broad daylight, Alere put his slippers into his carpetbag and looked out some collars.

In London he never wore a collar, only a bright red scarf round his neck; the company he kept would have shunned him—they would have looked him up and down disdainfully:—"Got a collar on—had no breakfast." They would have scornfully regarded him as no better than a City clerk, the class above all others scorned by those who use tools.

===Chinese===
这样浮肿发抖的手居然能握住铅笔，画出毫无瑕疵的精细线条，说来着实令人惊奇。

许多从未沾过那"神谕"的人手抖得几乎一样厉害——天生神经质的体质——却能完成出类拔萃的艺术作品。

阿莱尔的体质——弗拉玛家的体质——天生神经质，又像被炸药一般被酒瓶震得粉碎，杯沿磕在牙齿上叮当作响。每过两三年，当觉得自己像一棵被锯了一半的树快要倒下时，阿莱尔就收拾起毛毯旅行袋，跑到库姆橡树庄去。当他大白天坐在工作台前，老鼠开始沿墙往上爬的时候，阿莱尔就把拖鞋塞进旅行袋，找出几件衣领来。

在伦敦他从不戴衣领，只在脖子上围一条鲜红的围巾；他结交的那帮人会躲着他——他们会上下打量他，轻蔑地想："戴衣领——没吃早饭。"他们会鄙夷地把他看作跟城里职员一个档次——那是所有使用工具的人最瞧不起的阶层。

===Original===
"Got a collar on—had no breakfast." The City clerk, playing the Masher on thirty shillings a week, goes without food to appear the gentleman.

Alere, the artist, drank with the men who used hammer, and file, or set up type—a godless set, ye gods, how godless, these setters up of type at four o'clock in the morning; oysters and stout at 4 a.m.; special taverns they must have open for them—open before Aurora gleams in the east—Oh! Fleet Street, Fleet Street, what a place it is!

By no possible means could Alere work himself into a dress-coat.

Could he have followed the celebrated advice—"You put on a dress-coat and go into society"—he would soon have become a name, a fame, a taker of big fees, a maker of ten thousand yearly.

===Chinese===
"戴衣领——没吃早饭。"那个城里职员，拿着三十先令的周薪摆阔少架子，不吃东西也要装绅士。

阿莱尔这个艺术家，和那些抡锤子、锉刀或排字的人一起喝酒——一群不信神的家伙，天哪，多么不信神，那些凌晨四点就起床排字的排字工；凌晨四点吃牡蛎喝黑啤酒；专门有酒馆为他们开门——在黎明女神奥罗拉（Aurora）的光芒在东方闪现之前就开门——噢！弗利特街，弗利特街，那是个什么地方！

阿莱尔无论怎样也把自己塞不进一件燕尾服。

倘若他遵从了那句名言——"穿上燕尾服去社交"——他早已成为一个名字、一个名声、一个收取高额酬金的人、一个年入万镑的人物。

===Original===
To a man who could draw like Alere, possessed, too, of the still rarer talent—the taste to see what to draw—there really is no limit in our days; for as for colour, you do not require a genius for colour in an age of dinginess—why, the point, nowadays, is to avoid colour, and in a whole Academy you shall scarcely find as much as would tint a stick of sealing-wax.

"You put on a black coat and go into society"—that is the secret of commissions, and commissions are fortune. Nothing so clever in the way of advice has been sent forth as that remark. The great Tichborne said something about folk that had money and no brains, and folk that had brains but no money; and they as has no brains ought to be so managed as to supply money to those who had. But even the greatness of the great Tichborne's observation falls into insignificance before Chesterfield in one sentence: "Put on a black coat and go into society."

What are the sayings of the seven wise men of Greece compared to *that*?

===Chinese===
对于一个能像阿莱尔那样画画、又拥有更为罕见的天赋——品味，即懂得画什么——的人来说，在我们这个时代实在没有上限；至于色彩嘛，在这个灰暗的年代你根本不需要色彩方面的才能——说真的，如今的要点恰恰是回避色彩，整个皇家艺术学院里找出来的色彩加在一起，恐怕还不够给一根封蜡上色。

"穿上黑外套去社交"——这就是赢得委托的秘诀，而委托就是财富。从来没有哪条忠告比这句话更精妙。了不起的蒂奇伯恩（Tichborne）说过一些关于有钱没脑和有脑没钱的人的话；没脑的人应当被管理起来给有脑的人提供钱财。然而即便是蒂奇伯恩那番宏论，跟切斯特菲尔德（Chesterfield）的一句话比起来也黯然失色："穿上黑外套去社交。"

希腊七贤的名言跟*那句*相比算得了什么？

## XXVII

===Original===
By no possible means could Alere Flamma work himself into a dress coat. The clubs, the houses of the great, the mutual admiration dinners﻿—those great institutions of the day﻿—were all closed to him because of the Dress Coat.

If he had really desired to enter, of course he would have squeezed into the evening monkey-skin somehow; but, in truth, Alere did not want to enter.

Inside he might have finished a portrait a month at a thousand guineas﻿—twelve portraits per annum equals twelve thousand guineas a year; you see I am looking up the multiplication table, preparatory to going into the tallow trade.

What he actually did was to make designs for book-covers﻿—magnificent book-covers that will one day fetch their weight in banknotes﻿—manipulating a good deal of it himself﻿—"tooling"﻿—for the libraries of noble connours. They were equal to anything ever done in Paris.

For a week's work﻿—say half-an-hour a day﻿—he got perhaps about ten pounds. With the ten pounds he was satisfied﻿—ten pounds represents a good deal of brandy, or stout, or even wine, about as much as one man can manage at a bout; besides tobacco, the gallery at the theatre, and innumerable trifles of that kind. Ten pounds represents a good deal of street life.

===Chinese===
阿莱尔·弗拉玛（Alere Flamma）怎么也挤不进一件礼服外套。那些俱乐部、权贵的府邸、互相吹捧的晚宴——当今时代伟大的社交机构——全都因为这件礼服外套而将他拒之门外。

如果他真的渴望加入，他当然会想方设法把自己塞进那件晚间穿的猴子皮里；但说实话，阿莱尔并不想进去。

在里面，他本可以每月画一幅肖像，收一千几尼——一年十二幅就是一万两千几尼；你看，我正在查阅乘法表，准备投身油脂生意呢。

他实际上做的是设计书封——那些华丽的书封有朝一日能卖出与自身等重的钞票——大部分手工活他自己做——"烫金"——为贵族鉴赏家们的私人图书馆效力。这些作品不逊于巴黎的任何杰作。

干一周的活——比方说每天半小时——他大概能挣十英镑左右。十英镑他便心满意足——十英镑能买不少白兰地，或者黑啤，甚至葡萄酒，大约是一个人一次能灌下去的量；此外还有烟草、剧院的画廊票，以及诸如此类数不清的小开销。十英镑代表着丰富的街头生活。

===Original===
Sometimes he drew﻿—and engraved﻿—illustrations for books, being as clever with the engraver's tools as with the pencil; sometimes he cut out those odd, fantastic "initials," "ornaments," "finials," which are now so commonly seen in publications, catching the classical grotesque of the Renaissance to perfection, and deceiving the experienced; sometimes he worked in the press-room in the House of Flamma, Fleet Street, pulling artists' proofs, or printing expensively illustrated volumes﻿—numbered, and the plates destroyed﻿—actual manual work, in his shirt sleeves.

He could stop when he liked and take a swig of stout. That was the Alere style.

Smoking was forbidden in the old House of Flamma because of the worm-eaten beams, the worm-eaten rafters and staircase, the dusty, decayed bookshelves, the dry, rotten planks of the floor, the thin wooden partitions, all ready to catch fire at the mere sight of a match. Also because of the piles of mouldy books which choked the place, and looked fit for nothing but a bonfire, but which were worth thousands of pounds; the plates and lithographic stones, artists' proofs, divers and sundry Old Masters in a room upstairs, all easily destructible.

But Alere, being a son of the house, though not in command, did not choose to be amenable to rules and orders in fact, in fiction he was. He smoked and kept the glue-pot ready on the stove; if a certain step was known to be approaching the pipe was thrust out of sight, and some dry glue set melting, the powerful incense quite hiding the flavour of tobacco. A good deal of dry glue is used in London in this way.

===Chinese===
有时他画——还雕刻——书籍插图，用雕刻刀和用铅笔一样娴熟；有时他刻出那些古怪奇特的"首字母"、"装饰"、"顶饰"，如今在出版物中随处可见，完美地捕捉了文艺复兴时期的古典怪诞风格，连行家都被骗过；有时他在舰队街（Fleet Street）弗拉玛家族的印刷室里干活，拉艺术家样张，或者印制精美的插图藏书——限量编号，印版销毁——那是真正的体力活，穿着衬衫袖子干。

他想停就停，灌一口黑啤。这就是阿莱尔的做派。

老弗拉玛宅邸里禁止吸烟，因为那些蛀空的横梁、蛀空的椽子和楼梯、布满灰尘的朽烂书架、干枯腐烂的地板木板、薄薄的木隔板，一切都只需看见一根火柴就会着火。还因为那些发霉的书堆把整个地方堵得水泄不通，看上去除了当柴烧别无他用，却值几千英镑；楼上一间屋子里放着印版和石版、艺术家样张、各式各样的古代大师作品，全都极易损毁。

但阿莱尔作为这家的少爷，虽然没有掌权，却不愿受规章制度的约束——事实上如此，小说里写的则不然。他照抽不误，还在炉子上备着一罐胶水；一旦知道有人来了，烟斗就赶紧藏起来，同时点上一块干胶，那浓烈的熏香完全掩盖了烟草的味道。伦敦有不少干胶就是这么用的。

===Original===
If I could but write the inside history of Fleet Street, I should be looked upon as the most wonderful exponent of human life that had ever touched a pen. Balzac﻿—whom everybody talks of and nobody has read, because the discrimination of Paternoster Row has refused him a translation till quite lately﻿—Zola, who professes to be realistic, who is nothing if not realistic, but whose writings are so curiously crude and merely skim the surface; even the great Hugo, who produced the masterpiece of all fiction, *Les Misérables*; all three of them, the entire host of manuscript-makers, I am sure I could vanquish them all, if I could only write the inside life of Fleet Street.

Not in any grace of style or sweeping march of diction, but just pencil-jotted in the roughest words to hand, just as rich and poor, well-dressed ladies and next-door beggars are bundled into a train, so, without choice of language, but hustling the first words anyhow, as it were, into the first compartment. If I could only get Alere to tell me all he had seen in Fleet Street, and could just jot it down on the margin of a stained newspaper, all the world would laugh and weep. For such things do go on in Fleet Street as no man has written yet.

If only Victor Hugo were alive and young again!

===Chinese===
倘若我能写出舰队街的内幕史，我将被视为有史以来最了不起的人间生活书写者。巴尔扎克（Balzac）——人人都谈论他却没人读过他，因为帕特诺斯特街（Paternoster Row）的鉴赏力一直拒绝翻译他的作品，直到最近才有所松动——左拉（Zola），自诩为现实主义者，不现实便什么都不是，但他的作品粗陋得离奇，只浮于表面；还有伟大的雨果（Hugo），创作了所有小说中的巅峰之作《悲惨世界》（*Les Misérables*）；他们三位，加上整个手工匠人的大军，我确信只要让我写出舰队街的内幕生活，就能将他们全部击败。

不是靠什么优美的文风或磅礴的措辞，而是用铅笔潦草地记下最粗陋的词句，就像穷人和富人、衣着光鲜的贵妇和隔壁的乞丐被塞进同一节车厢那样，不加选择地把最先想到的词胡乱塞进第一节车厢。倘若我能让阿莱尔告诉我他在舰队街看到的一切，只需把它潦草地记在一张脏报纸的边角上，全天下的人看了都会笑、都会哭。因为舰队街确实在发生着那些从未有人写过的事情。

要是维克多·雨果还活着、还年轻就好了！

===Original===
Alere liked pulling off the proofs in his shirtsleeves, swigging his stout, smoking on the sly, working with all the genius of an inspired mechanic one moment and dropping into absolute idleness the next, spending infinite pains in finishing one bit of work, as if his very life depended on the smoothing of an edge of paper, putting off the next till the end of the month, pottering, sleeping, gossiping, dreaming over old German works, and especially dreaming over Goethe, humming old German songs﻿—for he had been a great traveller﻿—sometimes scrawling a furious Mazzinian onslaught in a semi-Nihilist foreign print, collecting stray engravings, wandering hither and thither.

Alere Flamma, artist, engraver, bookbinder, connoisseur, traveller, printer, Republican, conspirator, sot, smoker, dreamer, poet, kindhearted, good-natured, prodigal, shiftless, man of Fleet Street, carpetbag man, gentleman shaken to pieces.

He worked in his shirtsleeves and drank stout, but nothing vulgar had ever been recorded against Alere Flamma. He frequented strong company﻿—very strong meat﻿—but no vile word left his lips.

===Chinese===
阿莱尔喜欢穿着衬衫袖子拉样张，灌着黑啤，偷偷摸摸地抽烟，一时之间像个被灵感驱使的技工般天才迸发，一时又陷入彻底的怠惰；在完成一件作品上倾注无尽心血，仿佛他的命就悬在一条纸边是否磨平上；把下一件活推到月底再干；闲逛、睡觉、闲聊、沉醉于旧日的德语作品，尤其沉醉于歌德（Goethe），哼着古老的德语歌——因为他曾是个了不起的旅行家——有时在半虚无主义的外国刊物上潦草地写下一篇火药味十足的马志尼（Mazzinian）式讨伐文章，收集零散的版画，四处游荡。

阿莱尔·弗拉玛，画家、雕刻师、书籍装帧师、鉴赏家、旅行家、印刷工、共和派、阴谋家、酒鬼、烟客、梦想家、诗人、心地善良、脾气温和、挥霍无度、无所作为、舰队街的人、拎旅行袋的绅士、被摔得支离破碎的绅士。

他穿着衬衫袖子干活，喝着黑啤，但从没有任何粗鄙之事被记录在阿莱尔·弗拉玛的名下。他常与烈性人为伍——非常烈性的同伴——但从不出口恶言。

===Original===
There was a delicacy in all his ways in the midst of the coarsest surroundings, just as he appeared in the press-room among the printer's ink in the whitest of clean shirtsleeves, fit to wear with the abhorred dress-coat.

In his rooms at his lodgings there were literally hundreds of sketches, done on all sorts and sizes of paper, from the inside of an envelope hastily torn open to elephant. The bureau was full of them, stuffed in anyhow, neither sorted nor arranged; nothing, of course, could be found if it was wanted. The drawers of the bookcase﻿—it was his own furniture﻿—were full of them; the writing-table drawer; a box in one corner; some were on the mantelpiece smoked and gritty; some inside his books, most of which were interleaved in this manner; literally hundreds of sketches, the subjects as numerous and varied.

Views in English country lanes, views on the Danube, bands playing in band-loving Vienna, old Highgate Archway, studies from Canterbury Cathedral, statuary in the Louvre, ships battling with the north wind in the North Sea﻿—a savage fight between sail and gale﻿—horses in the meadow, an aged butler, a boy whipping a top, charcoal-burners in the Black Forest, studies from the nude﻿—Parisian models, Jewesses, almost life-size, a drayman heaving up a huge tankard, overshadowing his face like Mount Atlas turned over his thumb, designs to illustrate classical mythology, outlines expressing the ideas of Goethe﻿—outlines of Marguerite and Faust among the roses﻿—"He loves me; he loves me not," big-armed Flemish beauties with breasts as broad as the Zuyder-Zee was deep in the song, roofs of Nuremberg, revolutionary heroes charging their muskets in the famous year '48, when Alere had a bullet through his hat, in Vienna, I think; no end to them.

===Chinese===
他的一切举止都透着一种精致，即便身处最粗陋的环境中，正如他在印刷室里出现时，满是油墨之间，穿着最洁白干净的衬衫袖子，配上那件令人厌恶的礼服外套也毫不逊色。

在他租住的房间里，真的有几百幅素描，画在各种大小的纸上，从匆忙撕开的信封内页到象开纸。写字台塞满了这些画，胡乱塞进去，既未分类也未整理；当然要用的时候什么也找不到。书柜的抽屉——那是他自己的家具——里也塞满了；书桌的抽屉；角落里的一只盒子；有些搁在壁炉架上，被烟熏得积满灰尘；有些夹在他的书里，大部分书都这样夹着画稿；真的是几百幅素描，题材丰富多样。

英国乡间小路的风景、多瑙河畔的景色、在嗜好音乐的维也纳演奏的乐队、古老的海格特拱门（Highgate Archway）、坎特伯雷大教堂的写生、卢浮宫的雕塑、北海里与北风搏斗的船只——帆与风暴之间的一场恶战——草地上的马匹、一个年迈的管家、一个抽陀螺的男孩、黑森林里的烧炭工、裸体写生——巴黎模特、犹太女子，几乎真人大小，一个运货马车夫举起一只巨大的酒罐，酒罐的影子盖住了他的脸，像阿特拉斯山（Mount Atlas）翻过拇指一般，阐释古典神话的图稿、表达歌德思想的轮廓——玫瑰丛中玛格丽特（Marguerite）与浮士德（Faust）的轮廓——"他爱我；他不爱我"，壮硕的佛兰芒美女，双臂粗壮，胸脯宽阔如祖德海（Zuyder-Zee）在歌中唱的那般深邃，纽伦堡的屋顶，革命英雄在一八四八年那个著名的年份给步枪上膛——阿莱尔的帽子被子弹打穿，我想是在维也纳；这些素描无穷无尽。

===Original===
